REDACTION — 14 files (including `sniper.ts`, `openbook.ts`, `order-monitor.ts`, `wallet-check/index.ts`, `bundle-buy.ts`, `distribute.ts`, `gather.ts`) contain `// Private code` stubs; `base/sniper.ts` imports a non-existent `./transaction/transaction` module, so it is not runnable as shipped. Includes volume-boost wash-trading (integrity problem for Memeland).
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Not green-label. Solana/Jito/Yellowstone gRPC-locked (not portable to RH chain 4663 EVM), heavyweight infra (MongoDB/Redis/gRPC/dashboard), incomplete/redacted core, and a NEXT_PUBLIC-secrets anti-pattern Memeland must NOT copy. The bucketed wallet-risk scoring idea (SRC-081/SRC-162 already cover similar deterministic scoring with real code) is the only fragment worth borrowing, and even that is stubbed here.
- **Verdict:** **Skip** — broken/redacted shell, Solana-only, heavy, unsafe env handling, wash-trading focus; nothing Memeland should adopt. (Reference only for the bucketed wallet-risk-scoring *idea*, better implemented by SRC-081/SRC-162.)
### SRC-204 stefanoviana/crypto-pump-scanner (Python 3.9+ single file, ~1362 lines + Docker/ccxt)
- **What it is:** A real-time pump detection + auto-trading bot for BYBIT USDT perp futures (a CEX, not on-chain). Polls all 500+ Bybit perpetual pairs every 3s via ccxt, runs a 6-layer confirmation (volume spike vs 20-period EWMA >=5x, price +3% in window, >=3 consecutive green candles, RSI 60-85, buy-side volume ratio >65%, min $500K daily dollar-vol), scores a confidence blend, opens leveraged longs with ATR-based SL, a 3-level cascade TP (+5%/+10%/+20% with trailing after TP2), a 2h time-based exit, a -$50 daily circuit breaker, cooldown per coin, and new-listing detection from Bybit/Binance announcements. Telegram alerts + a JSON trade log.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** (1) Multi-layer momentum confirmation — the idea of requiring several independent corroborating signals (volume/EWMA spike, green-candle streak, RSI sweet-spot, buy-side dominance, min dollar volume) before acting, then folding them into a weighted 0-1 confidence score with a reject threshold. (2) Cascade TP exit engine with progressive size reduction (close 40/30/remaining), SL move-to-breakeven after TP1, trailing activation after TP2, and hard time-based max-hold exit — an exit ladder Memeland could reuse on-chain. (3) Daily circuit breaker (-$50 stop) + per-coin cooldown — cheap risk rails. (4) ATR-based stop sizing and safe OHLCV fetch wrapper. (5) Env-loaded keys, no secrets committed.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Single 1362-line monolithic script (hard to test; no tests). CEX-futures specific: leverage, margin, positions, ccxt exchange model — none of it maps to on-chain swaps. New-listing logic is CEX announcement scraping (Bybit/Binance), not on-chain. Conservative defaults though (5% risk budget, max 2 positions, -$50/day halt). No hardcoded API keys (read from env).
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Not a memecoin/on-chain source and not portable as-is (needs a Bybit account; Memeland trades RH-chain/Solana/BSC DEX). But the *confirmation-and-confidence* detection pattern and the *cascade-TP/trailing/time-exit* exit ladder are venue-agnostic exit/entry frameworks Memeland could re-derive from on-chain price/volume data. No GMGN dependency, low complexity, but no real on-chain edge on its own — it only reacts to already-spiking candles on a CEX.
- **Verdict:** **Study** (borrow the multi-layer confirmation + weighted-confidence detection and the cascade-TP/trailing/time-exit + circuit-breaker framework as generic momentum patterns) + **Skip** (direct adoption — CEX futures-only, monolithic, not an on-chain edge).
### SRC-210 thegreatola/memecoins-trading-agent (TypeScript, 2 files only — orchestrator.ts + walletTracker.ts, no package.json)
- **What it is:** README describes a full autonomous Solana memecoin agent: watches 10-30 "smart" wallets via Helius WebSocket, detects a convergence signal (2-5 trusted wallets buying the same token within ~5 min), scores the token on volume/holders/dev-history/distribution, adds a Grok/X "CT momentum" scanner, sizes 1-10 SOL by wallet-count×score×trust, executes via Jupiter, runs staged exits (2x/3x/5x + emergency sell if tracked wallets dump), and a learning engine that adapts scoring weights + wallet trust after each closed trade. IMPORTANT: only `orchestrator.ts` and `walletTracker.ts` are actually present; `package.json`, `signalDetector`, `tokenAnalyzer`, `ctScanner`, `tradeExecutor`, `positionManager`, `learningEngine`, `notifier`, `db`, `config/params` all referenced but missing — the two files cannot compile/run as shipped.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** `walletTracker.ts` is complete and genuinely useful: a Helius `transactionSubscribe` (WS, `type:'SWAP'`) client that drains buy events by parsing `preTokenBalances` vs `postTokenBalances` (detects the token whose balance increased in the signer account; filters meta.err; requires a known DEX program id from a hardcoded set of Raydium/Jupiter/Pump.fun/Orca program IDs; derives SOL spent from `preBalances`/`postBalances` deltas; sanity bounds 0<SOL<=1000; source classification by program id). Includes WS reconnect with backoff, 30s ping keepalive, and an EventEmitter→pipeline interface. The `orchestrator.ts` shows a clean stage pipeline framing: convergence signal → token score → CT bonus → composite-threshold skip → size → balance check → execute → insert trade → track position → notify, with an in-flight `processingTokens` dedupe Set and a position manager emitting `exit`/`ctMotion` events.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Repo is an incomplete skeleton (README overpromises; code won't run) — a maintenance/trust red flag. Dependencies are heavy and data-provider-coupled by design (Helius RPC + WebSocket, DexScreener, X/Grok CT API). Private key is base58 via env only (good). The buy-detection via balance-delta parse is Helius/jsonParsed-specific. No hardcoded secrets found. Heavily relies on trusting "smart wallet" lists (a follower/copy-cat edge, not independent — plus a CT/Grok narrative amplifier that is not deterministic).
- **For Memeland / edge check:** The WS balance-delta buy-detection pattern (`postTokenBalances`-increase + DEX-program allowlist + SOL-spent derivation) is a clean, portable example of cheap, deterministic on-chain buy detection — conceptually reusable on Solana and mirrors Memeland's log-listener approach. The convergence-signal framing (N-wallet co-buy within a timed window + dedupe + sized position) is a real, deterministic signal structure worth studying. But the shipped artifact is incomplete and locked to Helius/Jupiter/DexScreener/X — not a green-label edge by itself (would need GMGN/Helius+DexScreener data Memeland is trying to reduce).
- **Verdict:** **Study** (WS balance-delta buy-detection + timed N-wallet convergence-signal framing + stage pipeline pattern are worth borrowing, conceptually chain-portable) + **Skip** (adoption — incomplete skeleton, no package.json, Helius/Jupiter/DexScreener/X-locked follower edge).
### SRC-232 yllvar/gmgn-TrendingAnalyzer (Python, ~5 files — gmgn/client.py + analyzer.py)
- **What it is:** A read-only analyzer for trending Solana tokens pulled from the UNOFFICIAL GMGN.ai web API. `gmgn/client.py` is a thin wrapper around `https://gmgn.ai/defi/quotation/.../sol/...` endpoints (new pairs, trending wallets, trending tokens by timeframe, pump completion, sniped tokens, gas fee, realtime price, top buyers, security info, wallet info). `analyzer.py` fetches trending tokens across 5 timeframes (1m/5m/1h/6h/24h), aggregates by token address (mean price/volume, median market cap, distinct-timeframe "consistency count"), filters on volume>=1000 + market cap>=10000 + appearing in >=3 timeframes, sorts, and renders a matplotlib bubble chart + CSV.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** The aggregation idea is the one borrowable thing: grouping the same token across multiple trend windows and using a **cross-timeframe consistency count** (appears in >=3 of 5 windows) as a quality filter, with median (robust) for market cap and mean for volume/price-change, sorted by consistency then volume. It is a deterministic, dependency-light way to surface tokens that are trending on more than one timescale. (Conceptually similar to SRC-086's multi-source fusion and memeoy's revival gate, but weaker.)
- **Security/complexity flags:** The client defeats GMGN bot-detection by rotating randomized `tls_client` TLS fingerprints and `fake_useragent` strings on every call (`randomiseRequest`) — fragile and ToS-questionable; any GMGN backend change silently breaks it, and there is no auth/key (it scrapes instead). All URLs hardcode `chain=sol`, so it is Solana-only and not chain-portable. No tests. `analyzer.py` assumes the response has a `"rank"` key after the client already unwraps `['data']`, which is brittle against API shape drift. No secrets committed.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Fails the green-label test head-on: it is a pure GMGN-scraping dependency (exactly what Memeland wants to avoid), Solana-only (no RH-chain 4663 port), and the TLS-spoofing layer is fragile/unmaintainable. The cross-timeframe consistency-count filter is a genuinely portable signal idea, but Memeland would re-implement it over its own data source, not this GMGN client.
- **Verdict:** **Skip** (GMGN-scraping client — Solana-only, TLS-spoofing fragility, anti-goal) + **Study** (the cross-timeframe "consistency count + median market cap" aggregation as a conceptual quality-filter idea to rebuild over Memeland's own data).
### SRC-049 AI4Finance-Foundation/FinRobot (Python, large — ~250+ files across finrobot/, finrobot_equity/, web_app, tutorials)
- **What it is:** An open-source AI-agent platform for EQUITIES financial research. V0 is AutoGen multi-agent (research/forecaster/trade-strategist agents, RAG over SEC filings + earnings calls, LLM market forecaster, backtrader backtests); `finrobot_equity/` is a V1 project (OpenAI Agents SDK) generating professional equity research reports (company overview, competitors, valuation, risks, news summary, PDF/HTML renderers); plus a web app, RAG query layer, and numerous tutorials. Data sources: yfinance, Finnhub, FMP, SEC API, Reddit, tushare, OpenBB, mplfinance/backtrader.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** Conceptually, the tool-dispatch pattern (LLM calls typed Python tools like `BackTraderUtils.back_test`, `get_financial_statements`) and the "keep deterministic computation at the core, LLM only orchestrates/annotates" philosophy stated in the README (which is the right instinct and aligns with Memeland's green-label preference for deterministic local calcs). `functional/quantitative.py` has a small, clean Backtrader analyzer (return-on-deployed-capital). Data-source wrappers (`yfinance_utils`, `finnhub_utils`) show a tidy get-data-if-cached-else-fetch pattern.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Heavy: AutoGen/pyautogen, langchain, OpenBB, marker-pdf, backtrader, pandas/numpy pins, pinning python <3.12. Equities-specific throughout (SEC filings, earnings calls, tickers, financial statements) — nothing about DEX/memecoin/on-chain. `config_api_keys` and `OAI_CONFIG_LIST` contain only placeholders ("YOUR_...", "<your key here>") — no hardcoded secrets committed (good). Report examples embed nothing sensitive. Requires multiple paid API keys (OpenAI/Azure, Finnhub, FMP, SEC, Reddit, Twitter) and LLM spend.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Fails the green-label bar outright: it is a heavyweight LLM agent platform aimed at public-equity research, not an on-chain memecoin edge. It is not portable to RH-chain/Solana/BSC DEX trading (no chain/DEX integration), depends on many paid data APIs + an LLM backbone, and its complexity (multi-agent, RAG, report rendering) is far beyond a personal memecoin bot. The only borrowable *idea* — use LLMs to orchestrate/annotate while keeping deterministic calcs in typed tools — Memeland already gets more directly from SRC-051/memeoy's bounded tuner and SRC-081/086/147 deterministic scorers, without this framework's weight.
- **Verdict:** **Skip** — heavy LLM/equities research platform; no on-chain/memecoin edge, not chain-portable, high infra+API cost, and its LLM-orchestrates-deterministic-core philosophy is already better served by lighter sources in this audit.
### SRC-109 HKUDS/Vibe-Trading (Python 3.11+, ~400 files)
- **What it is:** "Natural-language finance research AI agent with backtesting" — a heavyweight LLM research/backtest assistant (langchain/langgraph + duckdb + FastAPI + React 19 frontend + MCP server). Backtest flex engines across crypto perps, A-shares, futures, forex, global equity, options; data loaders (binance/ccxt/okx/yahoo/tushare/akshare/yfinance/finnhub/fmp); optimizers (risk-parity, mean-variance, max-diversification). NOT an on-chain memecoin bot; the "Robinhood" mention is brokerage shares, not Robinhood Chain.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** (1) **Fail-closed figure-grounding gate** (`agent/src/agent/grounding/`) — the most original/portable idea: the LLM must DECLARE a role+shape (price vs vol vs sharpe vs drawdown) per number, and policies.py validates each declared figure against ledger evidence; unverifiable figures are cut and released as `(omitted※)`. Evidence intake is keyed on tool FIELD NAMES not prose; a figure is grounded only by same-kind evidence (a price never stands in for volatility); tolerance band 0.5%; fails closed. (2) **Shadow account / reconciliation** for live-vs-sim balance checks. (3) Backtest validation rigor: per-source annualisation table (365 crypto, 252/260 equity) and bars-per-day map; `perpetual_risk.py` deterministic cross/isolated margin + maintenance-bracket liquidation model with strict invariants (`_require_finite`, content-hashed bracket artifacts, funding settlement every 8h). (4) Fail-closed loaders that validate/verify before returning.
- **Security/complexity flags:** .env.example is clean (all placeholder keys `sk-xxx`, nothing committed). Heavy stack: langchain>=1.3.9, langgraph 1.2, duckdb, scikit-learn, joblib + many broker connectors (upbit, tushare, IBKR MCP). ~400 files, monolithic. LLM-gate correctness depends on meticulous edge-case handling (Chinese field aliases, metric-kind disambiguation) — a lot to maintain.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** No on-chain/execution edge; it is a research/backtest assistant on CEX/equity data — not portable to a memecoin sniper and not aligned with the green label. HOWEVER the fail-closed figure-grounding pattern (declare-a-claim → validate-against-evidence → else drop) could become a light, language-agnostic trace/derivation guard if Memeland ever surfaces LLM/AI-generated coin scores — port the CONCEPT (role+shape declaration + tolerance check + omit-on-unverifiable), not langchain. The per-source annualisation/bars-per-day map and deterministic perp-risk invariants are solid backtest-reference discipline for Memeland's own simulator.
- **Verdict:** **Study** (fail-closed figure-grounding gate concept + backtest validation metrics/annualisation + deterministic margin/liquidation model as correctness reference) + **Skip** (adoption: heavyweight LLM research platform, not an on-chain memecoin spot/perpetual execution edge; not portable across chains).

## Batch 11 - MCP-data, market-data & chain sources deep-read (subagent Russell)

### SRC-043 6551Team/opennews-mcp (Python, ~17 files)
- **What it is:** FastMCP server that proxies a proprietary 6551.io REST/WebSocket news API ("OpenNews"). Aggregates 85+ sources (news, exchange listings incl. Robinhood, Hyperliquid whale, meme sentiment, market signals, 12 AI "prediction" engines). Most tools require an OPENNEWS_TOKEN; a few /open/free_* endpoints are keyless. Thin async httpx client + FastMCP tool registration.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** pi_client.py shows a clean httpx async pattern with client reset + bounded retry (MAX_RETRIES=2) on ConnectError/RemoteProtocolError only; config.py clamp_limit() and env-over-config precedence; make_serializable() recursive JSON normalizer for Decimal/date/bytes; WS subscribe/receive with per-request ids and timeouts. That is about all the real code.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Token can live in checked-in config.json (pi_token) which is easy to leak; WSS_URL?token=... puts the bearer token in a query string (WS path, avoidable). Windows hardcodes WindowsSelectorEventLoopPolicy. Closed commercial API = single-vendor lock-in; no rate-limit/pacing logic of its own (relies on provider).
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Mostly a wrapper around another vendor's token-gated feed. Memeland already covers X/Telegram/news/sentiment via its own adapters; adding 6551 buys little provider independence (it is just another single vendor) and introduces a token + WS dependency. Its source taxonomy is a decent reference list of what coverage exists, but the code has no algorithmic edge.
- **Verdict:** **Skip** - provider-dependent closed-API MCP wrapper, redundant with existing news/sentiment adapters, no local algorithm to port; token-in-config and WS-query-string are anti-patterns.

### SRC-045 aahl/mcp-aktools (Python, ~7 files incl. vendored day-job)
- **What it is:** FastMCP server that wraps kshare for China A-share/HK/US stock quotes, news, financials, plus OKX candlesticks/loan-ratio/taker-volume and Binance "AI report" text. Crypto part is CEX spot only (BTC-USDT style OHLCV, technical indicators), no on-chain, no memecoin. Includes CORS-allowed HTTP/SSE mode, two-tier cache, and a 	rading_suggest stub that just echoes an LLM's buy/sell/hold.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** cache.py two-tier cache is the only non-trivial code: in-memory cachetools.TTLCache (maxsize 100) over a diskcache persist with short TTL + longer persist TTL, keyed by function name+args. dd_technical_indicators() is a compact pandas MACD/KDJ/RSI/BOLL implementation usable as a reference spec. k_cache wraps fetch+resilience behind one call name.
- **Security/complexity flags:** HTTP mode binds  .0.0.0 by default on port 80 with CORS allow_origins=["*"] + llow_credentials=True - client-agnostic open web endpoint, risky if ever deployed. Pulls Eastmoney news over plain http:// and scrapes unofficial endpoints; scraper/fragility risk. Heavy kshare/pandas dependency tree. No hardcoded keys/credentials found.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Domain is overwhelmingly China A-shares; the crypto side is Binance/OKX CEX spot, irrelevant to on-chain memecoin discovery/flow. Nothing here improves Memeland's GMGN independence or adds on-chain edge. The two-tier cache idea and indicator formulas are already covered by Memeland's existing cache/quant layers. Not portable to RH Chain/Solana/BSC feeds.
- **Verdict:** **Skip** for Memeland - wrong domain (China equities + CEX spot), heavy Python data-stack, open-CORS HTTP default is an anti-pattern; cache/indicator patterns add no net edge over existing modules.

### SRC-052 alpacahq/alpaca-mcp-server (Python, ~20 files incl. Helm chart + tests)
- **What it is:** Official Alpaca MCP server (v2) exposing stock/options/crypto trading + market data as MCP tools. Python/FastMCP + OpenAPI-driven tool generation with hand-written overrides for order placement and history. Paper/live split via env (ALPACA_PAPER_TRADE default true). Backed by real tests (trust-boundary, prompt-injection envelope, HTTP host/origin guard, paper integration).
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - security.py **TrustBoundaryMiddleware**: wraps every tool result in {_alpaca_mcp_security:{trust,risk,instructions}, data} envelope, with per-tool output-risk classification (pi_structured vs xternal_text) and explicit "treat as data, not instructions" injection warnings. Test 	est_prompt_injection_envelope.py proves injected news lands under data, never at top level. This is exactly Memeland's "untrusted text must never control execution" rule made mechanical.
  - overrides.py **timeout-idempotency**: on order POST ReadTimeout, returns "request MAY have been placed; check open orders before retrying; use client_order_id to retry safely" instead of blindly retrying - a correct fail-closed execution pattern Memeland's executor should copy.
  - 	oolsets.py allowlist model: only operationIds listed in a toolset are exposed; new endpoints excluded by default (OVERRIDE_OPERATION_IDS carve-out). Restricts LLM tool surface by construction.
  - cli.py + 	est_http_security.py: HTTP binds 127.0.0.1 by default, enables host_origin_protection=True, rejects untrusted Host(421)/Origin(403) via HostOriginGuardMiddleware. Good model for Memeland's loopback REST surface.
  - Tool annotations 
eadOnlyHint/destructiveHint/idempotentHint; relative-time convenience params (days/hours/minutes) because LLMs mis-compute ISO timestamps.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Keys only from env (no hardcoded secrets found). Requires API keys for most functionality; US-brokerage-specific. Moderate code size + FastMCP/Python stack; secrets to Helm chart. No obvious unsafe defaults; paper-mode default is fail-safe. No crypto on-chain data.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** The data side (US stocks/options/CEX crypto) is irrelevant to RH/Solana/BSC memecoin. But the **security architecture is portable and high-value**: adopt the trust-boundary envelope + output-risk registry on Memeland's LLM-facing control surfaces (Discord/Telegram inputs, tool outputs feeding voters), and copy the timeout-idempotency rule for transaction submission. Cheap to port to TS as a middleware/Decorator.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** the trust-boundary envelope, per-tool output-risk registry, timeout-idempotent order submission, and toolset allowlist (small TS middleware for Memeland's LLM surfaces + executor); **Skip** the Alpaca brokerage/CEX data plumbing itself. Well-tested reference-grade security patterns, no memecoin/on-chain edge.

### SRC-058 atilaahmettaner/tradingview-mcp (Python, ~90 files incl. tests + coinlists)
- **What it is:** Large FastMCP server ("TradingView MCP") for CEX/stock market data + technical analysis: 30+ TA tools, 9 backtest strategies, walk-forward backtesting with overfitting verdict, screeners/scanners, sentiment/news, smart-money OHLCV proxies. No TradingView account/login; data via public endpoints (Yahoo, TradingView screener/TA, Marketaux). Python/pandas + tradingview-screener; heavy but well-tested (unit + opt-in stress suite).
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - acktest_service.py pure-Python (no numpy/pandas) backtest engine: proper bar-window handling (Donchian compares prior window to avoid self-inclusion bug), dividend/split adjustment via Yahoo adjclose factor, orced_exit handling for open positions at final bar. **Walk-forward** 	rain/test split scoring OOS/train Sharpe with verdict thresholds ROBUST>=0.8 / WEAK>=0.2 / OVERFITTED<0.2 with explicit "do not trade live" gate - directly reusable for Memeland's quant/learning feed and swarm-weight validation.
  - indicators_calc.py zero-dependency EMA/SMA/RSI(Wilder)/Bollinger/MACD/ATR/Supertrend/Donchian - clean reference for a TS port if Memeland needs deterministic TA.
  - smart_money_service.py documents the honest limitation: banker/hot-money/retail decomposition and CMF/MFI/OBV/up-down-volume composite are **OHLCV-only proxies** for institutional flow, explicitly not real fund-flow data; every payload carries a disclaimer.
  - proxy_manager.py URL-encodes proxy credentials and handles PROXY_ENABLED toggling; resilience/retry + 60s TTL cache on the screener provider; async hot-path tools offloaded via syncio.to_thread.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No hardcoded secrets; keys/tokens/proxy creds all from env (.env.example). Marketaux token optional (graceful stub). Moderate-to-large Python/pandas dependency tree; 	radingview-screener pinned ==3.0.0 due to silent behavior change. Scrapes public Yahoo/screener endpoints (fragility risk). CEX/stock domain, not on-chain.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Data side is CEX/stock OHLCV - no on-chain, no memecoin, no GMGN-independence value. The real edge is the **walk-forward OOS overfitting verdict**: a deterministic, cheap rule ("do not trade live when OOS degrades below threshold") that fits Memeland's verified-outcome learning and ML-voter gating. Memeland's own on-chain flow is richer than these smart-money OHLCV proxies, so skip those.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** the walk-forward train/test + overfitting verdict concept into Memeland's quant/learning gate (port to TS, measure OOS Sharpe of consensus/voter strategies); **Study** the zero-dep indicator + backtest engine as a reference for Memeland's deterministic quant layer; **Skip** the MCP server wholesale (CEX-focused, heavy, Python/pandas, screeners). Not a memecoin/chain-edge source.

### SRC-059 autonsol/sol-mcp (JS/Node ESM, ~7 files; 1 large server.js 1870 lines)
- **What it is:** MCP server exposing Solana token risk-scoring, momentum signals, pump.fun graduation-alert decisions, wallet SPL scan, and market-regime classification. Free tier hits hosted Railway endpoints; PRO tier is gated by x402 micropayments ($0.01 USDC/call). The actual analysis engine is a black box on the author's Railway; this repo is a thin formatting/proxy layer.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** Clean MCP tool surface with zod schemas; `Promise.allSettled` for batch risk calls with safest-first sort; simple text-rendering of results (ASCII bars, labels). Reference pattern for x402 pay-per-call gating (x402ResourceServer + paymentMiddleware). Wallet scan uses raw public Solana RPC `getTokenAccountsByOwner` against TokenkegQfeZyiNwAJbNbGKPFXCWuBvf9Ss623VQ5DA.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No hardcoded secrets in repo (x402 configured via env with safe defaults); but all proprietary risk/momentum logic is server-side and unverifiable — no data lineage. Free tier rate-limits and obscures scores. `preview_wallet` may surface PII-ish wallet holdings without auth in free tier. No local algorithmic edge to audit; risk-score methodology is opaque.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** NOT an edge — no local computation, fully dependent on a single vendor's closed Railway backend (same GMGN-coupling anti-pattern in different clothes). Solana-only, not portable to RH chain 4663/BSC. Could only steal the thin MCP-tool + x402 monetization scaffolding, which Memeland doesn't need.
- **Verdict:** **Skip** — closed-vendor MCP proxy, no reusable algorithmic edge, Solana-only, opaque risk methodology. (Only marginal "Study" of its zod-schema MCP tool pattern, already seen better in SRC-052.)

### SRC-079 dbotx/dbot-mcp-servers (TypeScript, ~30 files across 4 packages)
- **What it is:** Monorepo of four MCP servers (fast-swap, limit-order, conditional-order, copy-trading) that wire Claude/agent clients to a hosted dbotx trading backend (`api-bot-v1.dbotx.com`). Multi-chain (Solana, Ethereum, Base, BSC, Tron). Each exposes buy/sell/tp-sl/limit/copy-trade tools plus token market & security info; all executions happen server-side on dbotx, not locally. Ships on npm as `@dbotx/*`.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** Clean zod-driven tool schemas with extensive env-defaulted tuning knobs (slippage cap, jito tip, priority fee, retries, concurrent nodes, `getEnvDefault` helper). Thin `DbotClient` (axios, `X-API-KEY` header) pattern. The BR, SL/TP and copy-trade *parameter* taxonomy is a good reference for what a trading bot should expose (dev-sell%, migrate-sell%, expiry semantics, ratio/fixed/follow buy modes).
- **Security/complexity flags:** No hardcoded secrets found (API key enforced from env, throws if missing); but key + wallet custody sits entirely on a third-party vendor — code signs nothing locally, so wallet keys OR wallet IDs are remote-trust. Broad default knobs (JITO enabled, 0.1 slippage) are unsafe defaults for a personal bot. All count-life logic (trade execution, order matching) is closed source: unverifiable and a big veto on green-label trust.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Good conceptual *feature checklist* (fast path, limit, conditional, copy-trade, per-wallet) and solid zod schema style Memeland could mirror in TS. But no local algorithmic edge (everything is vendor API), single-vendor coupling (worse than GMGN — it executes trades remotely), and while multi-chain in name, RH chain 4663 (Arbitrum Nitro) isn't supported. Not the execution layer Memeland wants to own.
- **Verdict:** **Skip** for ownership reasons (remote execution, closed backend). **Study** its zod tool-schema style + trading-parameter taxonomy as a spec for Memeland's own TS toolset.

### SRC-084 dynamolabs/solana-mcp (TypeScript, ~8 files, 2 core src files)
- **What it is:** MCP server for Solana letting AI query wallets/tokens/trades and run risk analysis. Uses Helius RPC/API (key in env) + DexScreener public endpoints + Jupiter quotes. Tools: balances, token info, recent trades, trending/new pairs, `analyze_token` risk score, bundled-launch detection, creator-token history, whale txs, swap quote. Real on-chain analysis logic implemented locally (not just a vendor proxy).
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** The `analyzeToken` risk rubric is the standout: additive risk score (cap 100) across mint authority (+25), freeze authority (+20), LP burned/locked (+20), token age (+15/5), holder concentration top-1/top-10 (+25/10/15), holder count (+10), market cap (+15), socials (+5), with per-check `passed`/`detail` safety list — clean, explainable, GMGN-independent (raw RPC + DexScreener). `checkBundledLaunch` groups first 50 txs by slot to flag same-block bundles (>=3 txs/slot). `getCreatorTokens` walks mint authority or first feePayer to find sibling tokens. Parallel `Promise.all` fetches. All tool schemas via MCP SDK `zod`.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No hardcoded secrets (Helius key from `process.env.HELIUS_API_KEY`, empty-string default that produces a broken RPC URL rather than failing fast — a mild footgun). No auth surface (read-only queries). `puppeteer` declared in deps but never imported (dead dependency ~heavy). Solana-specific (Helius + SPL token accounts + pump.fun constants), though the *rubric structure* ports to RH chain/BSC.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Strong green-label fit for the **risk-scoring edge**: simple, transparent, explainable, no closed vendor coupling, works off public RPC + DexScreener. The risk-rubric + bundled-launch + creator-track patterns are directly portable: Memeland can re-implement the same checks against Arbitrum Nitro (RH 4663) / BSC contracts (owner authority, LP lock/burn, holder distribution, age, market cap). Skip Helius (Solana-specific) but copy the scoring architecture.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** — port the risk-scoring rubric, LP/holder-concentration checks, and bundle detection to Memeland's multi-chain stack; the Helius/pump.fun specifics are Solana-only. Lightweight, no secrets, genuinely reusable edge.

### SRC-094 financial-datasets/mcp-server (Python, ~8 files, single server.py)
- **What it is:** MCP server exposing Financial Datasets' stock/crypto data API (income statements, balance sheets, cash flow, current/historical prices, company news, plus a few generic crypto price tickers). It is a thin `httpx` proxy — each `@mcp.tool` builds a URL against `api.financialdatasets.ai`, calls `make_request`, and stringifies the JSON.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** Minimal clean MCP-tool pattern (`@mcp.tool` + async + 30s timeout + error handling in one helper). Not much else — it adds no computation, caching, or normalization beyond passthrough.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No hardcoded secrets (API key read from env, sent as `X-API-KEY` header). Simplicity is a plus but so is triviality. Stock/fundamental focus is entirely off-domain for a memecoin DEX bot.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** No edge. Vendor-token-gated equities API, CEX/stock-market domain, nothing portable to RH chain/Solana/BSC DEX mechanics. The only crypto endpoints are generic centralized exchange prices, which Memeland doesn't need.
- **Verdict:** **Skip** — stock-market vendor proxy, zero algorithmic value, wrong domain. (MCP-tool boilerplate is the only lesson, already covered by better sources.)

### SRC-104 guangxiangdebizi/FinanceMCP (TypeScript, ~130 files, multi-package with tests)
- **What it is:** A heavyweight multi-source financial-data MCP server (v4.x) focused on China A-shares/HK/US stocks + funds via Tushare, with optional Qveris/Twingly/Binance routes and a cross-agent model cache gateway. ~20 tools (block trades, margin, convertibles, fund data, company performance, dragon-tiger, index/macro/money flow). Has its own HTTP server, cache gateway CLI, and a full test suite. Domain is equities/funds — not DEX memecoins.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** (1) `dataSourceRouter.ts`: a clean fallback router (`attempt` chain with `success/unsupported/failed` outcomes + source-priority config) — a strong pattern for Memeland's multi-provider (RH RPC/DexScreener/Jupiter/subgraph) fallback strategy. (2) Dependency-free technical indicator modules in `tools/stockDataDetail/` (MA/EMA, MACD, RSI, KDJ, BOLL) with a `dataCalculator.ts` that computes required backfill days per indicator — useful, portable TA math Memeland can extract/adapt for its candles. (3) Solid HTTP hardening in `httpServer.ts`: binds loopback by default, `hostHeaderValidation` allowlist for non-loopback, `trust proxy loopback`; the code logs a warning when broadening without `MCP_ALLOWED_HOSTS`. (4) canonical schema + response-translation + cache-gateway design (encrypted shared cache for model providers).
- **Security/complexity flags:** No hardcoded secrets (tokens via env; gateway master key generated). But `cors({ origin: '*' })` on the HTTP endpoint is a permissive default (mitigated by loopback default bind). Overall **heavy**: dozens of tools, 100+ isolated formatters, Docker/image playbooks, star-history bots — too much machinery for a "simple/green" Memeland. Domain-specific (A-shares, fund data) is irrelevant.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Not an edge on its own (equities domain, heavy). But the **data-source fallback router** is exactly the GMGN-independence tool: route each data call across multiple free providers with ordered fallback and error outcome tracking. The **TA indicator lib + backfill-days calculator** is a lightweight, dependency-free, portable TA core. HTTP host-validation hardening is a good model for any Memeland server surface.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** (router pattern + TA indicators + HTTP hardening) / **Skip** (the broader equity MCP, its weight, and its domain).

### SRC-125 kukapay/crypto-indicators-mcp (JavaScript CJS, ~12 files)
- **What it is:** An MCP server exposing ~70 TA "tools": technical indicators (trend/momentum/volatility/volume) and "strategies" that return -1/0/1 (sell/hold/buy). Data source is a single CEX via `ccxt` (default Binance, `EXCHANGE_NAME` env). Each tool fetches OHLCV then calls into the **`indicatorts`** npm library and stringifies the result. It is a pass-through adapter, not an edge.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** Clean tool-splitting pattern (`index.js` requires per-category modules that each register `server.tool`). Uniform OHLCV→Asset conversion (`dates/openings/highs/lows/closings/volumes`). The value is entirely in `indicatorts`/`trading-indicator` deps, not this repo's code.
- **Security/complexity flags:** `ccxt` is imported in `fetchOhlcvData.js` but **missing from package.json dependencies** — will fail on a clean install (latent packaging bug). No secrets. But it defaults to CEX OHLCV (Binance), which Memeland memecoins largely don't trade on; timeframe/pair conventions are CEX-centric. All TA logic delegated to third-party libs — no transparency/verification of indicator math.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Not a real edge (CEX-only, delegated math). Conceptually useful as a *menu* of which indicators/strategies exist (SMA/EMA/RSI/MACD/BOLL/Stoch/Ichimoku/AO/WILLR + strategies), but Memeland already gets better zero-dep TA from SRC-104 (FinanceMCP's own MA/MACD/RSI/KDJ/BOLL) and SRC-058's backtest engine. No chain portability — no DEX/RH-RPC candle feeds.
- **Verdict:** **Skip** — CEX OHLCV passthrough with delegated math and a packaging bug; no ownable edge. (Reference the `indicatorts` library name if Memeland wants a ready TA lib, but prefer SRC-104's zero-dep math.)

### SRC-126 kukapay/crypto-sentiment-mcp (Python, ~5 files, single main.py)
- **What it is:** MCP server wrapping Santiment's GraphQL API for social/sentiment metrics (sentiment balance, social volume, social dominance, trending words). Data source is a single vendor token-gated feed. Each tool calls `fetch_santiment_data` (a raw `requests.post` to the GraphQL endpoint) and returns a canned sentence. ~5 tools, minimal logic.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** `alert_social_shift` is a small but clean anomaly detector: compare latest day's social volume vs. prior-window average, flag % change against a threshold, direction-aware wording — a reusable "percent-vs-baseline spike/drop alert" pattern Memeland could apply to any volume/sentiment/activity time series. `get_trending_words` aggregates per-day top-word scores across the window then ranks (agg pattern). Both are tiny, portable, and vendor-agnostic in concept.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No hardcoded secrets (key from env, fail-fast `raise ValueError` if missing — good). BUT GraphQL queries are built via **f-string interpolation** of user/LLM-supplied `asset` and `metric` values into the query string — a GraphQL injection vector from a prompt-injection angle (asset strings could alter the query). No validation/escaping. Whole edge is third-party data passthrough (sentiment balances etc. come from Santiment, not computed locally).
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Santiment covers major coins, not memecoins with meaningful DEX-only social data; vendor-gated (no chain portability) and not GMGN-independent. The only ownable edge is the thresholded baseline-shift alert + cross-window aggregation math, which is trivial to reimplement (SRC-058-style). Not worth adopting the Santiment coupling.
- **Verdict:** **Skip** — vendor-token passthrough for major-coin sentiment, injection-prone query building. **Study** the baseline-shift alert pattern (apply to Memeland's own volume/activity series) without adopting the source.

### SRC-127 kukapay/dexscreener-trending-mcp (JavaScript CJS, ~4 files, single index.js)
- **What it is:** MCP server with one tool (`get_trending_pairs`) that scrapes DexScreener's public HTML page (BSC or Solana) and one analysis prompt template. No API key. It fetches the page, regex-extracts the embedded `window.__SERVER_DATA` JSON, runs it in a `node:vm` sandbox, pulls `route.data.dexScreenerData.pairs`, formats to clean objects, sorts by 24h volume, and returns both a Markdown table and structured JSON.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** (1) The **DexScreener page-scrape with no API key** technique (`fetch` + `window.__SERVER_DATA` regex + `vm.runInNewContext(timeout:1000)`) — GMGN-independent and free, though fragile against DexScreener markup changes and unofficial-scraping ToS risk. (2) Clean pair-normalization schema (name/symbol/address/priceUsd/volume24h/priceChange24h/liquidityUsd/marketCap/boostsActive/pairAddress). (3) Returning **both** a readable Markdown table and `structuredContent` JSON for downstream processing — a nice MCP output pattern Memeland can mirror.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Running untrusted page JS through `vm.runInNewContext` with regex-extracted code is a code-execution surface (sandbox mitigates but relies on the regex capturing only JSON; `displayErrors:true` leaks internals). Rate-limit-free scraping of DexScreener is ToS-fragile and breaks silently. Hardcoded `CHAIN_URLS` only for bsc/solana — no RH chain 4663. Note: the "sort by volume" line is actually commented out (`//const sorted = allPairs.sort(...)`), a bug — output isn't actually sorted.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Free, no-key, DexScreener-driven trending data is directly relevant to memecoin discovery and is portable-ish (DexScreener has RH/Arbitrum endpoints too, though this only maps bsc/solana). But it's a **scraper**, not an edge; the data is raw, unsorted (bug), and the vm-execution approach is more fragile/risky than using DexScreener's public JSON API (`api.dexscreener.com`) seen in SRC-084. Memeland should prefer the JSON API route, not this HTML-scrape.
- **Verdict:** **Skip** the vm/page-scrape approach. **Study** the pair-normalization schema + Markdown/structured dual output and the DexScreener-feed idea, but implement against DexScreener's official JSON endpoints instead (see SRC-084/226).

### SRC-129 kukapay/jupiter-mcp (JavaScript CJS, ~4 files, single index.js)
- **What it is:** MCP server for swapping on Solana via Jupiter's ULTRA API (DEX routing + RFQ). Two tools: `get-ultra-order` (fetch an order + unsigned transaction object) and `execute-ultra-order` (sign the transaction with the in-process wallet via `@solana/web3.js` and POST it back to Jupiter Ultra to execute). Wallet `PRIVATE_KEY` read from env, keypair loaded at startup. Uses real token decimals from the mint (`@solana/spl-token`) to convert a human amount to lamports.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** (1) The **order/execute split** isolates order-quote and finalization — a good idempotency/confirmation shape (mirrors SRC-052's timeout-idempotent execution lesson). (2) Exact-in **decimal conversion** from on-chain mint decimals (`getMint` → `amount * 10**decimals`) instead of hardcoding — correct and portable. (3) Tool errors returned as structured `isError:true` content rather than thrown. (4) It demonstrates signing/landing a real swap in-process — the execution reference Memeland could port to its RH chain 4663 signer (if it captures the unsigned-tx-plus-confirm pattern).
- **Security/complexity flags:** **Private key lives in the same process as an LLM-facing MCP server**, and `execute-ultra-order` signs *whatever base64 transaction the model passes* — a classic prompt-injection money-laundering surface (no validation/allowlist of tx contents). That alone is a strong counterexample to Memeland's security design: Memeland should follow SRC-052's trust-boundary envelope + action-allowlist instead of this trust-everything-serialized-tx style. Also destructures `{requestId, transaction, inputMint, outputMint, amount}` but the schema only declares requestId/transaction (latent undefined vars). Solana-only, Jupiter-API coupled (not portable to RH chain/BSC, though Jupiter's pattern of an unsigned-tx/confirm API is portable in concept).
- **For Memeland / edge check:** No discovery/signal edge — pure execution plumbing for a single chain and a single aggregator. The **unsigned-order-then-confirm execution flow** and **mint-decimals conversion** are the reusable nuggets; the *sign-anything-the-LLM-says* design is a red flag to avoid, not copy.
- **Verdict:** **Skip** for Memeland (Solana/Jupiter-only, insecure-by-default signing surface). **Adapt** at most the order/execute split + mint-decimals conversion into Memeland's own execution layer — but with SRC-052's trust-boundary and tx-allowlist safety.

### SRC-137 LLMQuant/data-mcp (TypeScript, ~60 files incl. large test suite)
- **What it is:** A polished MCP server (`@llmquant/data-mcp`) that fronts the hosted LLMQuant Data API (credit-metered). Tools span quant wiki, research papers, crypto klines/snapshot, Polymarket prediction markets, US equity OHLCV, macro indicators, SEC filings/13F, company news, and personal-portfolio context. Supports stdio local + hosted streamable-HTTP remote modes with session-scoped auth and rate limiting. Data comes from a closed vendor API, not local computation.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** (1) **Error-contract design** in `shared/errors.ts` is exemplary: typed error hierarchy (`LlmquantApiError` w/ stable public codes like `rate_limited`/`insufficient_credits`), a `toLlmquantApiError` that forwards the vendor's sanitized `{error:{code,message}}` envelope verbatim and falls back to a generic fail-closed message — never leaking raw status/URL/body to the LLM. Strong pattern for Memeland's service error surfacing. (2) **ApiClientProvider** abstraction resolving a client from session context (user_id, scopes, `tools:read` check) — clean authz-injection seam. (3) Uniform `formatToolResult({summary, data, meta})` and per-tool registry pattern; env parsing centralized in `env.ts` with tests; `zod` v4 schemas. Very well-tested (each tool has unit + contract tests).
- **Security/complexity flags:** No hardcoded secrets (only test fixtures); session-scoped authz + internal secret via env is solid. But it's a **vendor-credit-metro meter**: every call can deduct credits, and the data is 90% equities/macro/wiki — a **heavy** dependency surface (fastmcp, zod, tsup build, full test matrix). Crypto support is minimal (CEX-style klines for major coins, ticker in BASE-QUOTE), irrelevant to memecoin DEX tokens.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** No algorithmic edge (pure data access to a SaaS). The **error-envelope contract** and **authz-provider seam** are genuinely portable green ideas for Memeland's TS service layer, and the test discipline is a model. But the domain (equities/macro/polymarket, CEX crypto) and the vendor-metering model don't fit a simple, self-hosted, GMGN-independent memecoin bot.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** its error-contract + api-provider patterns (and testing rigor) into Memeland; **Skip** the server/data source itself (vendor-metered, equity-domain, heavy).

### SRC-143 manavaga/web3-signals-mcp (Python, ~90 files incl. ML models + big research docs)
- **What it is:** A full multi-agent crypto signal-intelligence system (not just an MCP wrapper). Five independent agents (whale flows, technicals, derivatives, narrative, market microstructure) each score assets 0–100; a `signal_fusion` engine combines them into a composite signal with directional label, calibrated probability, momentum tracking, LLM rationale, and 24h/48h self-grading of its own calls. Runs as FastAPI + MCP (SSE), Postgres/SQLite storage, x402 micropayment gate, Railway-deployed. Covers top-20 majors, not memecoins.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** This is the strongest algorithmic-edge source so far. (1) **IC-based per-asset weight learning**: weights per dimension fitted from Spearman/Pearson/Kendall correlations between past dimension scores and forward returns (`optimizer.py`), with config-driven asymmetric bullish/bearish weight sets. (2) **Walk-forward backtesting + Benjamini–Hochberg FDR correction** to avoid false discoveries across many features. (3) **Platt-scaled probability calibration** (`calibrator.py`: raw score→P(correct direction) via sigmoid(A*score+B), per-dimension, with linear fallback). (4) **Adaptive regime gating**: Fear&Greed-index-driven abstain zone + dampened contrarian bias in confirmed downtrends (`engine.py`). (5) **Config-driven engine** (`profiles/default.yaml`): all rules/thresholds/weights in YAML, engine has "zero domain logic" — cleanly testable. (6) Whale-credibility scoring (wallet size + label + smart-money score + net accumulation window). Whole repo ships self-graded accuracy rather than self-reported claims.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No hardcoded secrets (all keys via env; scanned `0x` addresses are public contracts/hot wallets, not keys; one commented "never hardcode" note + default-arg key in a payment bot). But it is **heavy**: Python ML stack (lightgbm, scikit-learn, scipy, pandas), Postgres, x402/CDP, Anthropic, multi-agent orchestration — overkill for Memeland's "simple, not too heavy" goal. LLM rationales add a vendor dependency. Major-coin (BTC/ETH/SOL...) focus with Coinglass/Binance/Arkham data — those feeds don't exist for long-tail DEX memecoins on RH chain 4663.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** The **signal-fusion architecture and its ML discipline are a green-label blueprint** even though the domain differs: config-driven per-asset IC-weight learning, Platt calibration to make scores meaningful, walk-forward + FDR-corrected validation, and honest self-grading. Memeland can port the *methodology* (IC weighting, calibration, abstain/regime gating) to its memecoin dimensions (holder flows, bundling, volume momentum, narrative) in lighter TS. Do NOT adopt the Python/ML/vendor stack wholesale.
- **Verdict:** **Adopt** the IC-weight-learning + Platt-calibration + regime-gating + walk-forward-validation methodology as the core of Memeland's scoring edge; **Skip** the repo's heavy Python ML/Postgres/x402/LLM stack and its major-coin data coupling. Highest-value source so far.

### SRC-215 tony-42069/solana-mcp (JavaScript CJS, ~22 files, no tests despite jest dep)
- **What it is:** A Solana "Memecoin Observatory" MCP server aimed at memecoin discovery/analysis: scan new memecoins, hype score (social + on-chain), whale tracking, meme-culture correlation, rugpull scan, and portfolio strategy. Heavy emphasis on social/cultural signals (Twitter via `twitter-api-v2`, Reddit/Telegram via crawls, `puppeteer` + `cheerio`). On-chain via `@solana/web3.js` + Jupiter for liquidity. SQLite-backed. Cron schedulers for token discovery/social/meme updates.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** (1) **Weighted opportunity-scoring model** (`scoring_algorithm.md`): on-chain 0.3 + social 0.3 + meme-correlation 0.2 + safety 0.2, with sub-scores for novelty/supply/holder-distribution/liquidity and weighted sentiment across Twitter/Reddit/Telegram — a reasonable template for Memeland's composite score. (2) **On-chain safety analysis** (`safetyUtils`): mint-authority, freeze-authority, top-holder %, liquidity checks → safetyScore (mirrors SRC-084's rubric, independently derived). (3) Dynamic function loading from a `functions/` dir into MCP schemas — clean tool-registration pattern. (4) Rugpull = 0.7*safety + 0.3*scam-similarity blend, risk-level bands.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No hardcoded secrets found (keys via env; `accessToken`/`apiKey` hits were false positives from config getters). But `allowedOrigins: ['*']` + bare `cors()` on an HTTP server exposing wallet/strategy endpoints is a permissive default. Several functions **require a pre-populated SQLite DB** (`SELECT * FROM tokens WHERE address = ?`) and throw "Token not found" otherwise — i.e. it's a scaffold that needs `init-sample-data` seeding; REPO_STATUS.md itself reports 0% code-quality/documentation/testing. `getPortfolioStrategy.js` uses Anthropic SDK (LLM-dependent advice). Social layer leans on fragile scrapers (Twikit-style/puppeteer) and Twitter API that no longer matches modern reality.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Conceptually the closest domain fit (memecoins, hype + safety + whales + meme-correlation) and the weighted scoring rubric is portable to Memeland's dimensions (on-chain + narrative + safety). But implementation is an incomplete scaffold: DB-coupled, LLM-coupled for strategy, scrapers for social, Solana-only. Memeland should borrow the *scoring rubric shape* (weights + sub-scores + safety checks) but implement with real RH-chain/BSC on-chain data (see SRC-084 for a cleaner on-chain implementation).
- **Verdict:** **Study** the weighted opportunity-score + safety-rubric template and dynamic function-loader; **Skip** the code itself (unfinished scaffold, DB/LLM/scraper-coupled, Solana-only, permissive CORS).

### SRC-091 Erfaniaa/financial-indexes-correlation (Python, ~6 files)
- **What it is:** Tiny educational script (~150 LOC) that downloads stock/commodity/crypto index daily closes from two sources (Nasdaq Data Link + yfinance), aligns all series onto a common date range (fill-forward / drop-extra logic), and prints + saves a Pearson correlation matrix to CSV. No DB, no service layer, pure pandas.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** (1) Cross-source time-series alignment: `fill_missing_data` forward-fills the last value into calendar gaps and `remove_extra_data` clips to the window — the right way to line up daily series with mismatched trading calendars (crypto trades every day, stocks don't). (2) Simple pluggable source dispatch in `get_data_for_pair_name` keyed by a `data_source` string — a minimal fallback-router seed. (3) A `(pair, column, source)` config tuple list that decouples *what* you fetch from *how* you fetch it.
- **Security/complexity flags:** API key read from a local `API_KEY` file (not hardcoded) — fine. No hardcoded secrets, no network exposure, no dangerous defaults. Extremely low complexity; but `pd.concat` in a per-row loop is O(n^2)-ish and slow on long ranges.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** The *methodology* (align mixed calendars → compute cross-asset correlation) is a useful regime/edge signal: correlating BTC/ETH/BNB price action with a memecoin sector proxy could gate "risk-on" windows. But the data sources are CEX/stock indexes (yfinance, Nasdaq) — not relevant for on-chain memecoin signal, and the code is a demo not a library. Reusable only as a ~40-line TS correlation helper; the fill-forward alignment trick is the portable piece.
- **Verdict:** **Study** (time-series alignment + correlation technique only); **Skip** as a data source / codebase for Memeland (CEX/equity feeds, toy scale).

### SRC-154 monarchjuno/tradingcodex (Python/Django, ~600 files)
- **What it is:** "Local-first investment operating system" — a Django 5.2 monorepo that layers a research/decision memory system on top of the Codex CLI. A Head Manager dynamically selects specialist analyst agents (technical, macro, valuation, portfolio, risk, judgment-reviewer) whose outputs become versionable workspace artifacts (research, decisions, wikis, brains). Paper-first: research/LLM output never becomes a broker action on its own — provider connection, ticket drafting, approval, and execution are separate service-gated checkpoints. Ships a CLI (`tcx attach`), a read-only web viewer, and an MCP layer.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** (1) **Idempotent execution reservation** (`apps/orders/services.py`): `execution_idempotency_key` derived from ticket/portfolio/account/strategy, row-locked `select_for_update`, replay-safe (`IntegrityError` → return existing), explicit `RESERVED → finalize → NEEDS_REVIEW` state machine. (2) **Approval receipt locking**: receipt stores the exact order-payload hash, has an expiry, must not be superseded/consumed, and the ticket must be `APPROVED` before submission — an LLM cannot mutate an order after it is approved. (3) **Deny-first capability RBAC** (`apps/policy/services.py`): principal+action+resource_pattern allow/deny with `deny` overriding `allow`, stale-allow pruning, retired-role deactivation. (4) **Append-only audit** (`apps/audit/models.py`): every event stores request_hash/result_hash + payload, `save` rejects updates and `delete` raises. (5) Research-source fallback ladder (reuse snapshot → enabled skill/MCP → optional OpenBB → primary sources → evidence gap) — GMGN-independence compatible.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Very well-designed security posture (fail-closed, append-only audit, idempotent execution, credentials never in prompts/artifacts, explicit "needs review" on uncertain provider outcomes). Flags: it is a Codex-CLI+Django+uv runtime dependency (Node-free end-user, Python-only) — not a library you can drop into TS. Heavy machinery (Django ORM, migrations, MCP runtime, viewer frontend, workspace-templates skill bundles) far beyond Memeland's scale. Low risk, high effort.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** The **execution-governance patterns** are the portable gold: an approval gate that locks the exact order payload hash + expiry + single-consumption, an idempotency key derived from order context, a `RESERVED → finalize → NEEDS_REVIEW` lifecycle for uncertain provider outcomes, and an append-only audit log. These map 1:1 onto Memeland's execute path on RH 4663/BSC. The agent-team/memory layer is overkill and Codex-coupled for a personal TS bot. Re-implement the ~4 service functions in TS (a few hundred lines) rather than importing this framework.
- **Verdict:** **Adopt** (execution-governance concepts: idempotent reservation, payload-hash-locked approval receipts, append-only audit, deny-first capability RBAC — port to TS, not heavy); **Skip** (the Django/Codex runtime itself).

### SRC-213 tiagosiebler/OrderBooks (TypeScript, ~6 source files + samples)
- **What it is:** A tiny, well-known npm library (`orderbooks`, by the CCXT/Bybit SDK author) for maintaining in-memory LOB (limit order book) state across many symbols: feed CLOB `snapshot` and `delta` (delete/update/insert) events, keep books trimmed to a max depth (balanced per side), query best bid/ask, spread in % or basis points, and estimate slippage for a market order by simulating level-by-level fills. Zero runtime dependencies; compiled TS with JSDoc-typed single source files (`src/OrderBook.ts`, `src/OrderBooksStore.ts`, `src/OrderBookLevel.ts`). Ships Bybit + Binance + a slippage-test sample.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** (1) `OrderBooksStore.getEstimatedSlippage(baseOrderSize, side)` walks the relevant side of the book, fills level-by-level (`fillQty = min(remaining, level.qty)`), returns `executionPrice`, `slippagePercent`, `slippageBasisPoints`, and throws if the order cannot be fully filled. (2) Snapshot/delta state-machine with timestamp ordering guard (`checkTimestamps`) to reject out-of-order ticks. (3) Multi-symbol `Record<string, OrderBook>` store that lazy-creates a book per symbol — a clean in-memory per-pair state map pattern. (4) `trimToMaxDepth` keeps memory bounded by trimming top sells / bottom buys evenly. ~0 deps, very portable.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No secrets, no network, no I/O beyond `console.table` printing; pure in-memory data structure. Minor: `structuredClone` on every `getBookState` call is a perf/GC cost on high-frequency feeds; `handleDelta` inserts without dedup in one path (minor correctness smell). Overall low risk, well-maintained by a known crypto-SDK author.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Directly usable as-is *if* Memeland trades CLOB perpetuals (Bybit/Binance). For memecoins on RH 4663 + Solana AMM pools there is no CLOB LOB, but the **level-by-level impact simulation** maps exactly to DEX pool-depth slippage estimation (walk pool reserves to compute price impact for a given trade size). The slippage-estimator function and the multi-symbol store pattern are the portable pieces; reimplement the fill-walk against a pool's reserve curve rather than importing this LOB library wholesale. Real edge: yes, slippage/impact-aware entry sizing is an execution edge; lightweight and chain-portable.
- **Verdict:** **Adopt** (the slippage-impact fill-simulation pattern — port to pool-depth impact calc in TS); **Adapt** (use the LOB class directly only for CLOB perp legs).

### SRC-222 vincentkoc/dexscraper (Python, ~12 files + 8 test modules)
- **What it is:** A GPL-3, well-tested PyPI CLI/SDK that scrapes DexScreener in real time via its **keyless public WebSocket endpoint** `wss://io.dexscreener.com/dex/screener/v5/pairs/{tf}/1`. Builds a URL from `rankBy[key|order]` + a rich query-param filter set (chainIds, dexIds, liquidity/volume/txn min-max, pairAge, priceChange h1/h6/h24, fdv, marketCap, boosts, pumpfun maxAge/launchpadProgress), then parses the (partly binary) WS payload with heavy regex + IEEE-754 field classification heuristics to extract token profiles, OHLC/OHLCVT, trading pairs, and metadata (addresses, urls, protocol, age). Ships CLI presets (`trending`, `top`, `gainers`, `new_pairs`, `boosted`, `pumpfun_trending`), a Python SDK, output to JSON/OHLC/MT5, and optional cloudscraper Cloudflare bypass.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** (1) **Free, keyless, multi-chain DexScreener screener feed** covering Solana, BSC, Arbitrum, Ethereum, Base, etc. — a direct GMGN-independent discovery feed aligned with Memeland's goals. (2) `ScrapingConfig.build_websocket_url()` + `Filters.to_query_params()` — the exact filter/rank param encoding (trendingScore*, volume, txns, liquidity, priceChange*, pairAge) is copyable verbatim. (3) Preset configs (`gainers`, `new_pairs`, `top_volume`, `boosted_only`, `pumpfun_trending` with maxAge/launchpad) are ready-made memecoin discovery screens. (4) `Filters` dataclass + `to_query_params` is a clean example of typed config → query-string translation. (5) Extensive test suite (test_scraper, test_config, test_edge_cases, test_enhanced_ohlc) — good discipline for a fragile scraper.
- **Security/complexity flags:** **Hardcoded** transparency risk: none hardcoded except `Maxsize`/UA rotation arrays. Flag: the "enhanced" binary-protocol extraction (`pairs_pos + 20` offset, magic-tolerance ranges, whitelist/blacklist token-name heuristics, IEEE-754 value classification) is **reverse-engineered and extremely fragile** — it WILL break when DexScreener changes their stream; high maintenance. `cloudscraper` Cloudflare bypass (optional `--cloudflare-bypass`) is a ToS-gray-area practice and a heavy dependency. `requests`/`cloudscraper`/`websockets` deps are heavyweight for a data feed. Proxy via `DEXSCRAPER_PROXY` env (no hardcoded credentials).
- **For Memeland / edge check:** The real value: reuse DexScreener's documented WebSocket feed (solve the two-message handshake) with the filter/rank encoding for memecoin discovery across RH 4663 (Arbitrum), BSC, and Solana. Real edge: yes — a live, keyless screener is genuinely useful and reduces GMGN coupling. But do NOT port the fragile binary/heuristic parser for live signals; Memeland is better off using DexScreener's stable REST JSON API (pairs/boosts/search) plus this repo only as the query-param reference. Low complexity to reconstruct in TS (a WS client + URL builder).
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** (keyless multi-chain DexScreener feed via WS/REST + filter/rank param encoding + discovery presets — port to TS, drop the binary-parse heuristic and the cloudscraper bypass); **Skip** (the fragile protocol reverse-engineering & GPU-adjacent Python deps).

### SRC-226 w3laba/DexScreener-Trending (JavaScript/Node, 1 script + README)
- **What it is:** NOT a data extraction tool. It is the client bot for a paid marketing service ("we guarantee #TOP 1-10 for 24h", "$1500/24h", also sells reactions/utils for other trackers). `dexscreener_main.js` repeatedly opens visible Puppeteer browsers at `https://dexscreener.com` through rotated SOCKS proxies from `proxy.txt` with a `puppeteer-with-fingerprints` API key, to manufacture traffic and game DexScreener's trending algorithm. Config is read from a `config.json` (not shipped, gitignored) with thread count / restart / delay knobs.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** Essentially none for legitimate data — and the pattern herein is **abusive**: bot-fingerprint spoofing (`plugin.fetch(fingerkey)`), proxy rotation with username/password parsed from a text file, repeated headless-not browser visits to inflate trending. The only thing worth *learning from* is defensive: DexScreener/Dextools "trending" ranks are gamed by paid traffic, so trending lists are a **low-trust, manipulable signal** — Memeland should never treat trending rank alone as alpha.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Loads of risk with little code touch: loads proxy **credentials from `proxy.txt`** and reads a fingerprint API key — it would launch `headless:false` visible windows, depends on a paid fingerprint-API service, and violates DexScreener ToS (traffic manipulation). No tests, no docs beyond a pricing menu, huge maintenance surface against bot-detection changes.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** No reusable code, data, or algorithm. It flags a market-structure fact (trending is paid/manipulated) rather than offering an edge. Anything Memeland would *not* want to adopt; the only positive is the listed chain requirements (SOL LP>30k/vol>500k; ETH/BSC LP>30k) as rough thresholds — but those come from an untrustworthy marketing page.
- **Verdict:** **Skip** (traffic-bot for trending manipulation; no usable data/signal, ToS risk, credential-from-file). Note the manipulation risk in Memeland research: do not weight DexScreener/Dextools trending heavily.

### SRC-223 vybenetwork/solana-top-holders-api (TypeScript/Express, ~18 files + frontend)
- **What it is:** An official Vybe Network demo/start-kit — an Express proxy server + framework-free web dashboard (the "Solana Top Token Holders & Whales API" app). Given a token mint it fetches token metadata (`/v4/tokens/{mint}`), top holders (`/v4/tokens/{mint}/top-holders`), recent trades (`/v4/trades`), labeled program accounts, and (optional) top traders. Computes holder-concentration metrics (Top-10/Top-100 % of supply sums), cohort distributions, labeled vs unlabeled supply rollups, and USD value estimates. Requires a `VYBE_API_KEY` (keyed, IP-restrictable); MIT licensed.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** (1) **Clean axios wrapper** (`src/api/client.ts`): X-API-Key instance + `withRetry` (3 retries / 2s) + `toHumanReadableError` that maps 403/404/5xx to actionable messages — a genuinely portable, well-done API-client pattern. (2) **On-disk JSON cache** for symbol + program-label lookups (mint→symbol, program→label) to avoid re-hitting paid endpoints (`src/cache.ts`). (3) **Holder-concentration risk math** (frontend): sum % of supply for Top-10/Top-100, labeled-vs-unlabeled supply split, cohort USD vs market-cap share — a ready-made "is this token concentrated/manipulated" rubric. (4) Typed `src/types/api.ts` interfaces matching the vendor response shapes. (5) Batch POST symbol/label warmup endpoints to prefill caches.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Environment-key-only (throws if `VYBE_API_KEY` missing); key never logged. No hardcoded secrets — `.env` only, key server-side. Reasonable: 60s timeout, retries. Flags: **Solana/SPL/Token-2022 only** (no RH chain 4663/BSC); proprietary **VYBE_API_KEY dependency** (costs, IP-restricted; a vendor gate vs Memeland's "low-vendor" goal); `data/` disk caches unbounded (no eviction/TTL); the "whale/concentration" output is frontend-only logic, not a library.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** The **holder-concentration + entity-labeling risk signal** is directly useful for Memeland's on-chain risk rubric (aligns with the SRC-084 holder-concentration finding) — "top 10 hold X% of supply / labeled vs unlabeled" is a real rug-gate input on Solana. But it is vendor-keyed and Solana-only; for cross-chain portability Memeland should compute the same sums itself via RPC token-account enumeration rather than a paid keyed API. Port: the axios retry/error client, the disk-cache pattern, and the concentration-sum logic (~40 lines). Simple, small-deps TS — green-label friendly in design, not in data source.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** (concentration/entity-label risk calculus + axios-retry + disk-cache patterns — reimplement on top of cross-chain RPC; do not call Vybe per-token); **Skip** (the proprietary keyed Solana-only data source & demo dashboard as-is).

### SRC-224 vybenetwork/solana-top-traders-api (TypeScript/Express, ~18 files)
- **What it is:** Official Vybe demo/start-kit, near-identical architecture to SRC-223 (same Express proxy + framework-free SPA + on-disk cache). Focus: **top-trader/wallet intelligence**. Supports two modes — *token mode* (rank the top PnL wallets for a given mint via `/v4/tokens/{mint}/top-pnl-traders`) and *wallet mode* (query a wallet's PnL & trades via `/v4/wallets/{owner}/pnl`), plus the shared top-traders leaderboard `/v4/wallets/top-traders` sorted by `realizedPnlUsd` over a `resolution` (1d/30d), labeled-program lookups, and token metadata. MIT; `VYBE_API_KEY` required.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** (1) **"Smart-money" led by realized PnL** — ranking wallets by `realizeedPnlUsd` at a resolution is exactly the signal Memeland wants to follow top traders (aligns with the SRC-143 informed-trader idea). The concept is portable even though this data is Vybe-only. (2) Same clean `withRetry`/`toHumanReadableError` axios client and disk-cache pattern as SRC-223. (3) `encodeURIComponent` on mint/owner path params (correct path injection hygiene, worth copying). (4) Pagination + sort-by asc/desc param encoding shared across endpoints. (5) Two-mode (asset-centric vs wallet-centric) query design is a useful UX/serving split.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Same as SRC-223: `.env`-keyed only (throws if missing), key never logged, 60s timeout + retries; **Solana-only** and **VYBE_API_KEY vendor-gated** (paid/IP-restricted). `top-traders` default `limit=1000` per page is heavy; no rate-limit handling for the free tier. No hardcoded secrets found. Data-source coupling is the main weakness for Memeland.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** The **follow-the-profitable-trader** pattern (rank wallets by realized PnL, copy/screen their picks) is a real edge and directly portable in principle — but this repo cannot feed RH 4663/BSC and leans on a paid Solana vendor. For Memeland: implement the same "top PnL trader → their current holdings → momentum alignment" loop yourself where feasible (Derive/equivalent RPC or DEX aggregator data), reuse only the client/retry/cache shell. Combinable with SRC-143's IC-weight approach: weight traders by their own realized PnL (their "track record") rather than trusting labels.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** (realized-PnL-ranked trader-following concept + axios-retry/cache).

### SRC-225 vybenetwork/solana-trader-pnl-api (TypeScript/Express, ~18 files)
- **What it is:** Official Vybe demo/start-kit, same architecture family as SRC-223/224 (Express proxy + framework-free SPA + on-disk cache). Focus: **per-wallet PnL**. Given a wallet address or partial name (`ilikeFilter`), fetches token-level wallet PnL (`/v4/wallets/{owner}/pnl`), a related-wallets / top-traders leaderboard (`/v4/wallets/top-traders`), token-scoped top-PnL traders (`/v4/tokens/{mint}/top-pnl-traders`), supporting trades, program labels, and token metadata, all at a chosen `resolution` window and sort. MIT; `VYBE_API_KEY` required.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** (1) **Per-wallet token-level PnL history = a trader's verifiable track record** — the basis for weighting whose signals to trust (combine with SRC-143 IC-weight learning: use realized PnL as the prior/confidence for a trader's edge). (2) **Related-wallet discovery** (same `ilikeFilter`/mint → cluster of wallets trading the same tokens) — useful for detecting coordinated wallets/bundles. (3) Identical clean `withRetry`/`toHumanReadableError` axios client, `encodeURIComponent` path hygiene, disk-cache pattern as SRC-223/224. (4) `resolution` + sort-asc/desc + pagination param convention repeated consistently across endpoints.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Same strengths/flags as SRC-223/224: `.env`-keyed only, key never logged, 60s timeout + retries, no hardcoded secrets. **Solana-only, VYBE_API_KEY vendor-gated** (paid/IP-restricted free tier), unbounded disk caches, heavy default `limit`. For Memeland the blocker is data-source and chain coverage, not code quality (this is clean starter-kit TS).
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Strong conceptual alignment with Memeland's "follow profitable traders" idea: a wallet's per-token realized PnL over time is the raw material for a **smart-money / informed-holder signal** on the Solana leg. But the data is Vybe-gated and Solana-only; Memeland needs an on-chain equivalent for RH 4663/BSC (e.g., replay swaps from a public RPC/aggregator and compute realized PnL yourself). Reuse: the client/cache shell + the "wallet PnL → weight → top related wallets" query flow, not the vendor call. Keep it lightweight: this is genuinely simple TS to reimplement.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** (per-wallet realized-PnL track-record + related-wallet/bundle discovery concepts — implement against public RPC across chains); **Skip** (the proprietary keyed Solana-only API & demo UI).


## Batch 12 - trading-risk, agents & chain-execution deep-read (subagent Meitner)

### SRC-041 51bitquant/ai-hedge-fund-crypto (Python, ~40 files)
- **What it is:** LangGraph-based LLM hedge-fund framework for Binance CEX. Builds a configurable DAG workflow: Start -> per-interval DataNodes (parallel timeframe fetch) -> merge -> strategy nodes -> RiskManagementNode -> PortfolioManagementNode (LLM makes final buy/sell/short/cover decisions). Ships MACD/RSI/custom strategies, a long+short backtester, indicator library (ADX, ATR, RSI, Bollinger, Hurst, vol regime, momentum), and a Binance gateway/WS client.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - Config-driven multi-timeframe DAG: strategies and intervals declared in `config.yaml`, nodes instantiated dynamically from a registry - good template for composing Memeland's voters without hardcoding.
  - `general_indicators.py`: deterministic TA (weighted signal combination, Hurst exponent, ADX, vol-regime z-score) that could inform the quant voter as a TS port.
  - `backtester.py`: long+short position accounting (weighted cost basis, margin for shorts, realized PnL, gross/net exposure, long-short ratio) and metrics (Sharpe, Sortino, max drawdown) - reasonable reference for Memeland's dry-run/trade-journal attribution.
  - RiskManagementNode: per-ticker 20% single-position cap with remaining-limit and max-shares pre-computed BEFORE the LLM, so the LLM only sizes within pre-checked bounds (fail-soft shape).
- **Security/complexity flags:**
  - Final decisions are made by an LLM that is only *prompted* to respect max-shares/cash/margin; rules are not enforced programmatically - high-risk if copied as-is. No fail-closed gate.
  - `.env`/`config.yaml` are placeholders; no hardcoded keys found. Binance keys live in env as expected.
  - Heavy dependency stack (langchain/langgraph/pandas/matplotlib/uv); full platform weight, not a lean module.
  - Backtester invokes the whole LLM graph per bar (slow, expensive); `mismatched lengths` hard-fail on data alignment.
- **For Memeland / edge check:**
  - Not chain-native (CEX OHLCV only), not memecoin/flow/rug aware, and decision logic is LLM-first rather than deterministic-gates-first. The reusable value is only the deterministic indicator math and the "pre-compute limits, LLM sizes within them" pattern - both small ports.
  - Would add Python/pandas/langchain runtime for marginal edge over Memeland's existing TS voters; not green for live path.
- **Verdict:** **Study** - keep the deterministic multi-timeframe indicator library and the pre-computed-position-limit pattern for the quant/risk voters; skip the LangGraph/LLM decision architecture and the platform itself.


### SRC-042 555cute/r20-quantum-trader (Python + Vue/TS, ~479 py + 195 ts/vue)
- **What it is:** A full institutional-style, multi-exchange (OKX/Binance/Gate) futures quant OS: macro market-regime detection via "calculus" (velocity/acceleration/jerk/energy), a multi-slot LLM "council" (cross-exam debate + CIO arbiter), configurable Prompt Studio, 17-item fail-closed physical risk gates, policy version snapshots with hash rollback, self-evolution ledger learning, multi-tier logging/audit, and a big Vue admin/dashboard frontend. Python FastAPI backend + scheduler daemons.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - `scripts/risk_constants.py` = single source of truth for all hard risk thresholds (env-driven, DEFAULTS table shared with UI schema so display can't drift from engine). Strong pattern for Memeland risk config.
  - `scripts/order_risk.py` + `r20_backend/execution/risk_gates.py` = deterministic, pure, fail-closed gates: R:R floor, leverage/margin clamp, cross-venue same-direction exposure reject (reject not clamp), NaN/Inf/finite checks, "LLM has proposal rights only, physical gates have veto" philosophy - exactly Memeland's fail-closed shape.
  - `scripts/backtest/metrics.py` = pure-function performance metrics (win rate, profit factor capped at 99.0, Sharpe/Sortino/Calmar, avg R), entry candidate with 2x ATR stop + rr take-profit + risk-% sizing. Good TS-portable backtest math.
  - `scripts/calculus/regime.py` = four-state macro regime (trend expansion / wide oscillation / low-vol choppy / volatility shock) with trend+volatility scores feeding strategy preset selection - a cleaner regime abstraction than Memeland's current regime voter.
  - Council cross-exam (independent proposals -> cross-question -> CIO final) and 0.5s atomic policy snapshot/hash rollback are credible governance patterns, though LLM-heavy.
- **Security/complexity flags:**
  - No hardcoded private keys found; credentials live in `.env`/admin Fernet-encrypted store; demo/live axes are separate and cross-authorization is explicitly refused. Good security hygiene.
  - Enormous footprint: 1200+ tests, 4000+ line single-function history (now refactored), Vue frontend, multiple daemons, backup bridges, notification fan-out - far too heavy for a personal bot.
  - CEX futures-only (OKX/Binance/Gate), no on-chain/memecoin/flow/rug awareness, no Robinhood Chain/Solana path. Not portable to Memeland's venues.
  - Past silent-failure incident (missing import inside swallowed `except: pass` caused 30h zero-price blackout) shows the codebase's own fragility at scale; they added free-name static gates.
- **For Memeland / edge check:**
  - The four-state regime detector + pure backtest metrics + single-source risk constants are the only genuinely reusable ideas, and they are small deterministic TS ports, not the platform. R:R floor / exposure-reject gates already exist in Memeland's RiskManager shape.
  - Platform adoption would add Python/FastAPI/Vue/scheduler weight for zero chain edge. Not green as a system.
- **Verdict:** **Study** (regime four-state detector, pure-function backtest metrics, single-source risk-config pattern as TS ports) / **Skip** (the platform, council/LLM stack, CEX futures execution).


### SRC-053 AmadeusGB/alpha-arena (Python MVP, ~13 files)
- **What it is:** A thin LLM trading-decision comparison MVP: runs OpenAI vs Claude heads against 5 CEX spot tickers (price-only, polling), scores each model's trade decisions, no account/trade execution/storage/scheduler/risk layer. Basically a demo harness for "which LLM picks better next-bar direction".
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - `adapters/` = abstract `LLMAdapter` base with OpenAI and Claude implementations behind a common `analyze()/make_decision()` interface - a clean seam for Memeland if it ever wants vendor-agnostic LLM voters (already have 7-voter consensus, so marginal).
  - Strict JSON-schema parse of LLM output with **default-HOLD on parse failure/timeout** - a fail-soft shape Memeland already uses for LLM voters that shouldn't abort the loop.
  - Multi-model divergence compare = educational; Memeland already supersedes with its cross-model consensus + vote weighting.
- **Security/complexity flags:**
  - `adapters/exchange_api.py` imports a user-specific absolute path `/Users/binguo/workspaces/cex_scripts/scripts/tools` -> `BitgetVerifiedAPIClient` that is **not in the repo**; the project cannot run standalone (broken/assumed dependency).
  - LLM-only decision with no deterministic risk gate; price-only, no flow/rug/attribution awareness.
  - Keys only in `.env` placeholders; no hardcoded secrets found.
- **For Memeland / edge check:**
