  - CeX spot only, no on-chain/memecoin edge, no execution quality, no risk; does not advance any of Memeland's edge axes. The two ideas it has (default-HOLD on LLM failure, adapter seam) are already present in Memeland's design.
- **Verdict:** **Skip** - thin CeX-only MVP with broken standalone dependency and no edge over Memeland's deterministic-consensus + fail-soft LLM voters. (Note near-duplicate with SRC-171 alpha-arena-okx.)

### SRC-065 brokermr810/QuantDinger (Python/FastAPI + JS frontend, huge SaaS, ~600 py + admin UI) — near-duplicate: this IS `OpenByteInc/QuantDinger` (SRC-177) mirror/fork; product of Open Byte Inc.
- **What it is:** A full self-hosted "AI Trading OS": Python Strategy API V2 + server-side backtesting, paper/live execution across 10+ CEX (Binance/OKX/Bybit/Bitget/Gate/HTX) and brokers (Alpaca/IBKR), Celery durable workers + long-lived trading processes, PostgreSQL/Redis state, audit logs, optional Grafana/Prometheus observability, web+MCP+Agent-Gateway access. Massive commercial-scale monorepo with 500+ backend tests.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - `services/live_trading/spot_sizing.py` = close-size aligned to exchange free balance + open quote buffer (0.995) so buys don't consume 100% of quote and closes don't leave residual dust. Directly portable to RH/Solana/USDC-denominated memecoin buys.
  - `utils/risk_guard.py::trailing_exit_locks_net_profit` = rejects a trailing exit that doesn't clear round-trip fee breakeven (`2*fee + buffer`); and `coerce_fee_rate` defensively normalizes percent vs decimal rates. Exactly Memeland's fail-closed concern (don't "profit"-exit at a net loss).
  - `services/execution_streams/` = durable, idempotent fill/PnL reconciliation: claims orders via `pg_advisory_xact_lock` + 60s refresh-window to avoid duplicate reconciliation, normalizes venue reports into `qd_execution_events`, builds `realized_pnl` from exchange-reported PnL vs internal events. Best-in-class pattern for Memeland's cross-chain "did the trade actually land / what did it cost" execution-quality layer.
  - `services/grid/` = grid bot engine with `levels`, `fill_handler`, `ledger_reconcile`, `order_audit`, `runtime_state` - a clean grid/ranged-reentry reference if Memeland ever does ladder entries.
  - Process-role separation (HTTP API vs scheduler vs trading worker), separate cache-Redis vs durable-job-Redis, non-root read-only prod container, secret-scan + release-gate CI = strong ops hygiene memes.
- **Security/complexity flags:**
  - `env.example` defaults `ADMIN_PASSWORD=123456` (only a first-login reminder) - an unsafe default to note, not to copy; `SECRET_KEY=` empty by design (refuses to start).
  - No hardcoded private keys in source scan; credentials env/encrypted-store driven; gitleaks config + security CI present. Good hygiene.
  - Enormous footprint (FastAPI + Celery + Postgres + Redis + Vue/H5 + MCP + Grafana); CEX/broker only - no on-chain/memecoin/flow/rug awareness, no Robinhood Chain/Solana. Way too heavy for personal bot.
- **For Memeland / edge check:**
  - Green transferable primitives: the fee-aware trailing-exit breakeven guard, free-balance-aligned spot sizing, and the durable idempotent fill/PnL reconciliation claim pattern - all small deterministic TS ports that directly serve Memeland's "execution quality" and "no fake edge" goals.
  - Platform/backtester/strategy-runner is over-fitted to CEX futures+brokers and Postgres/Redis infra; does not help chain-native memecoin edge.
- **Verdict:** **Adopt** (fee-aware trailing-exit breakeven + free-balance spot sizing + idempotent fill/PnL reconciliation as TS modules) / **Skip** (the platform itself). See SRC-177 for the same repo; audit once, apply findings to both.

### SRC-083 Drakkar-Software/OctoBot (Python/asyncio monorepo, ~3200 py files)
- **What it is:** A mature, modular open-source crypto trading platform (Linux/Windows/macOS/RPi) built on a package ecosystem (`octobot-commons`, `octobot-backtesting`, `octobot-trading`, `octobot-evaluators`, `tentacles`, etc.). Real+simulated exchange connectors (ccxt-based), plugin "Tentacle" system for evaluators/strategies/trading modes/services, backtesting engine with data collectors/importers/comparators, portfolio/profitability accounting, web UI, automation (DCA/grid), social/signal feeds. Asynchronous channel/pub-sub core.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - Channel pair pattern: every data stream has `X_updater` (live) + `X_updater_simulator` (replay) emitting on the same channel (kline, markets, funding, ohlcv) so live and backtest consumers share identical logic. This is exactly the topology Memeland should mirror for "same analysis feeds live and historical replay on RH/Solana/BSC."
  - Backtesting engine (`packages/backtesting`): `data/` importer+collector+comparator pipeline, time-management, sqlite market-data DB - a clean reference for building Memeland's own replay harness around stored per-chain candles/trades.
  - Simulated exchange adapter + `trader_simulator` (`exchanges/connectors/simulator`) - models fills/balances/market-filter rules; the `market_filters` concept (exclude untradable symbols) maps to Memeland's "skip rug/honeypot/lp-capped pairs" filtering.
  - TA evaluators as self-describing plugins: `tentacles/Evaluator/TA/{momentum,trend,volatility}` each declare their own user inputs/schema (RSI/trend/volatility evaluators with pertinence scores 0..1) - a nice template for Memeland's deterministic TA voters exposing tunable params.
  - abstract trading mode + portfolio/profitability APIs for consistent accounting across modes.
- **Security/complexity flags:**
  - Only "secret" hit is a fake EC key inside a Coinbase test file - no real hardcoded keys; credentials via config/ccxt env.
  - GNU GPL/LGPL licensed (ignored per task); enormous footprint (async web client+UI+~20 packages+sqlite); Python/asyncio runtime, ccxt CEX-only - no native on-chain memecoin/flow/rug awareness, no Robinhood Chain/Solana execution.
  - Complexity/scale (thousands of files, plugin manifests, proto, web frontend) far exceeds a personal bot's needs.
- **For Memeland / edge check:**
  - The channel live-vs-simulator pattern and the self-describing TA evaluator plugin schema are the two genuinely portable ideas for Memeland's voter + backtest design. The fill/balance/market-filter simulation model is a useful backtesting reference for realistic replay including fees.
  - Platform adoption would drag in an asyncio/Python/tentacles/ccxt/web stack for zero chain-native edge. Not green.
- **Verdict:** **Study** (live/sim channel pattern, evaluator plugin-input schema, realistic simulated-exchange backtester) / **Skip** (the platform).

### SRC-085 edtechre/pybroker (Python, ~65 files, compact backtesting framework)
- **What it is:** A focused, well-engineered backtesting/strategy framework for stocks/ETFs/crypto (Alpaca + Yahoo/AlpacaCrypto data sources). Clean separation: `strategy.py` (execution loop, `Executor`/`Strategy`), `portfolio.py` (portfolio/position accounting, order matching, stops, slippage), `eval.py` (stats & robust evaluation), `optimize.py` (walk-forward + hyperparam search), `indicator.py`/`model.py` (indicator/ML model registration), `scope.py` (per-bar frame + pending orders + symbol rotation/selection). Python/numpy/pandas.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - `portfolio.py` = Decimal-based money handling, `FeeMode` (percent/amount/cash), fractional-share clamping with a `_SHARES_EPSILON` guard so aggregated `pos.shares` never drifts out of step with entry rows (stranded-dust prevention) - a strong, tested recipe for Memeland's position ledger.
  - `Stop` model = stops that trigger on percent / points / bars / exit-price-type with fill price + limit price - a clean declarative exit schema Memeland can mirror for its stop/TP/trailing logic.
  - `eval.py` = bootstrap **BCa confidence intervals + jackknife** estimators for `profit_factor` / `Sharpe` (with epsilon guards `1e-10` to avoid divide-by-zero) - directly addresses Memeland's "learning without double count / is the edge real, not overfit" need. Portable pure functions.
  - `optimize.py` walk-forward + `interval.py` `IntervalBoundIndicator` / multi-timeframe indicator compression - clean multi-timeframe management reference.
  - `scope.py` pending-order + symbol-rotation model (dollar-cost rotation across a symbol set) = a clean way to express "rotate capital across memecoins" with survival/selection.
- **Security/complexity flags:**
  - No hardcoded secrets found; clean, small, unittest-heavy single-purpose lib. Apache 2.0 + Commons Clause (ignored per task, but note it reserves commercial SaaS use - fine for personal use).
  - Python/numpy/pandas stack; primarily CEX/equity data (AlpacaCrypto/yfinance); no on-chain memecoin/flow/rug/honeypot awareness, no custom chain execution.
- **For Memeland / edge check:**
  - The robust-evaluation and portfolio-accounting pieces (BCa CI, jackknife, fee-aware fractional-share ledger, declarative stops, walk-forward) are exactly the kind of deterministic, portable core Memeland should port to TS for its backtest + "is the edge real" validation layer.
  - `vect.py`/numpy vectorized internals keep it fast for a full exchange matrix, but Memeland only needs a subset; platform is equities/CEX-centric so adopt ideas, not the framework.
- **Verdict:** **Study** (read-only reference: BCa bootstrap CI, jackknife profit-factor/Sharpe, Decimal position-ledger with epsilon clamping, declarative stops, walk-forward) - port the small math to TS for Memeland's backtest/replay evaluator; no platform adoption. Consider **Adapt** for the metric functions if Memeland builds a TS backtest analyzer.

### SRC-089 enzoampil/fastquant (Python, ~53 files, thin backtrader wrapper)
- **What it is:** An educational "backtesting for the mainstream" library exposing SMA/EMA/RSI/MACD/BBands/sentiment/buy-hold strategies via a strategy registry and a 3-line `backtest()` API; data from Yahoo/PSE equities and Binance OHLCV; adds walk-forward split, grid-search optimization (via vectorbt), portfolio returns, and Slack notifications. Core is really a thin facade over `backtrader` (+ pandas/numpy).
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**

  - `backtest.py::backtest()` = one-config entrypoint unifying commission, slippage, init_cash, fractional, allow_short/short_max, sentiments, multi-strategy dict - good ergonomic template for a unified Memeland replay CLI.
  - `post_backtest.py` analysis + walk-forward data split for train/test discipline.
  - Data format mapping (`config.py:DATA_FORMAT_MAPPING`) normalizes column-layout CSV dicts - minor.
- **Security/complexity flags:**
  - No hardcoded secrets; tokens via env (Twitter/Slack). MIT license.
  - Heavily reliant on `backtrader` (mostly unmaintained) + pandas/numpy; CEX/equity/PSE only; no short-fill realism for memecoins, no on-chain/flow/rug awareness. Feature set is deliberately beginner-level; optimization path adds vectorbt weight.
- **For Memeland / edge check:**
  - Adds no chain-native edge and duplicates what Memeland's own backtester should do better (fee/slippage handling, per-chain replay). The registry + unified-config ergonomics are the only transferable ideas and are trivial.
  - Not green: educational framework, stale dependency, equity/CEX bias, no memecoin dynamics.
- **Verdict:** **Skip** - thin, backtrader-bound educational wrapper with no chain edge and stale deps; nothing Memeland can use beyond a trivial registry scheme it already has. (No near-duplicate noted.)

### SRC-105 GuntharDeNiro/gunbot-quant (Python + React/JS, ~25 py + frontend)
- **What it is:** Self-hosted quant toolkit by the Gunbot team: a market **screener** with "market character heuristics", a portfolio-level **multi-strategy backtesting engine**, a strategy library (trend/mean-reversion/breakout; also ships JS `gunbot_strategy_files` for the proprietary Gunbot bot), and an optional live Gunbot dashboard/deploy companion. FastAPI + React frontend; uses Binance/ccxt/yfinance data. Includes a merged `combined_code_for_llms.md` (repo intentionally prepared for LLM ingestion).
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - `core/screener.py` heuristic metrics are the standout and directly map to Memeland's bot/rug edge:
    - `volume_concentration_pct` = top-3 volume days / total 90d volume -> flags pump-and-dump/volume-spike concentration (their own doc: avoids "pump-and-dump schemes").
    - `max_daily_spike_pct` = max 90d daily range % -> spike/rug whipsaw; `volatility_consistency` = std of ATR% 90d -> erratic-asset filter.
    - `dist_from_ath_lookback_pct`, `avg_vol_30d_quote` liquidity floor, `roe/roc` momentum, RSI/StochRSI/ADX, `sma50_vs_sma200`. Screener filters are declarative `{metric, condition, value}` triples over an audited metric frame - a clean, portable schema for Memeland's pair-universe screening.
  - `core/backtest_engine.py` returns an `exit_reason_counts` distribution (take-profit/stop/timeout etc.) breakdown + Buy&Hold benchmark alongside equity curve - a good template for Memeland's "why did I lose" post-trade analysis.
  - Numba ATR/ADX/Ichimoku/supertrend/Heikin-Ashi indicator set; strategy engine with `get_entry_signal` / `get_exit_signal` / `update_trailing_stop` separation and ATR-based stop via `price - atr*mult`.
- **Security/complexity flags:**
  - No hardcoded secrets (Binance keys via env); MIT; Python/FastAPI/pandas/numba/ccxt stack + optional `gunbot-sdk-python` (proprietary Gunbot coupling).
  - Backtest weakness: fills at **close price only** with no fees/slippage/partial fills in the main loop, and Sharpe is naively annualized with 365 days - useful conceptually, not financially rigorous. Screener/metrics skip per-trade fee realism.
  - CEX (Binance/cex+yfinance) only, no on-chain/memecoin flow/rug/honeypot awareness, single-venue top-N volume universe.
- **For Memeland / edge check:**
  - The volume-concentration and spike/volatility-consistency heuristics are exactly the deterministic, chain-agnostic signals Memeland wants for "bot vs organic / rug-readiness" screening, computed from raw price/volume - GMGN-independent and TS-portable. Adopt these metric definitions.
  - The `{metric, condition, value}` filter grammar + exit-reason distribution are small, clean patterns to reuse. Backtester itself is too crude (close-price only) and the platform is CEX-centric/Python.
- **Verdict:** **Adopt** (port `volume_concentration_pct`, `max_daily_spike_pct`, `volatility_consistency`, distance-from-ATH + liquidity screening metrics and the declarative filter schema into Memeland's universe/bot-rug voters) / **Skip** (backtest engine & platform - crude fill model, Python, CEX/Gunbot-bound).

### SRC-149 Miasyster/QuantGPT (Python + Rust engine + React/TS, ~100 py + rust + frontend)
- **What it is:** An agent-driven **factor-research engine**: an LLM agent (Claude via MCP, 15 tools) autonomously generates/evaluates/iterates alpha factors, backtests them in a fast **Rust engine**, runs anti-overfit validation, then uploads survivors to QuantGPT Cloud for independent out-of-sample verification. Has mutation/crossover "evolution" engines, market-regime detection, composite building, and a cross-session **knowledge base** (rules/findings/failures markdown). Focused on Chinese equities (baostock/akshare) + WorldQuant BRAIN-style dollar-neutral simulation.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - `quantgpt/anti_overfit.py` is the crown jewel for Memeland's "learning without double count" edge-validation: a **4-test anti-overfit battery** - (1) IC stability (yearly Spearman-IC consistency, no sign reversal), (2) sub-sample stress (IC same-sign across bull/bear/sideways and high/low-vol regimes, >=60% consistency), (3) placebo test (real IC > 95th pct of within-date-permuted ICs + time-shift decay), (4) IC half-life estimation (exponential decay fit, half-life > 5 days). All pure functions on factor/forward-return frames - directly TS-portable for Memeland to prove a voter/edge is real rather than fitted.
  - Knowledge-base convention (`docs/knowledge/{rules,findings,failures}`): one markdown file per discovery, labeled `[Agent+DS Consensus]` vs `[Agent+DS Disagreement]`, dated - a clean anti-double-count, cross-session memory model Memeland can mirror for its voters.
  - `engine/src/backtest/metrics.rs` (sharpe/sortino/max_drawdown/win_rate/profit_factor/spearman/rank_ic) shows a tight, fast metric core (Rust not needed for Memeland scale, but the API surface is a good checklist).
  - `market_regime.derive_market_regime` + rolling/walk-forward validator + `adversarial_validator` - reinforces the "validate across regimes/time" theme.
- **Security/complexity flags:**
  - No hardcoded secrets (DeepSeek/JWT/SMTP keys via `.env` with placeholders); `.env.example` defaults `AUTH_DISABLED=true` with an explicit "never in production" warning (safe default for a local tool, do not copy to a networked bot).
  - Heavy: FastAPI + SQLAlchemy + Celery/Redis + React + Rust build + WorldQuant BRAIN integration; equities-only data (baostock/akshare); factor-research orientation, not a live execution bot, no on-chain/memecoin awareness.
- **For Memeland / edge check:**
  - The 4-test anti-overfit battery and the rules/findings/failures knowledge-base with consensus labels are exactly the deterministic guardrails Memeland wants for "does this voter/edge survive regimes and time" and "learn across sessions without double-counting". Portable, chain-agnostic, GMGN-independent.
  - Whole platform (factor mining, Rust engine, WorldQuant cloud, Chinese-equity data, evolution engines) is out of Memeland's scope and adds heavy infra for zero memecoin/RH-chain edge.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** (port the 4-test anti-overfit battery + the rules/findings/failures knowledge-base and `[Agent+DS Consensus/Disagreement]` convention for Memeland's voter-learning/edge validation) / **Skip** (the factor-mining platform, Rust engine, WorldQuant/equity integration).

### SRC-161 nautechsystems/nautilus_trader (Rust core + Python control plane, very large ~30+ crates)
- **What it is:** Production-grade, Rust-native event-driven trading engine for multi-asset/multi-venue systems. Python is the control plane (strategies/config/orchestration) while the performance-critical engine (event bus, order execution, risk, portfolio, backtester, serialization, indicators) is a compiled Rust core. Adapters for many venues (Binance, OKX, Bybit, Coinbase, Kraken, Hyperliquid, dydx, Deribit, Polymarket, interactive-brokers, etc.). Research + deterministic simulation + live share one architecture so "strategy and execution-algorithm code run identically across backtest and live."
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - `analysis/statistic.py::PortfolioStatistic` = clean abstract interface with `calculate_from_returns`, `calculate_from_realized_pnls`, `calculate_from_positions`, `calculate_from_returns_with_benchmark` - a tidy, TS-portable contract for Memeland's performance-analysis module.
  - The "**same code path for backtest and live**" principle: backtest uses the same order/execution/risk components as live, differing only in transport/persistence. Mirrors Memeland's "deterministic gates identical in replay and live" goal (a strong correctness design to emulate, not copy wholesale).
  - Event-driven component model (`core`/`model`/`execution`/`risk`/`portfolio` crates) with a message bus - the decoupling pattern behind auditability; a `risk` + `portfolio` engine enforcing limits at the engine level (not just in strategy code) = fail-closed by construction.
  - Indicators crate (well-tested TA) + backtest crates with realistic fill/slippage models; adapter pattern isolates each venue behind a common interface.
- **Security/complexity flags:**
  - No hardcoded secrets found; key handling is config/secret-management driven. Strong eng discipline (Rust memory safety, extensive tests, codegen `pyo3`).
  - Enormous footprint: Rust workspace (~25 crates) + Python wrappers + FIX/data adapters + persistence; CEX/Futures/equities + some L1 venues - no Robinhood Chain/memecoin flow/rug awareness; way beyond a personal bot. Compiling Rust adds CI/toolchain burden.
- **For Memeland / edge check:**
  - Reusable ideas are abstractions, not code: the `PortfolioStatistic` interface, "identical backtest/live execution path", and "engine-level (not strategy-level) risk/portfolio constraints". All are things Memeland can structure its own TS modules around without adopting the platform.
  - No GMGN-independent memecoin edge, no chain-native memecoin set; a Rust-backed institutional engine is the opposite of "simple/lightweight" for a personal bot.
- **Verdict:** **Study** (adapt the `PortfolioStatistic` interface, same-code-backtest-and-live principle, engine-level risk/portfolio enforcement into Memeland's TS design) / **Skip** (the platform - Rust core, huge, institutional venue focus, no memecoin/chain-native edge).

### SRC-171 oficcejo/alpha-arena-okx (Python, ~6 files, small single-symbol BTC bot) — near-duplicate NAME of SRC-053 alpha-arena, but a different project (BTC/OKX LLM auto-trader, not an LLM-comparison MVP)
- **What it is:** A personal single-symbol (BTC) OKX perpetual auto-trader: DeepSeek LLM emits BUY/SELL/HOLD with stop_loss/take_profit on 15m/1h OHLCV + MA/RSI/MACD/trend context; deterministic functions then validate/clamp the LLM output before submission; places real OKX exchange TP/SL algorithm orders per fill, with a Streamlit web monitor. Chinese-language README with VPS/afilliate deploy docs.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - `deepseekok2.py::validate_ai_signal` = the meatiest pattern: **deterministic rules that downgrade or override the LLM signal** - RSI>80 BUY (lower confidence), RSI<20 SELL (lower), strong-trend conflict -> force HOLD unless HIGH confidence, MACD-divergence downgrade, and TP/SL sanity clamps (SL must be below entry for BUY, TP above entry, else clamp to ±2-3%). This is a concrete, copyable example of Memeland's "LLM proposes, deterministic gates veto" fail-closed shape.
  - `identify_market_state` = ATR% -anchored regime (high-vol >3%, low-vol <1%) combined with SMA5/20/50 trend strength - a clean two-axis regime classifier.
  - `calculate_dynamic_tp_sl(signal, price, market_state)` = regime/volatility-conditional TP/SL targets instead of fixed percentages - adaptable to Memeland's volatility-aware exits.
  - `safe_json_parse` + `create_fallback_signal` + `analyze_with_deepseek_with_retry(max_retries)` = LLM output robustness (default fallback on parse/API failure, bounded retries) - fail-soft that Memeland already practices.
  - Position sizing helper `calculate_intelligent_position(signal, price, current_position)`.
- **Security/complexity flags:**
  - Keys only in `env.template` (DEEPSEEK/OKX); no hardcoded secrets found in py scan.
  - Personal-grade code quality (monolithic `deepseekok2.py`, `print()` logging, insufficient edge-case hardening); BTC-only on OKX CEX; Chinese doc + referral-linked deploy guides; no on-chain/memecoin awareness, no portfolio-level risk or multi-asset support.
- **For Memeland / edge check:**
  - The LLM-signal validation/clamping rule set and ATR-regime-conditional TP/SL are small, deterministic, and directly portable to Memeland's voter gating - a good concrete template, but the concepts (not the code) are the value; Memeland's RiskManager already covers fail-closed.
  - No GMGN-independent memecoin edge, single-pair CEX execution, no bot/rug/flow signals. Platform is not worth adopting.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** (LLM-signal deterministic validation/clamping rules + ATR-regime-conditional dynamic TP/SL as patterns for Memeland's voter gating and exit sizing) / **Skip** (the platform - single-pair CEX BTC bot, low code quality, no chain-native edge). Note name near-duplicate of SRC-053 alpha-arena; unrelated projects.

### SRC-175 olaxbt/ai-market-maker (Python/LangGraph/FastAPI + Next.js web, ~150 py + ~120 ts/vue)
- **What it is:** A heavy "AI Market-Making" multi-agent system built as a LangGraph workflow recreating the AI-Hedging-Fund "desk" pattern: 9 perception "desks" (monetary sentinel, news/narrative, pattern, statistical alpha, TA, retail hype, pro bias, whale behavior, liquidity/order-flow) each emit a canonical **Tier-0 contract**, then a deterministic **Weight Assigner** fuses them into per-agent composites -> a global weighted composite + consensus-ratio confidence, a **Risk Guard** governance agent with veto power (hard kill-switch env, drawdown stop, gross-leverage clamp), a Tier-1 "Blueprint/Applier" that applies declarative veto rules + multi-factor alpha matrix, and optional LLM arbitrator overlay. Includes its own backtest engine, paper OMS, Nexus proprietary data feed, deploy presets (policy/deploy JSON), and a folio/profile web console. AGPL.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns (this is the closest architectural mirror to Memeland found so far):**
  - `tier1/tier0 contract` canonical JSON schema (`schemas/tier0_contract.py`): every agent's output is normalized to a fixed `{schema_version, agent, ticker, status, <PascalCase fields>}` contract that the arbitrator consumes; a `build_tier0_contract_json` + `tier0_consensus_for_arbitrator` produce deterministic bull/bear tilts with `parts` reasons and a `block_aggressive_long` news-shock flag. This "canonical per-voter contract + rule-based fusion to a small set of booleans" is a first-class template for Memeland's voter consensus.
  - `workflow/weight_assigner.py`: **factor-level normalization** - raw scalars mapped to [0,1] bullish-direction via `_normalize_linear`/`_invert`/clamps (RSI sweeet-spot 55, z-score -> 0.5±z/6, macro regime 0/1/2 -> linear), then `composite = Σ factor_weight*normalized` per agent, `global = Σ agent_weight*composite`, and `confidence = |composite-0.5|*2 * min(1, 0.5+consensus_ratio*0.5)`. This explicit two-level weighted fusion with a magnitude*consensus confidence is directly portable to Memeland's vote weighting.
  - **Alignment/min-factors gating**: directional buy/sell requires >=3 contributing factors (`alignment_gating`) and min composite+confidence thresholds, else forced HOLD - a concrete deterministic "don't trade on one voter" gate Memeland can mirror.
  - `tier1/models.py` (Pydantic): `VetoRule{metric_id, operator, threshold, veto_target: ALL/LONG_ONLY/SHORT_ONLY}`, `AlphaFactor{metric_id, operator, threshold, weight_pct, vote_direction}`, `StopLossCriteria{sl_atr_multiplier=2.0}` / `TakeProfitCriteria{target_rr_ratio=2.0}` / trailing activation - a declarative, validated blueprint schema for Memeland's voter/Veto config.
  - `risk_guard.py`: Risk Guard **veto** (not just scoring): kill-switch envs, MDD stop that only blocks *risk-increasing* proposals and clears when flat, gross leverage soft-clamp (penalize near 90% of max, hard ~95% above), and "insufficient snapshot -> mild caution 0.35 (never veto)" - good nuance Memeland's RiskManager can borrow (veto vs medium vs hold).
  - `tier1/validate.py` weight-sum parity checks (alpha factors sum 100, persona mix ~1.0, MA fast<slow) with optional strict mode - cheap blueprint drift guards.
  - Single-source risk config `config/fund_policy.py` (position cap, stop-loss pct, max leverage, MDD stop) mirroring SRC-042's risk constants pattern.
- **Security/complexity flags:**
  - `.env.example` commits a real shared `NEXUS_API_KEY=4Qbp6biPAKPS1gOksAySOlqK` (explicitly labeled "rate-limited shared demo key") - a committed credential to note, not copy; personal use but still flag hygiene. No hardcoded exchange private keys in src scan.
  - AGPL (ignored per task); enormous footprint: LangGraph/LangChain + FastAPI + Postgres/SQLAlchemy + Alembic + Celery-adjacent workers + pandas/TA-Lib/statsmodels + Futu/Hyperliquid/ccxt/Binance adapters + a full Next.js admin UI + proprietary Nexus data service (OLAXBT_NEXUS_DATA_*). Runtime weight dwarfs a personal bot.
  - Opinion/weakness: data depends on their **Nexus bundle** (sentiment/KOL heatmap/telemetry/deep orderflow are only proxies, agents `skipped` without it); whale/retail agents read many key-name permutations defensively - signals are string/DOM-key fragile. Final LLM arbitrator overlay can add noise on top of the deterministic fusion (they keep LLM signals as deltas over deterministic contracts).
- **For Memeland / edge check:**
  - Architecture is the closest match to Memeland's multi-voter + veto design, and the *deterministic* layer (canonical Tier-0 contract, factor normalization, two-level weighted fusion, confidence=magnitude*consensus, min-factors alignment gate, veto vs caution risk guard, declarative VetoRule/AlphaFactor blueprints) is exactly the green-label shape Memeland wants - and all of it is chain-agnostic / GMGN-independent logic on raw price/vol/flow inputs.
  - Not green as a system: AGPL, heavy LangGraph/FastAPI/Postgres/Next stack, proprietary Nexus data coupling, primarily CEX/Hyperliquid/Futu venues (no Robinhood Chain or Solana memecoin execution, no rug/honeypot/liq-native filters beyond flow proxies).
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** (port Memeland's voter layer to: per-voter canonical JSON contract with status, factor-normalization to [0,1], two-level weighted fusion with confidence = magnitude*consensus_ratio, min-factors alignment gate, and a veto-vs-caution risk guard with MDD-stop-blocks-risk-increase + gross-leverage clamp; adopt the Pydantic VetoRule/AlphaFactor/ATR-stop/RR-TP blueprints as TS schemas) / **Skip** (the platform, Nexus data coupling, LangGraph/FastAPI/web stack).

Product of Open Byte Inc.

Enormous footprint (FastAPI+Celery+Postgres+Redis+MCP+web), CEX/broker-only, no on-chain memecoin/flow/rug awareness, no Robinhood Chain/Solana.

Docs/API/MCP structure is organizational, not needed. Platform over-fitted to CEX futures+brokers + Postgres/Redis infra.

### SRC-182 paperswithbacktest/pwb-alphaevolve (Python, ~38 files, compact evolution/backtest framework)
- **What it is:** "AlphaEvolve" - an autonomous evolutionary search that applies DeepMind AlphaEvolve-style LLM mutation to algorithmic-trading strategies. A seed strategy (Backtrader `Strategy` subclass) is marked with `# === EVOLVE-BLOCK: <name>` regions; an LLM (OpenAI o3) returns a JSON `{blocks:{name: code}}` diff that a regex patcher surgically replaces; each child is backtested to KPIs (total_return, CAGR, Sharpe, max_drawdown, Calmar), stored in SQLite as a **program** with `parent_id` lineage + island assignment, pruned to a population while keeping an elite archive, and best ("hall-of-fame") selected as future parents. Optional MAP-Elites niches + prompt-level GA. Data via Papers-With-Backtest (equities/ETFs) with feather cache. MIT.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - `store/sqlite.py` ProgramStore: population cap + **elite-archive keep + random prune**, island (`num_islands`) diversity, `parent_id` lineage on every program - the persistence/selection core Memeland would want for any strategy-parameter or voter-weight search: keeps an audited family tree and preserves diversity to fight overfit.
  - `controller.py::_select_parent`: staged selection - elite-selection ratio (10%) picks from archive, exploitation ratio (70%) picks global best, exploration ratio (20%) samples a random island - a clean exploration/exploitation balance Memeland can reuse for config/voter-weight search.
  - `patching.py::apply_patch` EVOLVE-BLOCK regex: bounded, indentation-preserving mutation of delimited code regions with a full-`code` fallback; "LLM edits only marked regions" is a safety pattern if Memeland ever auto-generates strategy snippets.
  - `evaluator/metrics.py` pure numpy KPIs (CAGR/Sharpe/MDD/Calmar, std/NaN-safe) + `evaluator/backtest.py` "evaluate code -> JSON KPI dict" loop with serial deterministic Backtrader run.
  - Config-driven evolution via Pydantic `Settings` + YAML defaults; SQLite `:memory:` for prompt-GA eval.
- **Security/complexity flags:**
  - `.env.example` keys blank; no hardcoded secrets in code scan. `_load_module_from_code` executes arbitrary LLM-produced Python via `exec_module` (temp file + import) - a real **remote-code-execution-by-LLM** risk vector; acceptable for a local research tool, but a hard "do not copy the code-exec" pattern for Memeland.
  - `~/.alphaevolve/*.db` + `~/.alpha_trader_cache` global writes; Backtrader (mostly unmaintained) + pwb-toolbox/pwb-backtrader (Papers-With-Backtest closed data ecosystem) + OpenAI o3 dependence; equities/ETF data only, no crypto/chain.
  - Whole premise is **generating Backtrader strategy code** - far outside Memeland's voter/risk design and very LLM-token-heavy (each spawn = an o3 call).
- **For Memeland / edge check:**
  - Only the *selection/persistence* concepts transfer: MAP-Elites/island diversity, elite-archive-keep + lineage, staged exploration/exploitation selection - useful if Memeland wants to search voter weights or config presets robustly across regimes (complements SRC-149's anti-overfit battery and pybroker's walk-forward). These are small deterministic TS ports, chain-agnostic, GMGN-independent.
  - The LLM-code-mutation + Backtrader + equities stack is not green for a chain-native memecoin bot and carries an LLM-exec risk pattern to avoid.
- **Verdict:** **Study** (the island/elite-archive/lineage selection + staged exploration-exploitation search loop as a TS pattern for robust voter-weight/preset search) / **Skip** (the LLM strategy-code evolution engine - Backtrader, OpenAI o3, equities data, arbitrary-code-exec by LLM).

### SRC-077 danilobatson/ai-trading-agent-gemini (TypeScript/Next.js, ~25 ts files, light demo)
- **What it is:** A Next.js 15 + Inngest + Supabase demo web app: pulls LunarCrush social metrics (altRank, galaxyScore, mentions, interactions, creators, sentiment) for top-100-by-AltRank coins, feeds them to Google Gemini (`gemini-2.5-flash-lite`) which returns BUY/SELL/HOLD + confidence + reasoning, runs a 7-step Inngest background workflow with live progress tracking, and saves signals to Supabase with optional Discord alerts. Educational "social buzz -> trading signal" reference; no execution, no risk, no portfolio.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - `lib/lunarcrush.ts`: a clean typed LunarCrush API client (top-coins list by altRank + per-symbol topic data) with `AbortSignal.timeout(10s)`, explicit 401/429/5xx error mapping, and zeros-on-failure fallback. The **LunarCrush SocialMetrics schema** (`mentions/interactions/creators/altRank/galaxyScore/sentiment`) is a real, chain-agnostic social-hype data source Memeland could wire into its retail-hype voter (mempool/flow-agnostic, complements on-chain data).
  - `lib/gemini.ts::createFallbackSignal`: a deterministic **rule-based fallback** scoring 4 binary indicators (engagement ratio>100, altRank<100, galaxyScore>70, creators>1000) -> BUY if >=3, SELL if <=1, confidence 60-100 - a concrete "LLM proposes, thresholds fall back" shape Memeland already has but this is a nice compact consumer-grade illustration.
  - Regex-parse of `SIGNAL:/CONFIDENCE:/REASONING:` text (rather than JSON schema - weaker than Memeland's structured output) with **default-HOLD on parse failure/timeout**; signal confidence clamped to [0,100].
  - Inngest 7-step workflow w/ per-step progress writing to DB = a lightweight durable background-job pattern (memes, but shows step/blob orchestration Memeland could map to piggyback-free runners on EventSource-processor infra).
- **Security/complexity flags:**
  - No hardcoded secrets; all 5 keys via env (`LUNARCRUSH_API_KEY`, `GOOGLE_GEMINI_API_KEY`, Supabase, Inngest). `.env.example` uses placeholder values, fine.
  - Next.js/React + Inngest + Supabase SaaS stack - web-oriented, not a trading engine; no account/execution/risk/portfolio. LunarCrush is a **paid subscription API** and only covers established coins (top-100 altRank), not fresh memecoins.
  - No price action in the signal (metrics only + LLM judgment); signals are honestly labeled research, not audited for edge.
- **For Memeland / edge check:**
  - The transferable bit is the **LunarCrush social-metrics schema + typed client** as an optional external input for the retail-hype voter, and the deterministic 4-indicator social fallback function (TS-portable, chain-agnostic). Not GMGN-dependent; but it is a paid, top-100-coin API - low coverage of new memecoins where Memeland's edge lives, and Memeland already has better fallback-rule practices.
  - Not green as a system: pure signal-demo, no execution/risk/backtest, SaaS-heavy, social-only (no price/flow/rug checks).
- **Verdict:** **Study** (LunarCrush social-metrics client/schema + the deterministic positive-indicator social BUY/SELL fallback as a possible secondary input to Memeland's retail-hype voter) / **Skip** (the platform - demo web app, no execution/risk, paid top-100 API).

### SRC-099 ginlix-ai/LangAlpha (Python 3.13 + FastAPI + LangChain/LangGraph, very large monorepo ~hundreds of files + Next.js web + Electron desktop)
- **What it is:** A "vibe investing agent harness" - a commercial-grade LangChain/LangGraph research assistant platform for **equities/indices/FX** (FMP, SEC EDGAR, Polygon.io via ginlix-data, yfinance). Not a trading bot: it runs persistent per-goal "workspaces", interviews the user, dispatches parallel subagents, and produces research reports/long-short pair ideas. Ships its own `market_protocol` OHLCV schema, PTC (Programmatic Tool Calling), skills, MCP integration, automations, sandboxed code execution (Daytona/Docker), credential leak detection, Postgres/Redis infra, Next.js/Electron clients. Apache-2.0.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - `market_protocol/models.py` (Pydantic v2): a notably rigorous **OHLCV wire schema** - `OhlcvBar{ts_event, open/high/low/close, volume(nullable), vwap, trades, is_final}`, `SeriesHeader{price_treatment, tier(realtime/delayed_15m/eod), feed_scope, coverage, revision, asof, watermark}`, `Coverage{gaps, is_complete}`. The coverage/gaps/watermark bookkeeping ("a range fetched through a provider outage must not freeze incomplete") is a genuinely reusable data-integrity idea for Memeland's per-chain candle/trade replay and would catch stale/faulty candles on RH/Solana/BSC.
  - `PTC (Programmatic Tool Calling)`: the agent **writes and executes Python to process data** rather than streaming raw frames into the LLM context; tool docs dumped to workspace, activated on demand. A token-efficiency pattern Memeland could apply if it ever feeds LLM voters, though Memeland's voters are mostly deterministic.
  - Multi-provider LLM abstraction with failover (`llms/`); sandboxed execution and credential-leak detection/redaction (good hygiene memes, not code Memeland needs).
  - Market-phase/calendar enums + `tier`/`feed_scope` vocabulary - clean domain modeling for bar ingestion.
- **Security/complexity flags:**
  - No hardcoded secrets found (API keys env/secret-store/PGCrypto-at-rest driven; dedicated leak-detection middleware). Enormous footprint: LangGraph<2 + deepagents + Playwright + scrapling + Postgres/Redis/Alembic + cloud sandboxes (Daytona) + AWS S3 + Next.js + Electron - far beyond a personal bot.
  - Equities/indices/FX research focus: no on-chain memecoin/flow/rug logic, no RH/Solana/BSC execution, no risk/position management for a live portfolio; `AssetClass.CRYPTO` enum exists but feeds are equity-style.
  - Requires Python 3.13; 100s of pinned deps with deliberate version-ceiling reasoning (complexity and maintenance burden).
- **For Memeland / edge check:**
  - The transferable bit is the **market-protocol OHLCV/coverage schema** (declared lineage, gaps/watermark, price-treatment) as a TS data-integrity layer for Memeland's replay/candle store - and, conceptually, PTC's "process data in code, don't dump into context" for any LLM voter. Both small, chain-agnostic ports.
  - Not green as a system: it's an equities research assistant, not a chain-native trading bot; adopting it drags in a huge LangGraph/FastAPI/Postgres/Redis/Electron stack for zero memecoin execution edge.
- **Verdict:** **Study** (the OHLCV `Coverage`/`gaps`/`revision`/`watermark` protocol model for Memeland's candle/replay data-integrity; PTC's process-data-in-code pattern for LLM voter context) / **Skip** (the platform - equities research harness, huge stack, no on-chain/execution edge).

### SRC-209 TheGigaQuant/frostybot-js (JavaScript/Node, ~80 js files + jqwidgets web GUI) — fork of CryptoMF/frostybot-js, default branch is "stable"
- **What it is:** A self-hosted CEX trade-execution endpoint (REST + Tradingview webhook + Linux CLI). Receives commands (long/short/buy/sell/close/cancel) and converts them into ccxt orders on Bitmex/FTX/Deribit/Binance(spot/margin/USDT/CoinM)/BinanceUS. No strategy engine - it is the "hands" that turn a strategy's signals into position-adjusted exchange orders, with a queue per stub+symbol, optional bundled stoploss/takeprofit legs, and a web GUI for key/config management. Secrets stored AES-256-CTR encrypted keyed to a per-install UUID.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - `core/mod.trade.js` position-target sizing is the standout: for long/short it resolves an *absolute target position* from base/quote/usd sizes (`target = +/- requested`), computes `order_size = target - current`, clamps target to a user `maxsize`, allows relative size (`+X`) only when `maxsize` is set (fail-closed requirement, warns otherwise), flags flip-prone orders, and forces `target=0 + preserve sizing denominator` on `close all`. Exactly the "build an order to reach a target position within a hard cap" builder Memeland needs for RH/Solana buys - and unlike LLM-size-then-sign, the cap is enforced here by arithmetic, not prompt.
  - `get_amount()` unit/size conversion with market-precision handling: `floor_amount()` for spot (drives toward a size the exchange accepts, no dust over-budget) vs `round_amount()` for futures, plus `round_price()` to tick precision; `usd`->quote/base conversion including a fallback pair-price path when the quote isn't a stablecoin. Portable fee/dust-avoidance math for Memeland's order builder.
  - Relative/limit pricing: `+`/`-`/`%` price offsets resolved against live bid/ask (`get_relative_price`), and layered (comma) pricing - a clean way to express "place limit X bps within the spread".
  - Bundled exits: a long/short order auto-sizes stoploss/takeprofit legs to the entry size by default (`params.stop{quote|base} = totalsize`) - one-call entry+SL+TP bundle, a convenience Memeland's execution layer could mirror for "buy then immediately set SL/TP."
  - `core/mod.queue.js` serializes orders per stub+symbol; entry->stop->tp legs submitted together to avoid inter-order races on the same pair.
  - Secrets: AES-256-CTR encrypt/decrypt of stored API keys (md5/UUID-derived key), `is_encrypted` round-trip guard, plus `mod.settings` for persisted config.
- **Security/complexity flags:**
  - README/docker defaults: SSH password `__frostybot123__`, default HTTP port 80 - unsafe defaults (README tells you to change them, but they ship set).
  - The AES key is derived from a UUID that lives in the *same* settings store as the ciphertext (co-located key+ciphertext - weak at-rest protection, standard for self-host tools but not a pattern to copy).
  - Old callback/Promise-mixed JS, `md5()` usage, ccxt pinned old (^1.41), webhook endpoint meant to run on a public VPS - auth surface. No hardcoded keys in source scan (only a donation BTC/ETH/LTC address in README).
  - CEX-only (no Solana/BSC/Robinhood Chain, no memecoin/flow/rug awareness); `conflict:` logic is futures/perp-first, spot is a constrained subset; no strategy/risk/learning layers.
- **For Memeland / edge check:**
  - The transferable value is the **order-builder math**: target-position sizing with a hard arithmetic cap, `floor`-to-precision dust avoidance, price-to-tick truncation, and relative limit pricing - all small deterministic TS functions directly reusable for Chain 4663 / Solana token buys regardless of chain. Not GMGN-dependent. This directly improves Memeland's "execution price / fill reliability" edge (constraint #4).
  - Not green as a system: it is a deprecated-style CEX webhook controller with no on-chain execution, no chain-native edge, stale deps and an old JS style; the GUI/ccxt/webhook parts are dead weight for Memeland.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** (port the position-target + maxsize-cap order sizing and precision/floor dust-avoidance + relative-limit-price helpers into Memeland's execution/order-builder as TS utilities) / **Skip** (the webhook/ccxt/GUI platform, secrets-at-rest scheme, old JS style). Note: only one frostybot-js source assigned; README/wiki actually point to the original CryptoMF/frostybot-js (this is its fork).

### SRC-236 zostaff/ai-quant-researcher (Python, ~60 files / ~5000 LOC, MIT, 111 tests)
- **What it is:** A compact "AI research engine where Claude generates strategies and the system statistically kills the bad ones." 6 modules + CLI: Agent loop (hypothesis/code/critic/risk/memory agents), a vectorized backtester (bar engine, event-driven, portfolio/costs), a feature pipeline (cross-sectional features, labels, leakage detector), a hard-gate validation layer (deflated Sharpe, purged/combinatorial CV, walk-forward, meta-labeling, factor attribution/PCA concentration), production ops (kill switch, paper trading, TCA, monitoring), a flow orchestrator with gates + sandbox. Deliberately only 5 deps (numpy/pandas/scipy/anthropic/pydantic) — and the validation/backtest parts run with no API key on synthetic data. Positioning: "generation is cheap, validation is expensive; put the expensive part where it belongs."
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns (best anti-overfit / attribution source so far):**
  - `validation/deflated_sharpe.py` (Bailey & López de Prado DSR): discounts observed Sharpe by the *honest trial count* N via the closed-form expected-max approximation `E[max]=sqrt(variance)*((1-γ)Φ⁻¹(1-1/N)+γΦ⁻¹(1-1/(N·e)))`, plus PSR/probabilistic Sharpe, empirical trial-variance estimator, `std<1e-12` tolerance guard. A "no override" hard gate — directly the "is the edge real / no double count" test Memeland's swarm-learning and voter-weight claims need, and it's pure math that ports cleanly to TS given a trial count.
  - `validation/purged_cv.py` + `walk_forward.py`: purged k-fold and **combinatorial purged CV** with `purge` (drop label-overlapping bars) and `embargo` (drop bars after the test block) — the correct way to validate time-series signals without look-ahead, incl. estimating out-of-sample Sharpe *variance*.
  - `features/leakage_detector.py`: **structural leakage test** (recompute the feature on bars truncated to each probe point t; if value[t] changes when bars after t are removed the feature peeks — "zero false positives"), plus a **correlation-based fallback** (corr with future-shifted vs past-shifted target) for value-only feature matrices. Perfect guard for Memeland's per-chain features so a "flow" or "momentum" voter doesn't accidentally consume future bars.
  - `orchestrator/gates.py`: gates composed as an AND (`critic -> deflated_sharpe -> max pairwise corr -> PCA concentration`), evaluated cheapest-first for efficiency, `rejection_reason` names the first failing gate, and a PCA-concentration gate catches "stealthy" redundancy pairwise corr misses. A clean templatable gate pipeline for Memeland's consensus/voter validation.
  - `production/kill_switch.py`: **latched** kill switch — configurable (`drawdown>X`, `daily_loss>Y`, `Sharpe<Z` over rolling window), once tripped stays tripped, `reset()` is manual and appends an audit record; predicate errors are logged loudly, never swallowed. Exactly the fail-closed shape Memeland's MarketSentinel/kill-switch should mirror.
  - `validation/meta_labeling.py`: **act/skip meta-classifier** on top of a primary side — target = "did the primary make money", a scipy-only logistic classifier with L2 + interpretable coefficients (no sklearn/torch). A deterministic "trade only when the consensus is *reliable*" filter Memeland can sit on top of its 7-voter consensus (complements the critic voter).
  - `backtest/costs.py` + `production/tca.py`: sober round-turn cost bps (spread+commission+slippage, says *crypto override upward*) + Almgren-Chriss `sqrt(participation)` market impact, and Perold TCA (`arrival_shortfall`, `implementation_shortfall`, impact bps) that **calibrates a `cost_bps` from real fills to feed back into the backtester** — closing the live<->backtest cost loop (drives Memeland's constraint #4/#6: don't validate a backtest with fake fees).
  - `agents/memory.py` + `backtest/portfolio.py`: SQLite narrow-schema trial log = single source of truth for `n_trials()` (feeds DSR) + full auditability, storing returns_json only for accepted trials (space discipline); position/sizing accounting is clean and tested.
  - `RiskAgent` deterministic core: returns-by-volatility-regime breakdown (low/mid/high buckets with mean Sharpe per bucket) even though the final size call is LLM — the regime-attribution helper is a portable TS idea.
- **Security/complexity flags:**
  - No hardcoded secrets found in scan; `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` env-driven and *optional* (validation/backtest run without it). `.env.example` placeholders only. Good hygiene.
  - `orchestrator/sandbox.py` says plainly "This is NOT a security sandbox" — LLM-generated strategy code is executed via a constrained `exec` allowlist (numpy/pandas/math only) + wall-clock timeout. Any pattern that executes model-generated code is an inherent code-exec risk; the whitelist+timeout is a *containment* measure, not a boundary. **Do not copy code-exec-LLM for Memeland.**
  - Community-awareness caveat: README compares itself to TokenQuant-style peers; repo is new/synthetic-data-tested (equities/crypto *bar* data, no on-chain memecoin/flow/rug data, no RH/Solana/BSC execution, not GMGN). Examples 1-5 rely on synthetic data; real edge needs real fills/costs.
- **For Memeland / edge check:**
  - This is the strongest source so far for Memeland's **"validate the edge is real before learning from it"** need (constraints #3/#6) and a great complement to SRC-085 (BCa CI) / SRC-182 (lineage selection) — it supplies the *statistical hard gates* those two frame, all chain-agnostic and GMGN-independent: DSR with honest n_trials, purged/combinatorial CV with embargo, structural+causal leakage detection, AND-composed gates, latched kill-switch, costs+TCA calibration, meta-labeling act/skip. These are small, deterministic, dependency-light TS ports targetable at Memeland's backtest/replay evaluator and swarm-learning layer.
  - Not green as a system: the Claude strategy-synthesis loop, `exec`-based code runtime, and Python/pandas/scipy stack are heavy and off-shape for a chain-native bot; adopt the validation primitives, not the generator.
- **Verdict:** **Adopt** (as TS ports for Memeland's learn/validate layer: deflated Sharpe w/ honest trial count, purged CV + embargo, structural & correlation leakage detection, latched kill-switch, round-turn cost + sqrt-impact, arrival/implementation-shortfall TCA, and consensus meta-labeling act/skip) / **Study** (the gate-composition pipeline and per-regime risk attribution) / **Skip** (the LLM code-generation loop + `exec` runtime + anthropic/backtrader-free bar VM as a system).

### SRC-153 mocasus/trade-agent (Python 3.11, ~30 py files / ~3700 LOC, MIT, 39 tests 100% coverage)
- **What it is:** A compact, deliberately framework-free modular "AI trading agent" built around a 6-slot plugin system (data_source / strategy / exchange / notifier / sentiment / risk_profile, plus indicators). Core loop: data -> indicators -> sentiment -> strategy -> risk -> execute -> notify -> store, running on config (data_source and strategy slots load via a discovery-based PluginLoader). Ships LLM, rule and partial-exit strategies; ccxt/paper/Bybit-futures exchanges; RSS+LLM sentiment; Telegram/Discord/webhook notifiers; conservative/moderate/aggressive/Kelly/trailing-stop risk profiles; a JSON-storage audit trail; and a simple backtester using the paper exchange. Safety by default: paper mode ON, first 24h forced paper, daily-loss limit, max-failure auto-stop, kill switch, everything decision-logged.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - `risk.py` + `interfaces.py::RiskProfileInterface`: a single abstract contract (`calculate_position_size / calculate_stop_loss / calculate_take_profit / check_risk_rules`) with clean conservative/moderate/aggressive tiers. The sizing formula is the reusable nugget: `pct = min_pct + (max_pct - min_pct) * (confidence/100)`, then `size = tradable * pct / 100`, with `tradable = available_capital * (1 - reserve_pct/100)` (a configurable cash reserve) and the trade gated by `decision.confidence < confidence_floor` → reject, `daily_pnl <= -max_loss_pct` → reject, and max-open-position count. A concise, tested template for Memeland's "confidence-scale position size between min and max, respect a cash reserve, reject on daily loss" logic.
  - `risk_profile/kelly_sizing.py` (fractional Kelly): half-Kelly by default (`fraction=0.5`), a `min_samples` floor below which it uses `default_win_rate`/`default_win_loss_ratio`, `kelly = max(0, (b*p - (1-p))/b)`, hard 25% allocation cap, and a `record_trade(pnl)` accumulating empirical wins/losses from *realized* trades. A small, portable "learn an edge from win-rate/win-loss-ratio out of live history then size by Kelly" primitive for Memeland's learning layer (complement to SRC-236's sturdier statistics).
  - `risk_profile/trailing_stop.py` (activation-threshold trailing): tracks per-symbol `_highest_price` (only ratchets up), stays inactive until `profit_pct >= activation_threshold` (e.g. +1%), then stops out when price <= high*(1-trail%/100), emitting a SELL Decision with confidence*0.9. The "don't trail until in profit" activation guard is a clean trailing-exit primitive (matches SRC-065's fee-aware trailing idea, minus the breakeven math - pair the two).
  - `strategy/rule_builder.py`: a **declarative condition registry** (`CONDITIONS = {"price_above": lambda..., "rsi_above": lambda..., "volume_above": ...}`) where named rules carry `{condition, threshold, action, confidence_weight}` and each fired rule adds its weight to a confidence capped at 100. A tiny, readable DSL for Memeland to express deterministic voter/entry rules in config rather than code.
  - `strategy/partial_exit.py`: scale-out ladder (default 25% at +5%, 25% at +10%, 50% at +15%), idempotent via a per-symbol `_triggered` set so each level fires once - useful staged-exit for memecoin winners.
  - `plugin_loader.py`: interface-seam discovery - each plugin module exports `register() -> {name, class}`, the loader scans builtin then user dirs (`~/.trade-agent/plugins`), caches per-slot, type-checks against ABCs and raises a clear error listing available names. A clean concrete pattern for *static* plugin-style registration if Memeland ever wants drop-in voters/exchanges (note: exploits dynamic importlib execution - Memeland should port only the registration interface, not the dynamic import).
  - Agent-loop safety defaults worth mirroring: `trading.paper_mode=true` default, `auto_stop_on_error=true` stopping after `max_failures`, interleaved kill-switch checks, daily-loss + max-position gating before execution, and decision/trade logging to storage (audit trail).
- **Security/complexity flags:**
  - No hardcoded keys found in scan; all creds (OpenAI/ccxt/Telegram) are env-driven. `.gitignore`-gated local `.env`. Good hygiene for a small repo.
  - CEX/equities only (ccxt Binance + Bybit-futures, RSS news sentiment), no on-chain memecoin/flow/rug awareness, no Robinhood Chain/Solana/BSC, not GMGN. Single-strategy LLM slot (openai SDK) is off-shape vs Memeland's deterministic 7-voter consensus; `rule_strategy`/`rule_builder` are the closer fits.
  - Small footprint and low maintenance burden (3.7k LOC, 39 tests, 100% coverage, MIT); Python is the only clear downside for a Node/TS codebase - the value is conceptual/portable, not the runtime.
  - `paper_exchange` is a simple simulated fill/exchange for backtest - thin, not a realistic fill/slippage/liquidity model (Memeland's replay will want more, cf. OctoBot's market-filter simulator / SRC-083).
- **For Memeland / edge check:**
  - Does not add chain-native/memecoin edge on its own, but its **risk-profile interface, confidence-scaled sizing with reserve + floor/cap, fractional-Kelly-with-empirical-records, activation-threshold trailing stop, and rule-builder condition DSL** are small, deterministic, chain-agnostic and GMGN-independent pieces that map almost 1:1 onto Memeland's RiskManager/RiskEngineV2 and its voter/exit design - plus the "paper-first, auto-stop, kill-switch, audit-log" loop mirrors Memeland's dry-run/fail-closed mandate.
  - Not green as a system (Python, ccxt-CEX-only, LLM-strategy slot); adopt the primitives as TS ports, not the platform.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** (port: RiskProfileInterface shape + confidence-scaled min/max sizing with reserve + daily-loss/max-position gates, fractional-Kelly with realized-trade parameters, activation-threshold trailing stop, rule-builder condition DSL, and paper-first/auto-stop/kill-switch/audit loop defaults) / **Study** (partial-exit ladder, plugin-registration interface) / **Skip** (the platform runtime, LLM strategy slot, ccxt/Bybit-futures execution, thin paper-exchange backtester).

### SRC-119 jito-labs/jito-ts (TypeScript SDK, ~30 ts files + generated protos, Apache-2.0, Node 20)
- **What it is:** The official Jito (Solana MEV) TypeScript SDK for talking to the block-engine, relayer and Geyser. Three clients: `SearcherClient` (gRPC) to submit bundles of Solana transactions that validators execute **atomically/all-or-nothing** and to fetch tip accounts / connected leaders / next scheduled leader; `GeyserClient` (gRPC) to stream real-time account/block/tx updates for accounts of interest; and an auth layer (server-issued JWT via signed challenge) plus a small `rpc` connection helper. Generated protos committed for convenience.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - `sdk/block-engine/types.ts::Bundle` + `addTipTx()`: compose several `VersionedTransaction`s into one bundle with a `transactionLimit`, then append a **priority-fee tip transaction** to a designated tip account (`SystemProgram.transfer` to `tipAccount`, tip in lamports). For Solana memecoin swaps, a Jito bundle lets Memeland land an atomic buy (or buy+approve+swap) in one block - directly improving execution/fill reliability and reducing front-running on the Solana leg (constraint #4).
  - `sdk/block-engine/searcher.ts::SearcherClient`: a disciplined gRPC client - `retryWithBackoff` (maxRetries 3, baseDelay 1s, exp factor 2, cap 10s) that only retries `UNAVAILABLE`/`RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`/`DEADLINE_EXCEEDED` and *not* DNS/SSL/INVALID_ARGUMENT; a typed `Result<T,E>` (`Ok`/`Err`) API everywhere instead of exceptions - a clean fallible-service pattern Memeland can reuse for its RPC/bundle gateways. `onBundleResult`/`bundleResults` subscription filters out *normal* stream closures so a reconnect isn't misreported as an error.
  - `sdk/block-engine/auth.ts::AuthProvider`: challenge-response auth (sign `pubkey-challenge` with ed25519) then JWT access+refresh token flow with expiry checks, a shared single in-flight `refreshing` promise, and explicit `rate_limited`/`auth_failed`/`network_error`/`invalid_response` reasons. A good template for authenticated gRPC/RPC service clients (applies to any token-auth gateway Memeland adds).
  - Leader-timing pattern (`examples/simple_bundle/utils.ts`): wait until the next Jito leader is `<=2` slots out, then fetch tip accounts and latest blockhash, build bundle + tip, submit, then **poll bundle results to a final state (rejected/finalized/processed/dropped) with a timeout** - a concrete "submit then confirm final fill state" loop for Memeland's Solana order lifecycle.
  - Geyser: subscribe to `accounts_of_interest` updates - an **independent (non-GMGN) real-time on-chain data stream** (account/tx) usable for Memeland's whale/flow/rug detection on Solana (constraint #7 provider independence).
- **Security/complexity flags:**
  - No hardcoded secrets found (all from `dotenv` / `AUTH_KEYPAIR_PATH` file / `RPC_URL`). Good hygiene for an SDK.
  - Operational weight: requires a Jito **auth keypair** (an extra Solana signing key to manage = one more secret to protect) plus block-engine URL(s) and a gRPC stack (`@grpc/grpc-js`, generated protos). Bundle tips cost lamports (fee on top of priority fees); leader-timing waits add latency; the SDK pins `@solana/web3.js ~1.77` (older major) which may conflict with newer Solana tooling.
  - Solana-specific only - does nothing for Memeland's primary Robinhood Chain (Arbitrum Nitro) venue or BSC/Base legs; it is execution/transport infrastructure, not a signal/edge source. Examples leak logs by design (print blockhash/signature) - not a security flaw, just noisy.
  - Complexity is moderate (gRPC + protos + token auth), but as a dependency it's narrow and official (Jito team, Apache-2.0), not a heavy platform.
- **For Memeland / edge check:**
  - High direct value **if/when Memeland executes swaps on Solana**: atomic all-or-nothing Jito bundles + tip accounts materially improve fill reliability and reduce front-running (constraint #4), and Geyser is a GMGN-independent real-time data feed (constraint #7). The `Result<T,E>` + retry-with-backoff gRPC client and the auth token-refresh lifecycle are portable patterns for any gateway Memeland builds.
  - Not green as a now-core dependency: Memeland's primary venue is RH Chain (not Solana), it adds an auth keypair + gRPC operational weight, tip cost, and it pins an older `@solana/web3.js`. Adopt as a **Solana-leg execution adapter** only when that leg goes live, not as a global dependency.
- **Verdict:** **Study** (SDK for the future Solana execution leg: Jito bundle + tip-account + confirm-to-final-state loop, Geyser as a non-GMGN data stream; borrow the `Result<T,E>` + retry-with-backoff + token-refresh patterns for Memeland's gateway layer) / **Skip** (as a current core dependency - RH-chain primary venue, gRPC/auth-keypair weight, older web3.js pin). No near-duplicates noted.

### SRC-124 krakenfx/kraken-cli (Rust, ~370 files)
- **What it is:** Official Kraken AI-native CLI (v0.4.1, actively maintained, MIT). Single binary covering spot, xStocks/forex, perp futures, earn; exposes 174 commands to LLM agents via a built-in MCP server (tool catalog + error catalog JSON), plus an event-sourced paper-trading engine and a research "lab / session / replay" stack for backtesting and post-hoc P&L explainability. Pure CEX (Kraken), no DEX/decentralized execution — overlap with Memeland is architectural, not domain.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** (1) Event-sourced paper account: locked JSONL `events.jsonl` journal with versioned stamped records + durable-appends-then-playback projection, giving atomic multi-process read-decide-write; ideal paper-first/fail-closed model to mirror. (2) "Lab" scoring: scores a stopped session into an honest Scorecard — metrics, round-trip trade stats, P&L decomposition (`explain_pnl`), and a "divergence caveat" comparing anchor vs replayed curve, with pass/fail "verdict" tests — a clean convert of replay/anchor into an audit trail. (3) `kraken-recording` frame sinks (DuckDB + JSONL) for book/ohlc/ticker/trade capture. (4) MCP server pattern with a generated command registry and structured stdout=json / stderr=diagnostics contract for agent tooling. (5) Session timeline with origin (Cli/Mcp) tagging and manifest. (6) Monotonic HMAC nonce via atomic counter; futures+spot sign helpers. (7) Strength: strict CI security lint (deny unsafe_code/unwrap/panic, broken-doc links) plus `securesdlc.yml`. All directly reusable ideas.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Excellent — `secrecy` SecretString wrapping with zeroize on drop, config file 0600 permissions, flag>env>config precedence, private Debug redaction, deliberate `serialize_secret` opt-in. No hardcoded keys found. Complexity is HIGH (large workspace, 9 crates, heavy lints) but it is genuinely an enterprise-grade reference.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Green-label edge: the **event-sourced paper account + lab scorecard honesty envelope (anchor-vs-replay divergence + caveats)** is the single most transferable pattern for Memeland's paper-first, fail-closed, anti-overfit requirement — steal the design, re-implement in TS. The MCP-command-registry + JSONL-JSON-output convention is a great model for Memeland's own agent surface. Not portable as-is (Kraken REST/WS, heavy Rust) but the contracts port cleanly.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt/Study** — Adapt the event-sourced paper journal + replay-anchored honest scorecard and MCP-registry/research-LAB patterns in TS; Study the session/timeline and recording-frame design as reference architecture.

### SRC-173 okx/agent-trade-kit (TypeScript, ~337 files)
- **What it is:** Official OKX "Agent Trade Kit" (v1.4.7, MIT, actively maintained). AI-native trading toolkit as a pnpm monorepo: `@agent-tradekit/core` (REST client + 167 tools across 11 modules), `okx-trade-mcp` (MCP stdio server for Claude/Cursor), `okx-trade-cli` (terminal CLI). Runs 100% local, no cloud, with demo-mode default, read-only flag, per-module filtering, and built-in rate limiter. Pure CEX (OKX) — domain is centralized exchange, but the safety/agent-interface architecture is a direct blueprint for Memeland's own MCP surface.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** (1) **Agent-safety controls** most transferable: `--read-only` gate that exposes only read/query tools, `--demo` mode default, per-module filter (`MODULE_FILTERED` reason code). (2) **Capability snapshot** tool (`system_get_capabilities`) returning readOnly/hasAuth/demo/moduleAvailability for agent planning — put the safety state in-band so the agent sees it before acting. (3) **Remediation-warning guard**: regex `WRITE_ACTION_PATTERN` on cancel/close/stop/withdraw suggestions + appended "do NOT auto-execute writes, diagnose read-only then confirm" — a concrete veto-on-remediation pattern. (4) [CAUTION] tags in every mutating tool description ("obtain ordId and confirm still open"). (5) Token-bucket client-side `RateLimiter` with max-wait fail-fast. (6) `safe-file` utilities: atomic write-then-rename, path-traversal + zip-entry validation — good for skills/downloads handling. (7) Skills registry with hash verification. (8) Structured JSON stdout / error envelope contract for CLI tooling.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Good. No hardcoded keys found; `.env.example`/`config.toml.example` use placeholders; secrets via `~/.okx/config.toml` + env + wizard. Demo profile default is a nice fail-closed touch. Complexity moderate (monorepo, tool-generation layer); codebase is large but focused. CEX-specific (OKX REST/signatures, instrument IDs) so the tools themselves aren't portable.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Green-label edge: the **AI-agent trading MCP safety stack — read-only/demo gates, capability snapshot, remediation-veto guard, per-module filter, token-bucket limiter** — maps almost 1:1 to Memeland's paper-first/fail-closed constraint and should be ported to TS as-is. Not portable for execution (OKX CEX vs Robinhood/Solana/BSC), and its market/bot tools are OKX-bound → Skip those; Adapt the safety + MCP contract layer.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** — port the MCP safety architecture (read-only/demo gates, capability snapshot, remediation-write veto, module filter, rate limiter, atomic safe-file) into Memeland; **Skip** the OKX-specific trading modules for chain portability.

### SRC-227 warp-id/solana-trading-bot (TypeScript, ~38 files)
- **What it is:** Raydium new-pool sniping/trading bot (v2.0.1) from the warp-id team (Jito-affiliated infra), runs `ts-node index.ts`. Watches live Solana DEX for new LiquidityPoolV4 pools, runs token-safety filters (LP burned / mint renounced / freezable / metadata mutable / pool size range / socials), auto-buys per snipe-list or all new pools, then auto-sells with take-profit/stop-loss + hard time limit. Direct @raydium-io/raydium-sdk + RPC — no GMGN dependency. Small, focused, single-purpose.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** (1) **`TransactionExecutor` interface** with pluggable concrete executors — Default (direct `sendAndConfirm`), Jito-RPC (random tip-account + tip transfer + bundle), Warp (`tx.warp.id` fee wallet + bundled serialize) — clean constructor-DI execution abstraction that Memeland can mirror for its own executor variants (incl. a paper stub). (2) **`PoolFilters` chain** — interface `Filter { execute(poolKeys) => {ok,message} }`, list of on-chain safety checks run via `Promise.all`, `every(ok)` gate; each filter fail-returns a reason (great for veto-with-reason). Includes Burn/Mutable/RenouncedFreeze/PoolSize. (3) **Cache layer** (Market/Pool/SnipeList) + in-memory `PRE_LOAD_EXISTING_MARKETS`. (4) Mutex `ONE_TOKEN_AT_A_TIME` serialization and buy/sell retry loops (MAX_BUY_RETRIES), unnecessary-second TX avoidance via consecutive filter matches. (5) TP/SL decision loop with `PRICE_CHECK_INTERVAL`/`DURATION` (fail-closed auto-sell on expiry). (6) Jito/compute-unit fee helpers, token metadata serializer.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Clean and simple, no hardcoded secrets found (`PRIVATE_KEY` from `.env.copy` placeholder; wallet Keypair from env). Some fragility: bare `catch (e: any)` with reliance on `e.code == -32602`, hardcoded Jito tip-account list (mainnet addresses, expected), public-RPC warnings (caching/market preload explicitly discouraged on public RPC), defaults like `BUY_SLIPPAGE=20` are risky but user-set. Overall very lightweight — few deps, no build framework beyond ts-node.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Green-label edge (Solana leg): close to ideal — **real DEX edge, simple, not heavy, not GMGN-dependent, portable**. The executor-interface DI + PoolFilters-with-reason veto + windowed TP/SL auto-sell + snipe-list whitelist + one-token mutex are all directly portable. Raydium-specific SDK coupling and its raw sniper posture (auto-buy delay 0) are not Memeland's style, so **Adapt** these pieces rather than adopt wholesale.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** — port the TransactionExecutor-interface DI, veto-with-reason pool filters, and fail-closed TP/SL+timeout loop; **Study** its Slim-jito/warp tip-bundling fee flow for execution cost.

### SRC-230 wwwwwworld/solana-trading-bot-v3 (TypeScript, ~40 files)
- **What it is:** The V3 (3.0.0, RC) continuation of the warp-id Solana trading bot (see SRC-227). The README explicitly states the previous @2.0.0 repo is no longer maintained and continues here — **near-duplicate / same lineage**: near-identical file tree (same cache/, filters/, listeners/, transactions/, helpers/, bot.ts) with the same Raydium new-pool sniper + token-safety filters + auto-sell TP/SL logic. Delta vs v2: adds `inquirer` (interactive setup/config prompts) and `validator` (env validation), and a `sellExecutionCount` counter guard to prevent overlapping sells. Audited here once; findings shared with SRC-227.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** Same as SRC-227 — TransactionExecutor interface with pluggable Default/Jito-RPC/Warp executors; PoolFilters veto-with-reason chain (burn/mutable/renounced-freeze/pool-size); one-token mutex + `sellExecutionCount`; windowed TP/SL (`PRICE_CHECK_INTERVAL`/`DURATION` fail-closed auto-sell); buy/sell retry loops; onProgramAccountChange Raydium AMM pool listener with memcmp filters (dataSize + quoteMint + marketProgramId + status byte). New v3-only reusable bits: interactive config wizard and a `consecutiveMatchCount` gate (require N consecutive filter matches before buying, reducing false-pool snipes). No GMGN dependency — direct Raydium SDK + RPC.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Clean; `.env.example` PRIVATE_KEY is an empty placeholder, no hardcoded secrets. Same fragility as v2: bare `catch (e: any)`, hardcoded Jito tip-account list, `BUY_SLIPPAGE=20` default, public-RPC caveats on caching. Slightly heavier than v2 only from added deps; still a tiny single-purpose script.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Green-label edge (Solana leg): same as SRC-227 — real DEX edge, simple, not GMGN-dependent, portable. The `consecutiveMatchCount` confirmation gate and `sellExecutionCount` overlapping-sell guard are small v3-specific improvements worth taking. Raydium coupling + raw sniper posture (delay 0, slippage 20) means **Adapt** the reusable pieces, not adopt the whole bot.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** — treat as the maintained successor of SRC-227; port the executor-interface DI, veto filters, consecutive-match gate, fail-closed TP/SL timeout loop, and one-token serialization to Memeland's Solana leg. **Note: near-duplicate of SRC-227 — do not double-count.**

## Batch 13 - chain-data-infra, MCP-data & meme-smart-money P0 (subagent Pasteur)

### SRC-063 blockscout/blockscout (Elixir, ~3480 files)
- **What it is:** Full open-source EVM block explorer and indexer monorepo (Elixir apps: `block_scout_web`, `explorer`, `indexer`, `ethereum_jsonrpc`). Ingests blocks/receipts/logs from RPC, decodes token transfers/NFTs, verifies contracts, serves a REST/GraphQL explorer API, with a generated TS API-types package (`types-package`). Supports hundreds of chains and L2s incl. Arbitrum Nitro.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** `EthereumJSONRPC.RequestCoordinator` + `RollingWindow`: per-method timeout counters with backoff `(wait_per_timeout + jitter) * recent_timeouts`, plus a separate all-request throttle window and in-flight timeout guard - a deterministic RPC health/backoff primitive. `EthereumJSONRPC.ERC20`: batched direct `eth_call`s for `name/symbol/decimals` via method-selector mapping. Token-transfer decoding from receipt logs is a reference for fill-tape parsing. Its `arbitrum.ex` variant confirms Arbitrum/Nitro indexer patterns exist, but not portable TS.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Massive monorepo (Postgres-backed DB, Docker, Kubernetes, GraphQL); not a component you copy. No hardcoded secrets seen; RPC/db config via env. Heavy maintenance surface; Elixir/OTP runtime is foreign to Memeland's Node stack.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** No green-label fit as a whole (would replace the bot with an explorer). Only portable ideas are the rolling-window timeout/backoff RPC coordinator and batched `eth_call` metadata fetch; Memeland already has RPC failover + API-key pools, so marginal edge is small.
- **Verdict:** **Skip** as a component; **Study** the `RequestCoordinator`/`RollingWindow` backoff + batched ERC-20 `eth_call` pattern if RPC failover ever needs adaptive per-method throttling.

### SRC-088 enviodev/hyperindex (Rust CLI + ReScript core, ~827 files)
- **What it is:** Envio/Hyperindex, a latency- and sync-speed-optimized blockchain indexer framework supporting EVM (incl. arbitrary rollups like RH chain 4663), Fuel, and Solana. Self-hosted or hosted; CLI in Rust (`packages/cli`), indexer engine in ReScript (`packages/envio`), Postgres persistence, optional Hasura GraphQL layer. Handles multi-chain cross-chain state, dynamic contract registration (factory/curve patterns like pump.fun), catch-up/backfill, and reorg rollback.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** `ChainFetching.res` block-range partition fetch of `eth_getLogs` with a `blockRangeFetchResponse`, `fromBlockQueried`/`latestFetchedBlockNumber` cursor, and per-chain state; `ChainState.registerReorgGuard` + `beginReorg`/rollback restoration of an events-processed counter when a reorg is detected (report-only or rollback mode); dynamic `contractRegister` handlers that watch a factory event and auto-register newly deployed contract addresses to index; `CrossChainState` for merging multi-chain views. These are the right primitives for a robust, portable on-chain fill/transfer tape.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Full Postgres-backed indexer + Rust build + ReScript toolchain; heavy to self-operate (memory, DB, migrations). `.env.example` only `ENVIO_API_TOKEN=your-api-token-here` placeholder - no hardcoded secrets. Framework-grade complexity, not a component you lift into a Node bot.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Running envio as an external indexer for RH chain is operationally expensive (Postgres + Hasura + the framework lifecycle) and overlaps Memeland's existing RPC adapters; not green as a deployment. The portable value is the **pattern**, not the tool: bounded block-range `eth_getLogs` batching with cursor, a reorg guard with rollback, and factory-based dynamic address registration. That maps onto Memeland's RH chain-flow adapter (goal 1/7, GMGN-independent) but Memeland already has batching/failover, so the marginal edge is confirmation-of-design, not a new capability.
- **Verdict:** **Skip** as a framework/dependency; **Study** the block-range batching + cursor + reorg-guard + dynamic contract-registration pattern to harden the RH chain-flow adapter if reorg handling is currently weak.

### SRC-148 messari/subgraphs (TypeScript/AssemblyScript GraphQL subgraphs, ~10693 files)
- **What it is:** Messari's industry-standard TheGraph subgraph library: 173 protocol subgraphs (DEX, lending, yield, bridges, perp futures, options, NFT) plus shared `_reference_` template, standardized `schema-*.graphql` definitions, a deployment registry, and Streamlit dashboard apps (`dashboard`, `apps/*-analytics`, `whale-watcher`).
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** The standardized DEX-AMM schema (`schema-dex-amm.graphql`): `Token` (with `lastPriceUSD`/`lastPriceBlockNumber` cache fields), pool/liquidity/volume entities, `Network` enum, `ProtocolType`. The shared price library (`subgraphs/_reference_/src/prices`): a multi-oracle abstraction (Chainlink, AaveOracle, CurveRouter/Calculations, Uniswap/Sushi fork routers) with a defined oracle-priority/fallback chain and per-chain constants, plus utility libraries for decimals, datetime, snapshots, getters. This is a clean "normalized schema + multi-oracle price fallback" reference.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Monorepo is enormous (10.7k files, 173 subgraphs) and coupled to TheGraph/AssemblyScript toolchain. `_reference_` package has no obvious hardcoded secrets; deployments/config are per-chain. Streamlit apps are Python dashboards - irrelevant to Memeland's Node stack. The `Network` enum has no RH chain 4663 entry - the library does not model the home chain.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** No green-label fit: TheGraph does not index Robinhood Chain 4663, so these subgraphs cannot provide RH coverage, and running a subgraph stack is heavy for a personal bot. Portable idea is the **schema + multi-oracle price abstraction**: Memeland could adopt a normalized pool/token analytics schema and a chainlink/curve/uniswap-fork fallback price resolver for BSC/Base/ETH feeds - but Memeland already aggregates GeckoTerminal/DexScreener/DeFiLlama prices, so the marginal edge is organizational (schema discipline), not signal.
- **Verdict:** **Skip** as a dependency/source (no RH coverage, heavy); **Study** the standardized DEX schema and multi-oracle price-fallback design as a reference if Memeland consolidates per-chain pool analytics.

### SRC-183 paradigmxyz/cryo (Rust, ~236 files)
- **What it is:** CLI/data tool to "freeze" EVM chain data into parquet/csv/json/python dataframes directly from any RPC (`--rpc`, or `ETH_RPC_URL` env; custom `--chain` for non-standard chains).
