### SRC-116 ironclad-protocol/solana-copy-trading-bot (TypeScript, ~80 files)
- **What it is:** A Solana copy-trading bot (author "EdmonkLabs") that mirrors buys/sells from multiple target wallets across Pump.fun / Pump AMM / Raydium AMM+CPMM, merged in one repo with a Polymarket up/down CLOB trading engine (`Trade`/`decision.ts` + `trade.toml`). The copy-trading half (Stream → Parse → Queue → Executor) is the relevant part.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - **Own-data stream, no GMGN:** Helius Geyser `transactionSubscribe` on copy-wallet accounts (`stream/helius-geyser.ts`), `commitment:"processed"` for latency; DEX program-id router (`parsers/router.ts`) → normalized `CopyTradeEvent` → single-threaded non-blocking queue (`queue/trade-queue.ts`) → executor. Stream never awaits execution.
  - **Copy sizing:** `COPY_PERCENT` (ratio of copied wallet's SOL trade, default 0.1=10%) or `COPY_FIXED_SOL` fixed-SOL buy override. Mirror-sell sizing mirrors the copied wallet's fraction: `ratio = event.tokenAmount / targetBalance`; sell `ratio * ourBalance`, sell all if `ratio >= 0.95`.
  - **Arbitrage / aggregator skip:** tx containing DFLOW/OKX/Jupiter aggregator program ids, or a Pump AMM swap plus a second DEX leg (Meteora/Raydium), is dropped as arbitrage/internal noise — a clean "copy only the primary intent" filter.
  - **SIMULATION_MODE env:** dry-runs via `simulateTransaction` before any on-chain send (built-in fill-simulation analog); `commitment:"processed"`, `skipPreflight:false`, `maxRetries:3`.
  - `retry.ts`: instant-retry helper that never retries on 401/`Unauthorized` or validation errors (fail-closed on auth).
  - Pluggable tx-submitter landing providers (Jito / NextBlock / Helius Sender / Astralane / Zeroslot) with tip-instruction builders.
- **Security/complexity flags:** **HIGH severity — live paid-API keys committed in plaintext:** Helius key `cf0c20ed-…022c` in `config/index.ts` and `dex/pumpfun/sdk/util.ts`; Jupiter key `46a30830-…42bd` in `dex/jupiter/swap.ts`. Wallet key is env-based. No rug/bundle detection beyond the arbitrage skip; no PnL/leaderboard scoring; copy-trade executes live swaps (paper broker only via SIMULATION_MODE).
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Port the chain-surviving copy-trading semantics: percent-vs-fixed buy sizing, mirrored-sell-fraction (with >=0.95 → sell-all), arbing-second-leg/aggregator filtering, non-blocking stream→queue→executor, and SIMULATION_MODE dry-run before live. RH 4663 needs EVM event parsing in place of the Solana DEX parsers. Do not copy the committed keys — read from env.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** (sizing, arbitrage-skip, sim-mode, queue patterns into Memeland TS); **Skip** the Solana DEX parser layer. Rotate/don't reuse the exposed keys.

### SRC-195 sergafon/solana-copy-trading (Rust, ~50 files)
- **What it is:** An ultralow-latency Rust copy-trading bot for Pump.fun with its own Yellowstone gRPC Geyser stream, transaction-simulation dry-run mode, Postgres (diesel) persistence, and 0slot landing. Claims 500–700k tx/s parse throughput, 2–5 ms Geyser→submit, slot 0–1 landing. Near-dupe concept of SRC-116 but a different, Rust, performance-focused implementation — no DEX breadth, no Polymarket side, so not a triple audit.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - Deterministic Geyser->copy loop: decode only pump-fun create/buy/sell instruction discriminators (`modules/decoder/config.rs`) → build tx → send; orchestrated by `copy_process_service.rs`.
  - **Fail-closed mirroring:** on target-sell, skip if target position absent from cache; on copy-sell, skip if copy position absent — no synthetic sells.
  - **Position-close prediction:** after a sell, recompute `amount_left` from the position cache and predict `Opened/Closed` from `amount_left/amount_total`; if Closed, burn residual + `close_account` (`modules/builder/pump_fun/instruction_builder.rs`) so a copy never leaves dust.
  - **Simulation-first:** `SIMULATE_MODE=true` default; `TransactionSender::simulate_transaction` on Helius RPC, never sends (paper/dry-run default).
  - **Fill-quality telemetry** (`transaction_service.rs`): wait-loop (60 s) for both target and copy signatures, then log the pair with `diff_time_s`, `diff_price` (copy fill-slippage), `diff_slot`, fees — a reusable copy-quality/impact measurement.
  - Config discipline: fixed per-order size `ORDER_SOL_AMOUNT`, 20% slippage, `skipPreflight:true maxRetries:0` on landing, priority-fee cache; **non-simulate mode `exit(1)`s when `FOLLOW_WALLETS` is unsound** (`copy_process_service.rs`).
- **Security/complexity flags:** No hardcoded secrets — `HELIUS_API_KEY`, `WALLET_PRIVATE_KEY`, `ZEROSLOT_API_KEY` all env-read with `.expect()` (fails fast at startup). No GMGN; own Geyser stream + Helius RPC + 0slot landing. No rug/bundle detection; it is pure execution speed and determinism.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Most portable ideas: strict position tracking + predicted-close cleanup, sim-mode-first dry-run, no-sell-without-cached-position, and confirm-pair fill-slippage telemetry (`diff_price`) as a copy-quality signal. Port in TS and keep the exit-on-unsound-live-config fail-closed guard, which matches Memeland's risk-gate philosophy. Pump.fun instruction crafting itself is not reusable.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** (copy-trading determinism, predicted-close, confirm/fill-slippage telemetry, sim-first); **Skip** as runnable dependency (Rust, Solana-locked instruction crafting).

### SRC-145 marksantiago290/KOLscan-leaderboard-scraping (Python, 1 file)
- **What it is:** A single-file Selenium + BeautifulSoup scraper of `kolscan.io/leaderboard` that extracts daily/weekly/monthly KOL leaderboard rows to CSV + Prisma (per-wallet upsert). Data is scraped from a public web page — no paid API, no GMGN API.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - A clean leaderboard scoring data model: `{period_days, wallet_name, wallet_address, account_name, win, loss, pnl_usd, pnl_sol, telegram, twitter}` — a ready field set for Memeland's KOL/leaderboard scoring.
  - Parses the Next.js RSC payload (`self.__next_f.push`) for wallet→social lookup merged with DOM rows; Prisma `upsert` keyed on `wallet_address` for idempotent storage.
  - Period iteration (Daily/Weekly/Monthly) with per-period error isolation.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No secrets. Extremely fragile: hardcoded hashed CSS classes (`leaderboard_leaderboardUser__8OZpJ`, `leaderboard_totalProfitNum__HzfFO`) and Next.js RSC string-slicing break on any frontend change. Scrapes a public site (ToS/anti-bot risk). No PnL math — reads displayed values only. No risk/sizing/execution logic.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Study the field schema (wallet identity + win/loss + pnl_usd/pnl_sol + socials per period) as a portable KOL-leaderboard record Memeland could compute from its own on-chain fills. Skip the DOM scraper implementation — brittle selectors, Solana-only, and scraped PnL is not a trustworthy trading edge.
- **Verdict:** **Study** (leaderboard field schema as a data-model idea); **Skip** the scraper as implemented.

### SRC-165 nirholas/kol-quest (TypeScript / Next.js + Bun + MCP, large multi-package)
- **What it is:** A full KOL/smart-money intelligence platform ("kol.quest") that ingests KolScan + GMGN + X/Twitter scrapers, writes to Postgres (Drizzle), and serves a Next.js app (leaderboards, real-time feed, community wallet submissions) plus a Bun REST API and an MCP (stdio) server. Heavy GMGN coupling: the smart-money category/PnL data model is GMGN-native.

  - Leaderboard/scoring field model from GMGN holdings/activities: `history_bought_cost`, `history_sold_income`, `realized_profit`, `realized_profit_pnl`, `history_total_buys/sells`, and per-timeframe `realized_profit_1d/7d/30d` + `winrate` — a complete PnL/leaderboard schema (fed by GMGN, but the field model is chain-agnostic).

  - MCP server exposing `kolscan_leaderboard`, `gmgn_wallets`, `wallet_stats`, `search_wallets` tools; community wallet-submission + vouch workflow with role-based moderation; per-user watchlist.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No hardcoded secrets found in scanned source — keys env-provided (GMGN_TOKEN, Helius/Birdeye/Moralis/etc. optional). GMGN access uses browser UA + `Referer` tokenless scraping (`fetchers/sources/gmgn.ts`) — ToS risk. GMGN-native data (PnL comes from GMGN, not computed locally). No execution/sizing/risk gates — analytics only, no rug/bundle detection.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Adopt the `fetchJSON` retry/429-backoff wrapper, idempotent ingest plus poll pipeline, and the multi-source dedup-merge by wallet address with the PnL/winrate field model as Memeland's leaderboard-scoring shape (computed from Memeland's own fills on RH 4663 rather than scraped/GMGN-priority). Study the MCP-wrapper pattern. Skip the GMGN-dependent fetch/scrape layer and the full Next.js platform under Memeland's low-GMGN-coupling constraint.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** (`fetchJSON` util, idempotent ingest/poll, multi-source merge + leaderboard data model into TS); **Skip** as a GMGN-coupling dependency and runnable platform.

## Batch 15 - agents, market-data & general deep-read (subagent Volta + manual)

### SRC-214 Tomortec/CryptoTradingAgents (Python, ~40 files) - P0
- **What it is:** LangGraph multi-agent crypto research/decision framework (fork of TauricResearch/TradingAgents, arXiv 2412.20138). Pipeline: 4 analysts (market/social/news/fundamentals) -> bull/bear debate -> research manager (invest judge) -> trader plan -> risky/neutral/safe debators -> risk judge emitting final Buy/Sell/Hold with entry/SL/TP. Report generator, no live execution.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - **Round-capped adversarial debate -> judge fusion** (`graph/conditional_logic.py`): invest debate stops at `2*max_debate_rounds`, risk debate at `3*max_risk_discuss_rounds`; speaker alternation (Risky->Safe->Neutral->Risky) drives the graph. Bounded LLM cost regardless of output; same shape fits Memeland's critic/whale voters fusion.
  - **Reflection + episodic memory loop** (`graph/reflection.py`, `agents/utils/memory.py`): after returns/losses are known, each component (bull, bear, trader, invest-judge, risk-manager) reflects on its own past call against the outcome, then stores situation->recommendation in per-agent Chroma vector memory (`n_matches=2`, embedding similarity). Decision time injects `past_memory_str` ("learn from past mistakes"). Directly maps to anti-overfit learning validation.
  - **Risk-judge deliverable** (`i18n/prompts/en.py` risk_manager): judge must refine the trader plan and output Buy/Sell/Hold + suggested entry, support, resistance, take-profit, stop-loss. Deterministic contract from a verbose LLM - reusable for Memeland's post-entry parameter gates.
  - **SignalProcessor** (`graph/signal_processing.py`): one cheap LLM call to reduce freeform final signal to discrete BUY/SELL/HOLD (drop-in candidate for a regex/structured-parse fallback).
  - **Dataflow tool wrappers** (`dataflows/interface.py`): every source returns a `"<source>: data unavailable"` string instead of raising, so the LLM degrades gracefully - a deliberate fail-degraded (not fail-closed) data contract.
- **Security/complexity flags:**
  - No hardcoded secrets found: all keys via env (`DASHSCOPE_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `GITEE_API_KEY`, `EMAIL_PASSWORD`) - low risk.
  - Heavy dependency surface (langchain + langgraph + chromadb + pandas + tqdm) - too heavy to adopt wholesale; port orchestration ideas, not the framework.
  - Pure LLM-first; no deterministic numeric validation of judge output - a hallucinated price level would pass through.
  - No execution/wallet surface (report-only), so no execution-security review applicable.
  - Chain-portability nil: Binance/taapi/CoinDesk/Reddit feeds, centralized CEX/news only.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** The strongest ideas are (1) round-capped adversarial debate then a judge that refines a trader plan into entry/SL/TP/TP2, and (2) per-voter reflection-memory keyed by situation similarity. Both port cleanly onto Memeland's multi-voter fusion and anti-overfit learning edges. Caveat: this is LLM-judged episodic memory, not statistical CV - Memeland should pair the pattern with its purged/lookahead-free validation so recalled "lessons" are not leaky or cherry-picked. Data layer is CEX-only and not reusable; final decision is freeform and must be hardened with deterministic gates (fail-closed) on entry/SL/TP and chain execution.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** (round-capped debate->judge fusion, reflection-memory loop, entry/SL/TP refinement contract; port orchestration to TS). **Study** the LangGraph plumbing. **Skip** the framework + CEX data layer.

### SRC-036 0xjeffro/tx-parser (Go, ~73 files)
- **What it is:** Library + example server that turns raw Solana `getTransaction` JSON into typed, human-readable actions. Covers Pump.fun (create/buy/sell + Anchor self-CPI logs), Raydium v4, Jupiter (route + DCA), OKX DEX, Photon, plus token/system/compute-budget primitives. No RPC calls of its own - the caller feeds it raw tx JSON.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - **Program-ID router -> per-program parser registry** (`solana/router.go`, `solana/registry/registry.go`): dispatch on program ID to a per-program `InstructionRouter` returning a typed `Action`. Unknown/parse-failed instructions degrade to `UnknownAction` that keeps program ID + error instead of aborting the whole tx (`solana/parser.go`). New programs (e.g. RH-chain AMMs) drop in without touching the router.
  - **Unified swap schema** (`solana/types/abstract_actions.go`): `SwapActionMixin{who, fromToken, fromTokenAmount, fromTokenDecimals, toToken, toTokenAmount, toTokenDecimals}` - exactly the row shape for swap->candle building.
  - **Mint/decimals resolved from pre/post token balances, amounts from inner transfers** (raydium `swap.go`, jupiter `route.go`): authoritative mints from `Pre/PostTokenBalances` for the user's source/dest accounts; amounts summed across inner `Transfer`/`TransferChecked` so aggregator multi-hop routes are handled correctly.
  - **Execution-governance fields surfaced:** Raydium exposes `MinimumAmountOut`, PumpFun buy exposes `MaxSolCost` + fee amount (detected via transfer to fee recipient), compute-budget parses `SetComputeUnitLimit`/`SetComputeUnitPrice`. These map to Memeland's slippage/cost gates.
  - **Anchor discriminator helper** (`utils/discriminator.go`): `sha256(instructionName)[:8]`, and PumpFun reads a merged self-CPI-log discriminator to recover realized amounts.
- **Security/complexity flags:**
  - No secrets anywhere - the library never holds keys or connects to RPC (caller supplies tx JSON). Low risk.
  - Go + `borsh-go`/`base58`/`gin` dependency stack; not TS. Either port the pattern to TS/viem or run as a Go sidecar.
  - Example `main.go` exposes an unauthenticated HTTP endpoint that parses arbitrary JSON (request-smuggling/DoS surface if exposed publicly - not a Memeland concern).
  - Router `recover()` swallows panics into `err` per-instruction - resilient, but a panic still returns a partial result rather than failing closed.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Directly fills the onchain swap->candle data-ingestion primitive for Solana and the whale-feed parser. The router/registry + unified swap schema + pre/post-balance mint resolution + inner-transfer-summing is the pattern to port to TS/viem (or keep the Go lib as a sidecar binary, which is cheap for a personal bot). Captured slippage/cost fields (`MinimumAmountOut`, `MaxSolCost`, compute-unit price) feed deterministic execution gates. Solana-specific, so portability is pattern-level, but the shape generalizes to EVM/RH via logs decoding.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** (port program-router + swap schema + mint-resolution + slippage capture to TS/viem, or run as Go sidecar). High value for Solana swap->candle and whale feed.

### SRC-050 akanz/onchain-trading-bot (TypeScript/NestJS, ~95 files incl. tests)
- **What it is:** A conservative multi-chain (Solana, BSC, Base, GMGN Robinhood chain) GMGN wallet/alert engine that never signs or submits a trade. Layered pipeline: seed candidate wallets from GMGN Smart Money/KOL/followed feeds + runner-contract scan -> track as `elite_observed` -> qualify repeatable wallets -> nominate tokens -> run fail-closed safety gates -> dedup and emit CALL/RESEARCH alerts over Telegram + SSE web API. Directly mirrors Memeland's own architecture.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - **Deterministic fail-closed scoring gates** (`src/scoring.ts` `scoreToken`, `screenTrackedBuyToken`; `src/signals.ts` `passesMarketGate`): every check becomes a PASS/FAIL reason string; `passed` = zero failures; `score` = fraction of checks passed (transparent, not learned). Missing/unknown critical fields FAIL by design; an all-zero GMGN holder-analysis block is treated as "unavailable," never as safe 0% concentration. `knownMax` counts unknown as a failure. This is exactly Memeland's "deterministic gates before LLM" principle.
  - **Wilson-adjusted win rate** (`wilsonLowerBound`): lower bound of the confidence interval (z=1.96) penalizes small samples - a direct anti-overfit signal for wallet qualification.
  - **Wallet qualification ladder** (`qualifyTrackedWallet`, `assessWalletPerformance`): deliberately OR-based multiple evidence paths - N distinct tokens each >=300% position ROI (>=5) or >=500% (>=3), OR 30d AND all-time realized ROI >=200% plus min sample, win rate, Wilson floor, and profit floors (30d >= $1k, all >= $5k). README: requiring every wallet to clear both windows prevents a few winners hiding weak wallets behind a group average.
  - **Co-buy clustering + price-chase gate** (`clusterEvents`): groups buys of the same token within `window_seconds`, requires min qualified wallets, computes median entry price and aggregate buy; gate rejects when current price exceeded median entry by `max_price_chase_ratio`. Directly maps to Memeland's whale/co-buy attribution + entry-chase risk gate.
  - **Surge detection + attribution** (`src/surge-attribution.ts`): volume-confirmed one-minute expansions (SIDEWAYS BREAKOUT / DIP REVERSAL / MOMENTUM EXPANSION) with 180s merge; then `profitablePreMoveTrader` requires explicit position ROI >=300% (realized OR unrealized) entered within a lead window, and excludes bundler/rat/dex-bot/sniper tags. Attribution requires per-position evidence, not blended totals.
  - **Weighted GMGN rate gate** (`src/gmgn-rate-limit.ts` `GmgnRateGate`): token-bucket + per-request-type weights (holders/traders=5, signal/kline/trending cheaper) + priority queue + persisted cooldown (state file 0600, +10s buffer) + hard "safe cap" that clamps any configured burst/rate to an env ceiling. Reusable governance primitive for managing any paid/rate-limited data feed Memeland depends on.
  - **Late-chase rejection on live signals** (`isActionableMarketSignal`): repeated price signal without a baseline is indistinguishable from a late chase and is rejected; cap on trigger multiple (1.5x) and signal count (2).
  - **Lifecycle/durable delivery separation**: wallet ingestion persists received/deduplicated/stale/safety_queued/passed/suppressed; token delivery has 6h per-contract cooldown and confidence upgrades (OBSERVE->POTENTIAL->BUY SIGNAL) use separate dedup keys so an early cooldown can't suppress a later upgrade.
- **Security/complexity flags:**
  - No hardcoded secrets found (clean scan): `GMGN_API_KEY`/`GMGN_PRIVATE_KEY`/`TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN`/`TWITTER_TOKEN` all via env; README explicitly says never commit keys. Low risk.
  - Heavy stack: NestJS + Mongoose/MongoDB + Playwright + Telegram bot + SSE web API - too much to adopt for a personal bot; port the pure logic modules (scoring/signals/attribution/rate-gate), skip the framework.
  - Fully GMGN-coupled by design (this is its core), which conflicts with Memeland's low-GMGN-coupling goal - decouple the gate logic from the GMGN feed.
  - Admin-invite + `/export1d` chat-history capture and SHA-256 single-use invites are thoughtful but out of scope for Memeland.
  - Research/alert-only: no trade execution, so no signing/order-flow security surface to compare against.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** The closest analogue in this batch. Highest-value portable TS pieces are the deterministic fail-closed gate scorer (with score-as-evidence audit trail), Wilson-adjusted wallet qualification, co-buy clustering with median-entry price-chase gate, position-ROI surge attribution with noisy-tag exclusion, and the weighted + persisted rate gate. These slot directly into Memeland's backtest/dry-run attribution and execution-governance edges. Adopt the logic as pure functions; keep data source pluggable so GMGN can be swapped for Memeland's own onchain feed.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** (deterministic gate scorer, Wilson qualification, co-buy cluster + price-chase, surge attribution, weighted rate gate - port as pure TS functions). **Skip** the NestJS/Mongo/Telegram app scaffolding and the GMGN-only data layer.

### SRC-112 holdout-labs/lookahead-free (Python, ~10 files)
- **What it is:** A zero-dependency Python library (v0.1.3, MIT) that proves look-ahead-freedom on the *value-independent fragment* of a data pipeline described as a declarative, temporally annotated DAG JSON. You declare every op with a temporal bound (`release` for reads, `window_end` for windows, `read_cutoff` for PIT/vintage reads, `decision_time` for decisions); `lf check` then proves in linear time that no decision consumes data not knowable at decision time. Grounded in Fonseca (2026) arXiv:2607.04958: look-ahead-freedom is undecidable in general (Pi-0-1-hard) but admits a linear-time decidable type-effect system on the value-independent fragment. Separate `lf scan` is an AST heuristic for value-dependent research code (explicitly not a proof).
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - Declarative DAG model: ops (`read/window/resample/join/pit_read/vintage_read/transform/decision/write`) with `op_id`/`inputs`/`outputs` plus an `explicit_bound()`; output availability = declared bound or max of inputs (monotone availability propagation, fixed-point loop in `checks.py`).
  - Core checks all P0/fail-closed: `dag` (resolve op_id-or-output-name refs, ambiguous-output rejection, cycle detection), `decision_availability` (every decision input available at/before decision_time), `window_boundary` (window_end required, window ending after a consumer's decision is a look-ahead), `pit_reads` (cutoff required), `monotonicity` (no op produces a fact earlier than its inputs).
  - Heuristic boundary explicit: `value_dependent: true` ops are flagged P1 (warning) because their temporal structure is checked exactly but the op cannot be proven - undecidability is stated in every report. Zero-dependency, exit-code CI gate (0 pass / 1 P0 fail / 2 usage).
  - `lf scan` AST rules: H001 future subscript (`series[idx + k]`, k>0), H002 future window slice, H003 (review) data-fetch call without an `as_of`/`available_at`/`cutoff`/`date` param - small, auditable rule set, "extend in code, not by magic".
  - `checks.py` pity/careful format: P0 = look-ahead (fail-closed), P1 = heuristic boundary/warning, P2 = informational; verdict string PASS/FAIL with per-check `passed` + detail trail.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No network calls, no secrets. Model is a dataclass (not pydantic) with hand-rolled validation - fine for this scope. Small, single-responsibility, ~450 LOC total.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Directly targets Memeland's **anti-overfit/data-leakage** edge. Highest-value ports: (1) model Memeland's signal build as a declarative DAG where every input carries `release`/`window_end`/`read_cutoff` and the decision carries `decision_time`, then enforce decision-availability at gate time - this is exactly the "deterministic fail-closed risk gate before consensus" pattern Memeland wants, applied to data timing rather than trade size; (2) adopt the P0/P1/P2 severity ladder and the "value-dependent fragment cannot be proven, say so" honesty boundary for LLM-produced signals (freeform LLM output = value-dependent frontier); (3) the monotonic availability check prevents a backtest that "decides" at 18:00 using 16:00 candle close as if it were known at 15:00. Portable to TS as a small pure `checkPipeline(dagJson)` with no deps. The heuristic scan (H001/H002 future subscript) is directly reusable for a Memeland backtest hardening lint.
- **Verdict:** **Adopt** - port the DAG + linear-time decision-availability checker and the P0/P1 severity + "value-dependent can't be proven" honesty boundary as a small TS library; wire it into Memeland's backtest/dry-run attribution so every signal build and trade decision is accompanied by a machine-checkable timing evidence layer.

### SRC-159 nansen-ai/nansen-cli (Node.js, ~100 source files + large test suite)
- **What it is:** A large commercial CLI (~200KB of trading code) that drives Nansen's managed trading API across Solana/Base: quotes, swaps, bridges, perps, limit orders, x402 (permit-based) payments, wallet/privy keychain management, alerts/webhooks, and MCP/agent integration. Heavily coupled to Nansen-hosted endpoints and proprietary quote/simulation infrastructure; essentially a thin-ish client over a private backend.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - `src/swap-simulation.js`: defence-in-depth fail-closed simulation. Run the swap tx through a **separate trace-capable RPC independent of the quote builder** (`SIMULATION_RPCS`), parse Transfer/Approval logs by topic, normalize native ETH via a sentinel (`0xeeee...`), compute net wallet balance deltas client-side, and assert they match the quote's inputMint/outputMint amounts - fail-closed on any mismatch. Deliberately excludes `SELFDESTRUCT` refunds and `STATICCALL/DELEGATECALL` value fields so net native delta is "stricter, never the reverse." Distinguishes degrade conditions (`NO_SIM_RPC`/`NOT_SIM_CAPABLE`/`SIM_RPC_ERROR`, warn-and-proceed per policy) from outcome problems (`SIM_REVERTED`, must not be silently ignored).
  - `src/trade-validation.js`: static tx validation with explicit sanity ceilings - `MAX_PRIORITY_FEE_LAMPORTS` (~0.01 SOL), `MIN_GAS_AMOUNTS`, `FEE_BUFFER`, `SOLANA_MAX_COMPUTE_UNITS` (1.4M), native-dust thresholds, SPL/ERC20 selector whitelists (`BARE_ERC20_OUTER_SELECTORS`), `HIGH_PERCENTAGE_THRESHOLD`/`AUTO_ADJUST_THRESHOLD_PERCENT` fee guards. Bounds-check constants kept central and commented with rationale.
  - Fail-closed broadcast semantics tested explicitly (`bridge-broadcast-failclosed.test.js`, `builder-fee-ceiling.test.js`); API key handling is strictly `process.env.NANSEN_API_KEY` (no baked-in creds), secrets only materialized transiently in keychain/privy.
- **Security/complexity flags:** **No hardcoded secrets** - all keys/credentials come from env (test files use obvious fake `test-key`/`nk_test_*` values; no live key found). Complex app surface (Privy, x402 Permit2/Perp, walletconnect, webhooks) far beyond Memeland's personal-bot scope; a private/commercial backend is the real dependency, so the "source" is not self-contained. No ML/backtest/anti-overfit code at all - it is pure execution/ops tooling.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Maps to Memeland's **execution-governance** edge: the swap-outcome-simulation pattern (simulate on an independent trace RPC, assert balance deltas match the quote, fail closed on mismatch, classify degrade vs outcome errors) is directly portable and is the strongest takeaway. The central sanity-ceiling constants (max priority fee, min gas, fee buffer, compute units) match the deterministic fail-closed gate philosophy Memeland already wants, and the "keep verification independent of the party that built the quote" rule is a good governance invariant. Nothing here helps anti-overfit/backtest attribution (execution-only). The full app is far too heavy and Nansen-coupled to adopt.
- **Verdict:** **Study** (overall framework too heavy, private-API-coupled, execution-only). **Adapt** the specific fail-closed swap-outcome simulation + sanity-ceiling gate pattern as a portable TS helper for Memeland's execution-governance layer.

### SRC-064 bmoscon/cryptofeed (Python, ~800MB repo incl. all exchange impls + test data; core package ~20 files)
- **What it is:** The most established open-source crypto market-data feed library (2017-2026, ~30 exchanges). `Feed` subclasses stream normalized L1/L2/L3 books, trades, tickers, candles, funding, open-interest and liquidations over websockets/REST and fan them out to async callbacks and pluggable backends. Repo is huge due to per-exchange implementations and fixture data; the `cryptofeed/` core package is small and self-contained.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:**
  - **Closed-candle timing (`feed.py`)**: `candle_closed_only=True` returns only completed/closed candles; `candle_interval` normalization map and `valid_candle_intervals` validation. Directly maps to Memeland's swap->candle builder - never emit/consume a forming (not-yet-closed) candle, the same timing discipline as lookahead-free's `candle_closed_only`-style gate.
  - **Connection resilience (`connection_handler.py`)**: `ConnectionHandler.run` reconnect loop - `retries` (or `-1` infinite), exponential backoff `delay *= 2`, successful connect resets retry count, `_watcher` watchdog trips when no message within `timeout` (checked every `timeout_interval`), optional `keepalive` pinger, `ExhaustedRetries` after exhausting. Robust pattern for Memeland's on-chain swap/feed watchers.
  - **Connection primitives (`connection.py`)**: `AsyncConnection` with a `ConnectionStats` dataclass + `weakref.WeakSet` live registry + `connection_stats()` observability; connection-level raw-data observers (`on_ws_open/message/send/close`, `on_http`, `on_http_poll`) - a clean tap point for ingesting raw messages before normalization; `HTTPAsyncConn` with 429 rate-limit retry (`retry_delay` default 60s); `HTTPPoll` periodic poller generator; `WSAsyncConn` with defaults `ping_interval=20, ping_timeout=60, max_queue=1024`.
  - **Subscription shaping (`feed.py`, `connection.py`)**: `WebsocketEndpoint` splits subscriptions by instrument/channel filters and per-connection `limit` (batches subscriptions that exceed a venue limit); `delay_start` staggers connections to dodge 429s.
  - **Data-integrity gates**: crossed-book detection with a tolerance streak (`CROSSED_BOOK_TOLERANCE=3`), exchange checksum validation toggles, per-update sequence numbers - fail after `N` consecutive corrupt updates (`BidAskOverlapping`).
  - **Lifecycle/supervision**: `Feed._run` under `asyncio.TaskGroup`; backend writers supervised with a single `_backend_error` and fail-stop on first writer failure; REST pollers supervised with `RetryPolicy` exponential backoff and attempt-reset after a healthy 300s (`RESTART_RESET_SECONDS`).
  - **Normalized schema (`defines.py`)**: constant taxonomy for book/trades/candles and explicit orderbook layout (bid/ask `SortedDictionary`, L3 by order-id, delta semantics with `size=0` = delete level). A clean contract for Memeland's normalized swap/trade events.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No hardcoded secrets in the core (config via env/config file). Market-data only - no order placement/execution code, so no signing surface to compare. `aiohttp`/`websockets` deps make the asyncio pattern portable but framework-heavy; the per-exchange layer is CEX-specific (no on-chain/DEX feeds), so only the ingestion/resilience skeleton transfers.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Best data-ingestion reference in this batch for the **websocket/feed -> candle building** edge: adopt the closed-candle-only timing rule, the watchdog + exponential-backoff reconnect loop, the connection-level raw-message observer tap, the 429 stagger/delay handling, and the normalized-schema constant taxonomy as portable TS/Python building blocks for Memeland's Robinhood/Solana/BSC swap->candle pipeline. The crossed-book/checksum/sequence integrity gates parallel the data-quality guards Memeland wants before feeding candles to models. No anti-overfit/CV code here (data-only); `candle_closed_only` is the one timing-relevant control worth carrying over. Skip the full framework and the CEX exchange implementations.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** - port the closed-candle timing, watchdog+backoff reconnect loop, raw-message observer tap, 429 handling, and normalized-schema taxonomy as small building blocks. **Skip** the full cryptofeed framework and CEX-specific exchange layer (no DEX/on-chain feeds).

### SRC-068 ccxt/ccxt (TypeScript, monorepo, 100+ CEX exchange implementations) - P2
- **What it is:** ccxt — the ubiquitous **unified cryptocurrency exchange trading library** (Python/JS/PHP), a single normalized API over 100+ **centralized** exchanges (Binance/Coinbase/Kraken/etc.). Repo is huge and cannot be cloned here (connection reset), so I audited two standalone, dependency-free TS primitives that ccxt ships as portable building blocks: **`Precise.ts`** (arbitrary-precision decimal math on `bigint` + an order-independent static lookup table of 10^n for powers-of-ten, with exponentiation fallback past the table) and the leaky-bucket **`Throttle`/rate-limiter** used to pace per-exchange API calls.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** (1) **`Precise` class** (`Precise.ts`, ~12KB): constructor parses scientific/exponent notation and decimal strings into `{integer: bigint, decimals}`; `mul`/`div`/`add`/`sub`/compare are exact `bigint` operations — no floating-point drift. Includes static helpers `stringGe/stringLt/stringLe` for safe string-number comparisons. The static bounded `powersOfTen` lookup table (index 0..128) with an exponentiation fallback is a neat order-independent O(1)-ish pattern for repeated `10^n`. (2) **Leaky-bucket Throttler**: ccxt's rate-limiter shapes calls per-exchange (cost bucket, min-time between calls, timeout/429-aware), used to stay under each exchange's request budget. (3) Normalized market/order/ticker `define` taxonomy across exchanges.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No committed secrets (exchange keys are runtime credentials, never in-repo). **CEX-only surface**: ccxt's unified API covers centralized order-book exchanges — **no DEX/AMM/rug/memecoin/RH-4663/on-chain logic at all**. Adding it as a dependency would drag in a massive multi-exchange abstraction that Memeland does not use (Memeland trades RH 4663 + Solana + BSC on-chain, not via CEX order books). Only the two small primitives (`Precise`, Throttler) transfer.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Not green as a dependency — ccxt targets CEX REST/WS order books, the exact opposite of Memeland's on-chain DEX execution; there is no RH 4663 or on-chain feed here. The portable slice is **`Precise.ts`**: Memeland's quoting/sizing layer should not accumulate `number` float drift on USD/token decimals — port this exact `bigint`-backed `Precise` (string-parsing + `powersOfTen` table + exact `mul/div` + `stringGe/Lt/Le`) into its TS math module, and adopt the leaky-bucket Throttler for RH explorer/RPC 429 pacing (complements the rolling-window backoff already noted from SRC-063/167/195). Re-implement as small files; do not install ccxt.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt** (port the `Precise` `bigint`-decimal math class and the leaky-bucket Throttler as small, dependency-free TS primitives for Memeland's quoting/sizing + RPC 429 pacing); **Skip** the ccxt library itself (CEX-only, no DEX/RH-4663/on-chain coverage, heavy 100+-exchange abstraction not used).

### SRC-176 OpenBB-finance/OpenBB (Python, large FastAPI "Open Data Platform" monorepo) - P2
- **What it is:** OpenBB — an open-source **"Open Data Platform"** (AGPL) that unifies market/company/on-chain data behind a single Python/SDK interface, exposed across multiple surfaces (REST API, Python library, and an MCP server). Equities/fundamental/macro-centric (free/paid provider aggregator + institutional terminal). Repo is too large to clone here (connection reset; tarball times out), so I audited its architecture from the README/provider/extension model rather than a full code walk.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** (1) **Provider/extension plugin architecture behind a thin unified facade**: OpenBB routes requests through a single normalized query layer to swappable, vendor-specific **provider extensions** (each provider = a plugin implementing the same query interface), so the caller-facing API stays stable while providers are added/removed behind it. (2) **Multi-surface exposure from one core**: the same domain logic is served as REST endpoints, a Python SDK, and an MCP tool set — one core, many I/O surfaces. (3) Vendor-isolation philosophy: no hard dependency on any single provider; providers degrade/are skipped when their key/endpoint is absent.
- **Security/complexity flags:** AGPL license; **equities/macro-centric** — its provider set is stocks/funds/options and some on-chain aggregates, **not memecoin/RH-4663/Solana/BSC trading**. Heavy platform (FastAPI + many optional providers + terminal app). No secrets committed (keys injected per provider at runtime).
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Not green as a platform to adopt — equities-focused data aggregation, AGPL, no RH 4663/memecoin/trading logic, and way heavier than Memeland needs. The **one idea worth copying is the architecture**, not the code: isolate Memeland's data sources behind a **thin normalized query facade with pluggable provider extensions** (each chain/API = a provider implementing the same interface, missing provider = graceful skip) and expose the same core over **multiple surfaces (REST/MCP/in-process)**. Memeland already has cleaner, lighter references for this in SRC-218 (BlockAtlas capability-interface adapter registry) and SRC-159 (nansen-cli tool/CLI surface); treat OpenBB as the conceptual template for "one core, many surfaces, vendor-isolated providers" rather than a source to port.
- **Verdict:** **Study** the provider-extension/plugin-behind-unified-facade architecture and the one-core-multi-surface (REST/MCP/Python) exposure pattern as a design reference for Memeland's data layer (superseded by lighter equivalents in SRC-218/159); **Skip** OpenBB as a dependency/platform (AGPL, equities-centric, no RH 4663/memecoin/trading, heavy FastAPI stack not portable to Memeland's TS bot).

## Batch 16 - meme-smart-money & GMGN external web deep-reads (subagent Kepler)

### SRC-001 GMGN Agent API (official agent skills/API overview) - P1
- **What it is:** GMGN's official AI-agent integration guide for multi-chain market queries, token research, portfolio inspection, and swaps. It documents the hosted-wallet/key-pair model, CLI/skills entry points, supported chains, and the boundary between read-only queries and trading.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** Install via `npx skills add GMGNAI/gmgn-skills`; CLI is `npx gmgn-cli` with formatted JSON or `--raw` single-line JSON. Query skills `gmgn-market`, `token`, and `portfolio` require `GMGN_API_KEY`; `gmgn-swap` additionally requires the matching `GMGN_PRIVATE_KEY`. API keys are created at `gmgn.ai/ai` after uploading a locally generated public key. Documented capabilities cover token basic info, live price, contract security, liquidity-pool status, top holders/traders, candlesticks from `1m` to `1d`, trending tokens, wallet holdings/transactions/PnL, and swap routing/slippage/order-status polling. The page does not publish raw REST paths, response schemas, numeric request limits, or API pricing. Only IPv4 requests are supported. Supported base tokens/chains listed are SOL, BSC/BNB, Base/ETH, and USDC; ETH and new-chain integration was described as in progress on this page.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Private keys must never enter git, logs, chats, or screenshots and must match the public-key pair uploaded for the API key. Hosted-wallet execution and IP allowlisting reduce local transaction construction but increase vendor/custody dependence. No hardcoded secrets or pricing are shown; numeric rate limits are undocumented. The page does not establish RH 4663 support, despite a later GMGN blog claiming website support.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Adopt the documented credential split and capability contract in `GmgnProvider`: API-key-only reads, separate execution credentials, explicit chain/base-token capability checks, strict raw JSON handling, and swap status polling behind the approval queue. Keep private-key handling in the existing signer boundary and never let voter/LLM code call swap directly. This helps quant, ML, security, whale, sentiment, regime, critic, and provider-independence requirements only when wrapped by a chain adapter; it adds GMGN coupling and undocumented quota risk, so retain a fallback provider. RH 4663 needs an explicit capability probe before use.
- **Verdict:** **Adopt**

### SRC-002 GMGN Callout OpenAPI (official partner callout API) - P1
- **What it is:** An official partner API for publishing and reading wallet callouts, with an OpenAPI 3.0 contract. The base URL is `https://papi.gmgn.ai/callout/openapi/v1`; every endpoint is POST JSON and uses per-request AK/SK HMAC authentication.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** Endpoints are `/global`, `/token`, `/get_record`, and `/create`, all under the documented base path. `/global` accepts only `page_token`, returns a `{code,message,data}` envelope with `records` and `next_page_token`, has strict reverse chronological ordering, last-seven-days retention, 100 records/page, no filters, and 1 QPS per `ak`. `/token` requires `chain` and `call_token`, accepts `cursor` and `limit`, caps page size at 50, and returns `messages`, `has_more`, `top_message`, `vip_message`, `declaration_level`, and `next_cursor`. `/get_record` requires `chain` and `call_wallet`; `limit` is 1-50, but out-of-range values fall back to default 20 rather than cap to 50. `/create` requires `chain`, `call_wallet`, `call_token`, and numeric-string `twitter_id`, with optional Twitter fields, thesis, and callback target; business success is `data.success`, not merely HTTP 200. Records expose chain, wallet/token, price/MC as strings, ULID, Unix-ms time, symbol/logo, multiplier, Twitter identity, holding percentage, source, KOL/declaration flags, and callback fields. Token messages add content, media/profile fields, follower/like/reply counts, ISO `created_at`, and optional `amount_usd` for `vip_message`. Sign `ak + timestamp + method + path + rawQuery + exactRawBody` with HMAC-SHA256 lowercase hex; headers are `X-Ak`, `X-Timestamp`, `X-Signature`; timestamp tolerance is +/-30 seconds. Other endpoints share an approximately 10-QPS default pool; `/create` also has a per-key 24-hour quota and business cooldown/wallet/Twitter limits. No price is published.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Keep SK server-side; exact serialized bytes must be signed and sent. IP allowlists, invalid timestamps, API permissions, and key validity can produce HTTP 401; over-limit is HTTP 429. Decimal values are strings and omitted fields are genuinely absent. The API requires GMGN-issued AK/SK credentials and partner approval form. No hardcoded live keys were found. Chain names are string-based but RH 4663 support is not documented.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Adopt the read contract in a `GmgnCalloutProvider` for sentiment/KOL/whale evidence: HMAC signer, NTP/clock-health check, exact-body signing, cursor persistence, 1-QPS global bucket, shared read bucket, 429 backoff, envelope validation, decimal-safe parsing, and `has_more`-aware pagination. Use `/get_record` when source attribution matters and `/token` when token-level callout context is enough. Do not expose `/create` to trading logic. This directly strengthens sentiment, whale, critic, and auditable consensus inputs, but is GMGN-coupled and needs a per-chain capability map for RH 4663.
- **Verdict:** **Adopt**

### SRC-003 AI Trader (GMGN skill-market demo) - P2
- **What it is:** A read-only demo showing a deterministic screening funnel with GMGN data, safety gates, consensus, ML ranking, LLM explanation, manual approval, and position escape monitoring. It explicitly says the demo is not an official GMGN trading feature; local cloning requires the user's own API key.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** The displayed funnel is scan -> safety -> consensus -> ML rank -> LLM -> pending/manual buy. Fields include renounced status, bundler %, dev %, top-10 %, smart/KOL counts, dev score, timing, LLM result, priority, and decision. Safety examples block honeypots, bundler >30%, dev holding >10%, top-10 >50%; consensus blocks zero smart-money/KOL evidence; developer reputation uses stuck-inner/graduation/reskin signals. The demo exposes escape levels from stable/watch/high-risk/escape, with mint-authority regain, concentration, and weakening momentum examples. It demonstrates exposure caps, max positions, daily loss, and a kill streak counter, but no HTTP endpoints, JSON schemas, rate limits, or pricing are documented in the page.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Demo data and sample addresses are not a production API contract. LLM is deliberately last and cannot override deterministic safety. The live page is view-only; local execution requires a user-owned key. No hardcoded secret was found. It shows SOL/BSC/Base/ETH/Robinhood labels but does not prove backend RH 4663 coverage or chain-specific semantics.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Adapt the funnel and reason-coded reject log into RiskManager/RiskEngineV2 and the approval queue: hard safety vetoes first, then seven-voter consensus, then ML/LLM enrichment, with explicit position escape signals. Use normalized GMGN adapter fields and chain-local thresholds rather than copying demo constants. This helps all seven voters, kill-switch behavior, explainability, and operational simplicity; it adds no provider beyond GMGN and no documented API contract. RH 4663 should be enabled only after field parity and contract-risk validation.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt**

### SRC-004 MemeX (GMGN skill-market demo) - P2
- **What it is:** A browser-only BSC analysis agent combining public-chain data and Binance Alpha with six rule-engine skills: contract audit, smart-money structure, top addresses, candlestick patterns, X narrative heat, and Alpha catalysts. The page says the API key stays in browser localStorage and is sent only to `openapi.gmgn.ai`.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** The documented workflow scans a contract across all six skills and produces a composite score, plus Alpha-new, 1h-hot, and seven-day smart-money views. It is explicitly information aggregation/rule output, not an investment recommendation. No concrete endpoint paths, request/response schemas, rate limits, pricing, or key-rotation contract are exposed. The page requires a user-supplied GMGN API key from the API management page; no live key is embedded.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Browser localStorage is weaker than Memeland's server-side secret store and could expose a key to XSS or browser compromise. The page is BSC-oriented and does not document RH 4663. Binance Alpha is an additional vendor/data dependency. No hardcoded key was found and pricing/quotas are undocumented.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Study the six-skill decomposition and map contract audit, smart-money, holders, candles, sentiment, and catalyst evidence into existing voter inputs. Implement it through provider-neutral interfaces and server-side GMGN key rotation, not browser storage. It helps security, whale, quant, sentiment, ML, and critic voters but adds no new verified API contract and increases GMGN coupling if copied literally.
- **Verdict:** **Study**

### SRC-015 CoinMarketCap Meme View (meme-token category index) - P3
- **What it is:** A continuously updated public category/index page listing meme tokens by market capitalization, with trending/new/gainer views, market cap/volume, chain/category filters, and explanatory editorial content. It is a presentation page, not a trading API specification.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** The page exposes table concepts: rank, name/symbol, price, 1h/24h/7d changes, market cap, 24h volume, circulating supply, and a seven-day sparkline. It reported 5,358 category assets over 54 pages at scrape time, and links to CMC Crypto API, DEX API, keyless API, WebSocket, pricing, and documentation, but this page gives no endpoint schema, auth header, rate limit, or price tier. It references all-chain category browsing and live market-cap/volume summaries. The page includes a Robinhood-related token example but does not establish RH 4663 contract coverage.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Category membership and rankings are centralized, dynamic, and potentially stale relative to on-chain liquidity; symbols can collide. The page has sponsored buy links and market/editorial content, so it is unsuitable as a safety oracle. API credentials/pricing must be obtained elsewhere; no hardcoded secret was found. No RH chain guarantee is documented.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Skip as a runtime feed. At most, use a CMC API adapter later for broad market-regime or cross-check data, normalized by chain/address and never by symbol alone. It adds a paid/limited vendor and little incremental edge over GMGN plus on-chain feeds; it does not improve RH 4663 execution or provider independence unless used as a low-weight independent critic input.
- **Verdict:** **Skip**

### SRC-028 Mobula GMGN APIs Guide (public multi-chain alternative comparison) - P2
- **What it is:** Mobula's guide contrasts GMGN's restricted ecosystem APIs with Mobula's public multi-chain market-data API and examples. It positions Mobula as a broad data fallback rather than an execution replacement.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** Documented examples are `GET https://api.mobula.io/api/1/market/blockchain/pairs?blockchain=solana&sortBy=createdAt`, `GET /api/1/market/history?asset=solana&blockchain=solana`, and `GET /api/1/market/vesting?asset=tokenName&blockchain=ethereum`. The guide claims real-time prices/volume/liquidity, historical token data, vesting/unlocks, wallet transactions, 50+ chains, SDKs, and customizable queries. It does not publish request/response schemas, numeric rate limits, exact authentication header, or pricing on this page; it only says API-key integration support exists. The linked OpenAPI specification is external and was not treated as part of this page. RH 4663 is not named.
- **Security/complexity flags:** “50+ chains” does not establish RH 4663 data quality or contract-level token coverage. The guide is comparative marketing and claims GMGN APIs are not public, so validate freshness, pagination, quotas, and pricing before operating it. No hardcoded key was found. A second vendor adds normalization/cache/budget complexity.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Adapt only as a candidate provider interface for historical price, pair, liquidity, vesting, and wallet enrichment. Put it behind `MarketDataProvider` with per-chain capability discovery and cache/quotas; use it as a GMGN-independent critic/regime/quant fallback, not as an execution source. It could improve provider independence and chain portability if RH 4663 is verified, but the page alone does not justify integration cost.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt**

### SRC-033 Meme Sentinels (ETHGlobal showcase) - P2
- **What it is:** A HackMoney 2026 showcase for an AI DeFi intelligence/trading platform that combines fast indexed ERC-20 data, social sentiment, wallet analysis, multi-agent scoring, and restricted smart-contract execution.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** The project claims Envio Hypersync ingestion across Ethereum, Polygon, Arbitrum, Base, and Optimism; aggregate metrics for liquidity, market cap, yield, pairs, and transaction history; social scoring from X/Reddit; refresh cadence of five minutes for top 20, 20 minutes for top 100, and 30 minutes for the rest; and wallet tracking for profitable traders. Its six agents are Scout, Yield, Risk, Alert, Settlement, and Orchestrator, coordinated with Google A2A/pub-sub. It uses Node/TypeScript/Express, caching/batching, Viem, React Query, and a Uniswap V2 router with approval/slippage protection. The agent may buy/sell but cannot withdraw funds; Hedera HCS records decisions. No public API endpoint/schema, auth, rate limit, or price is documented. RH 4663 is not listed.
- **Security/complexity flags:** This is a showcase description, not an operational API contract or evidence of profitable edge. It adds Hypersync, social APIs, Groq, A2A, Hedera, and custom execution complexity. The no-withdrawal permission boundary is good, but multi-agent orchestration and high-frequency indexing increase failure modes. No hardcoded secrets/pricing were found.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Adapt the refresh-tier cache policy, immutable decision/audit record idea, approval-only trading permission, and separate discovery/risk/alert services. Map Scout/Yield/Risk/Alert/critic responsibilities into the existing seven-voter and decoupled MarketSentinel architecture rather than adding A2A/Hedera. This helps security, quant, sentiment, regime, critic, auditability, and provider independence, but adds no documented feed contract and no RH 4663 proof.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt**

### SRC-237 Robinhood Chain Meme Coins with GMGN (GMGN blog guide) - P2
- **What it is:** GMGN's guide describes discovering, auditing, wallet-tracking, and trading meme coins on Robinhood Chain, using CASHCAT as a case study. It is workflow documentation, not an API reference.

The audit checklist includes honeypot/sellability, LP burned, contract permissions/renounce status, mintability, developer/top-10 concentration, liquidity versus market cap, and official contract verification. It says deposit/convert routes can provide RH ETH and states a usual 1% GMGN platform fee plus network gas. No REST endpoint, JSON schema, auth/key requirement, request limit, or tiered price sheet is documented. It describes RH support in the website but does not state chain ID 4663 or guarantee Agent API coverage.

The guide is marketing/educational and contains volatile case-study statistics; do not treat them as predictive edge. 1% is a blog-level fee statement and must be revalidated against official fee docs. No hardcoded secrets were found. RH infrastructure is young and the article itself notes sequencer/decentralization limitations.

Keep execution behind approval queue and kill-switch. This directly helps security, quant, critic, regime, and chain-portability requirements and validates a GMGN RH adapter, but does not supply an API contract; verify actual endpoint capability before wiring reads/trades.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt**

## Batch 17 - MCP-data, trading-risk & chain-execution external web deep-reads (subagent Boole)

### SRC-026 Dune MCP (onchain analytics remote MCP) - P1
- **What it is:** Dune's official remote MCP server for discovering indexed datasets, creating/running DuneSQL queries, inspecting executions, materializing views, and managing visualizations/dashboards. The documented transport is Streamable HTTP at `https://api.dune.com/mcp/v1`, with POST for MCP requests and GET for stream resumption/event replay.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** The documented read/query registry includes `searchDocs`, `searchTables`, `listBlockchains`, `searchTablesByContractAddress`, `getTableSize`, `getDuneQuery`, `executeQueryById`, `getExecutionResults`, `listMaterializedViews`, `getMaterializedView`, `getUsage`, and query-result resources; write/mutating tools include `createDuneQuery`, `updateDuneQuery`, materialized-view create/refresh/update/delete, visualization create/update/delete, and dashboard create/update/archive. The page gives names and purposes, but not per-tool JSON input/output schemas. Auth is OAuth 2.0 (PKCE S256, scope `mcp:dune:full`, authorization/token/register/JWKS endpoints under `dune.com/oauth/mcp`) or API key via `x-dune-api-key` header or `?api_key=`. Dune exposes indexed-chain discovery, but the fetched page does not confirm RH chain 4663 or its table count. Billing is credit/usage based; `getUsage` reports current billing-period credit usage. No numeric request rate limit or free-tier amount is documented on this page.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Never put an API key in a committed URL; query-string auth is specifically documented but weaker operationally than headers. OAuth adds browser/refresh-token state. Long `getExecutionResults` polls can exceed a 60-second Codex timeout and leave the transport closed; the docs recommend `tool_timeout_sec = 300` and a session restart after closure. Query scans and materialized refreshes can incur credits; this is a closed, vendor-hosted registry and the fetched docs do not establish RH 4663 coverage.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Adapt the closed read-only discovery/analysis pattern into the existing read-tools MCP surface: expose chain capabilities, contract/table discovery, bounded query execution, execution status/results, and usage/budget telemetry behind a provider adapter. Keep Dune for offline regime/DEX-volume research only, not the live GMGN path; normalize results to `{chainId, address, timestamp, metric, value, source}` and reject missing chain support. This adds Dune credentials/credits and a second analytics dependency, but improves GMGN-independence and supports deterministic quant/regime/critic evidence. It helps provider independence, chain portability, cheap deterministic math, and auditable consensus evidence; it does not itself provide an RH-4663 guarantee or trading execution. The MCP is a useful closed read-only registry pattern Memeland can replicate locally for RH 4663, while mutating dashboard/query tools should stay out of the trading runtime.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt**

### SRC-246 Bitquery MCP (multi-chain market intelligence and investigations) - P1
- **What it is:** A hosted MCP server at `https://mcp.bitquery.io` exposing Bitquery's market, trader, transfer-tracing, labels, AML, and SQL datasets to MCP clients. The landing page documents more than 100 tools and guided prompts rather than a static complete schema.
- **Key reusable pieces / patterns:** Documented prompt workflows are `money_flow`, `risk_screen`, `address_report`, and `entity_report`. Tool families include `find_tokens`, `find_currencies`, `token_chains`, `token_dex_venues`, `chain_capabilities`, `token_price`, `token_ohlcv`, `pair_ohlcv`, `pool_recent_trades`, `trending_tokens`, `top_traders`, `profitable_traders_by_token`, `accumulating_traders_by_token`, `trader_profile`, `trader_positions`, `trader_activity`, `tx_trades`, `address_labels`, `labels_for_addresses`, `addresses_by_label`, `find_label_values`, `execute_sql`, and per-chain `*_address_flow_summary`, `*_flow_edges`, `*_trace_next_hop`, `*_trace_dominant_path`, `*_transfers_in/out`, `*_tx_transfers`, `*_token_holders`, `*_find_events`, and `*_find_calls`. The site says tools/list returns the authoritative current input/output shapes and chain matrix. DEX trades cover Ethereum, Arbitrum, Base, Polygon, Optimism, BNB Chain, Tron, and Solana; transfer tracing covers Ethereum, Polygon, Arbitrum, Base, Optimism, Tron, Bitcoin, and Solana. RH 4663 is not listed. OAuth 2.1 browser login is documented; tokens refresh for about 30 days. A Bitquery-account free tier is inherited; heavy rates, historical scans, and `execute_sql` require paid plans. No numeric rate limit or price is published on the fetched page.
- **Security/complexity flags:** OAuth is safer than hardcoded keys but introduces hosted-account and token lifecycle dependency. `execute_sql` is paid and can create unbounded scan/cost risk; enforce query time, rows, and credits. The tool catalog and schemas can change server-side. Chain coverage omits RH 4663, so blindly treating EVM output as portable is unsafe. AML labels are evidence inputs, not an autonomous trade verdict.
- **For Memeland / edge check:** Adapt the normalized read-only provider interface around `token_ohlcv`/`pair_ohlcv`, `trending_tokens`, `pool_recent_trades`, `profitable_traders_by_token`, `accumulating_traders_by_token`, token holders, labels, and `chain_capabilities`; add an RH adapter only if Bitquery later documents 4663. Place it in shared price-feed/whale/security enrichment, never execution. Normalize to `{chainId, token, venue, ts, price, volume, liquidity?, trader?, confidence, source}` and cache/budget it. This can reduce GMGN coupling and strengthen quant, ML, whale, security, and critic voters, but adds a paid vendor and network-specific schema mapping. Its `tools/list` plus read-only registry and `chain_capabilities` are directly reusable for Memeland's RH-4663 MCP surface.
- **Verdict:** **Adapt**

It is read-only, so it cannot bypass Memeland approval queue or kill-switch, but it also cannot validate execution venue prices.

It could help price-feed redundancy and critic disagreement, but adds little chain portability, does not reduce GMGN coupling for token discovery, and provides no RH-4663 evidence. No hardcoded keys were found; pricing is stated free, but limits are undocumented.

## Batch 18 - general GitHub repos deep-read (subagent Rawls)
### SRC-037 0xmfox/rabiq (TypeScript) - RH-4663 token dossier + deployer memory, two-lane RPC throttle
- **What it is:** A personal browser/CLI research tool for Robinhood Chain (id 4663): paste a contract address, get a live "dossier" of chain facts (Pons V2 launch record, deployer, fee recipient, creator tax, phase), DexScreener market data, linked GitHub repo facts, the deployer's full launch history, and curve/pool pricing when DexScreener has no pair. Dossiers persist in localStorage; a pure `diffSnapshots` layer turns successive snapshots into change alerts.
- **Key pattern:** The strongest transferable piece is `src/lib/chain.ts`: a viem transport wrapper that throttles the public RH RPC through two priority lanes (foreground live reads vs. background history scans) with slot gaps (`GAP_MS`, `BG_GAP_MS`) and an adaptive pause on 429/CORS throttle, so a long log scan can never starve `eth_blockNumber` or a fresh launch read. `src/lib/pons.ts` `logsSplit` splits indexed `getLogs` ranges on query-cap/timeout and warm-retries before splitting. Chain reads batch into one Multicall3 `eth_call`; the L2 block comes from ArbSys `arbBlockNumber` because RH is an Arbitrum chain. Pons V2 factory phase (`curve|swept|graduated|rescued`) plus curve reserve/trade scans price tokens when no pair exists yet.
- **For Memeland:** Directly relevant and low-coupling: it does NOT depend on GMGN, instead combining public RPC + DexScreener, so it supports the de-coupling goal. Adopt the two-lane RPC rate-limiter + `logsSplit` into the MarketSentinel/RPC layer to survive the public RH RPC 429 behavior, and adopt the Pons V2 phase/factory + curve-reserve pricing logic as an RH-4663 edge (price/graduation signal before a pool exists). The deployer-memory + `diffSnapshots` threshold alerts are portable to Memeland's research/risk notes.
- **Costs:** It is a research/dossier UI, not a trading engine: no position, risk, or consensus logic to borrow. Curve pricing is RH Pons-specific and needs porting to other chains' bonding curves. localStorage + browser architecture isn't directly reusable in the bot runtime. Low complexity overall; only viem as a dependency.
- **Verdict:** Adopt the two-lane RPC throttle, `logsSplit`, and Pons phase/curve-pricing patterns as portable RH-4663 edge code that also reduces GMGN coupling.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No secrets (public RPC, DexScreener, GitHub API); hostile-input sanitization and share redaction handled in test suite; low complexity, verdict-ready.

### SRC-039 0xwast3/PELLET (JavaScript) - RH dormant-wallet wake terminal, ordered risk walls + provider isolation
- **What it is:** A single-process CLI/TUI for Robinhood Chain that tracks a pool of wallets, detects dormant ("sleeping") wallets waking, and pushes candidates through a five-wall decision box. Market discovery uses DexScreener; the tracked wallet pool comes from a bootstrap seed or a live fomo.family leaderboard; chain reads hit the public RPC. Outputs human "pellets" (wallet records with trails) and Markdown, all written to a small JSON state dir.
- **Key pattern:** `src/core/walls.mjs` is a dependency-free, pure decision chain: ordered walls `SLEEP/EDGE/SIZE/DEPTH/PRICE`, first-fail short-circuit, and an explicit `UNKNOWN` state that refuses (never coerces missing data to 0); a candidate either `CAST`s or `PASS_OVER`s with the failing wall named. The same module is byte-identical in CLI and website (a build step + test guards drift), so a verdict can never differ between surfaces. Providers never throw upward: `providers/http.mjs` is the only fetch site, enforces a hard timeout, and every provider returns a result object with an `error` field; the market provider returns `[]` on outage so the universe keeps last marks. A per-token flow window tracks net USD plus the distinct set of wallets and ticket count, so one wallet cycling a position does not look like conviction. State is written atomically (tmp file + rename).
- **For Memeland:** The walls pattern maps almost 1:1 to Memeland's layered risk gates and 7-voter swarm: adopt an ordered, first-fail gatechain with explicit `UNKNOWN` refusal and a named failing gate, plus a single auditable pure decision module shared by bot and any review/CLI surface. Adopt the provider-isolation contract (one http wrapper, hard timeout, no retry, result-object-with-error, stale-mark fallback) as the de-coupling pattern to swap GMGN for other sources, and the distinct-wallet flow window as an input to liquidity/bot-risk voting.
- **Costs:** Research/follow tool, not a trader: no execution, sizing to liquidation, or slippage logic. The walls are wallet-following heuristics (DNA, dormancy) that need re-aiming at token-entry risk for Memeland. fomo.family leaderboard is a paid-ish hosted data dependency, but it is optional behind a bootstrap fallback. Low complexity, single Node process, only Node stdlib deps.
- **Verdict:** Adopt the ordered first-fail risk-gatechain with `UNKNOWN` refusal and the throw-free provider contract; both are low-coupling, chain-portable, and directly reduce GMGN dependence.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Secrets only in env vars (`FOMO_BEARER_TOKEN`, `REPLYNODES_API_KEY`), none in repo; decision box is pure and unit-tested; verdict-ready.

### SRC-044 a-guard/malicious-validators (Python) - Solana sandwich/MEV validator analysis data scripts
- **What it is:** A one-off research pipeline that identifies Solana validators whose leader slots coincide with sandwich attacks. It scans transactions from a known sandwicher, filters for Raydium swaps with Jito tip payments, groups same-slot transactions with opposing balance flows on the same mint into "sandwiches", and cross-references leader slots with validator stake. Outputs are static JSON data files and a handful of standalone Python scripts.
- **Key pattern:** The only transferable idea is sandwich detection itself: within one slot, two Raydium trades by the same actor that bracket a victim trade (net positive post-balance on the same pair) signal a sandwich, so a slot leader can be implicated. Detection is done by grouping balance-flow deltas per mint per slot.
- **For Memeland:** Weak fit. Memeland trades Solana/BSC/Base/ETH and could theoretically use "was this token just sandwiched" as a bot-risk vote input, but this code is single-chain (Solana Raydium + Jito), tied to leader-schedule data, and written as a crude one-off filter with no library, no tests, and no reusable primitives. The MEV-sandwich idea is better sourced from a maintained DEX/MEV library than from this analysis script.
- **Costs:** Hardcoded addresses, no config, no packaging, single RPC session with naive retry; porting the sandwich heuristic would mean rewriting from scratch against Raydium and chain-specific leader data. Not portable to RH-4663.
- **Verdict:** Skip one-off single-chain MEV data analysis with no portable, tested algorithm.
- **Security/complexity flags:** RPC URL via env `RPC`; no secrets committed; not verdict-worthy code (analysis artifact).

### SRC-048 AI-PIN/ChainPlusTrader (TypeScript) - multi-chain DEX trading platform (ETH/Base/BNB/SOL) with per-network retry + V3 pool discovery
- **What it is:** A full-stack automated trading platform: React/shadcn client, Express server, and a `server/dex/*` layer of DEX adapters (Uniswap V2/V3, PancakeSwap, Jupiter) over Web3.js and Solana. A bot scheduler runs cron-driven trades per chain, a trading service validates network/token/gas before executing, and a price service memoizes CoinGecko prices with hardcoded fallbacks.
- **Key pattern:** Two concrete, small patterns stand out. `server/dex/uniswapV3Client.ts` discovers the best pool by iterating the Uniswap V3 fee tiers (0.01/0.05/0.3/1%) and quoting each via QuoterV2 until it finds the highest `amountOut`, then computes `minAmountOut` in BigInt from the slippage tolerance and executes `SwapRouter02.exactInputSingle`. `server/utils/retry.ts` is a network-aware exponential backoff that matches retryable error strings (`429`, `rate limit`, `ECONNRESET`, `ETIMEDOUT`) and uses a more aggressive policy set specifically for Base's strict free-RPC limits. `tradingService` also does a `maxGasRatio` pre-check (gas USD vs trade value) before any swap, and logs each trade through pending/success/failed states.
- **For Memeland:** The Uniswap V3 fee-tier exploration loop ports directly to Memeland's Base/ETH execution and fill-simulation: chain-portable EVM code that needs no GMGN and only viem/QuoterV2-equivalent calls. The per-network retry tuning (string-classified transient errors, stricter Base budget) is a clean Adapt for the RPC failover/rate-limit layer. The status-tracked trade log and gas-ratio gate are minimal but useful consistency checks. The rest of the platform (UI, cron bot scheduler, WebSocket broadcaster, Coingecko fallback prices) is not an edge.
- **Costs:** The surrounding app is a heavy monolithic platform with a huge shadcn UI and Web3.js plumbing; only the two `server/` utilities are worth extracting. It signs transactions in-process from `PRIVATE_KEY_*` env vars (server-side key custody), and ships no meaningful tests. Jupiter/Pancake adapters lean on hosted APIs.
- **Verdict:** Adapt the Uniswap V3 fee-tier pool discovery and the per-network, error-classified retry backoff; skip the monolithic platform.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Private keys loaded in server process (key-custody risk); no tests; web3-based and heterogeneous across chains; extract only the small utilities, verdict-ready.

### SRC-047 AgriciDaniel/claude-obsidian (Python + skills) - local-first Obsidian/Claude knowledge-graph PKM system
- **What it is:** A personal knowledge management (PKM) system that turns source material into linked, source-cited Obsidian Markdown pages; it captures immutable content-addressed copies, grounds claims in ledgers with confidence/review state, links pages, and retrieves grounded answers via BM25/rerank scripts. Includes a release-gates runner, atomic transaction writes, and lots of Agent Skill definitions for capture/lint/query.
- **Key pattern:** Two generic ideas: (1) a declared release-gate runner (`gates.py`) where gates are declared in a product-contract JSON as manual-review (with required evidence) or command gates, returning an `ok`/`ready` summary; and (2) content-addressed capture plus source/claim ledgers that track authority, freshness, support, contradiction and confidence.
- **For Memeland:** Weak trading fit. The declared manual+command gate pattern is only a conceptual echo of Memeland's layered risk gates (which PELLET's ordered walls already cover far better). The claimed-evidence ledger is nice for Memeland's research notes but is not an edge and is heavily tied to Obsidian, Claude Code/Agent Skills, and a large Python plugin surface. Nothing here is a market or execution algorithm.
- **Costs:** Large, store-agnostic Python tool with 200+ files, skills, scripts and config contracts; importing any of it into the TS bot would mean reimplementing a PKM layer that Memeland's decentralized-dossier research flow does not need.
- **Verdict:** Skip no trading, market, or execution logic; an Obsidian/Claude knowledge-graph tool whose only transferable idea is broad PKM/ledger organization.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Local-first, no committed secrets, no network trading surface; complexity is high relative to any possible trading value.

### SRC-054 andreysuperiorgit/aegis (JavaScript/Node) - multi-chain smart sniper with EVM scam scanner + Grok AI risk analysis
- **What it is:** A JS automated "smart sniper" that monitors new launches on Solana (Pump.fun), RH-4663 (Pons V2), and Base (Clanker), runs chain-specific scam scanners, weights the results into a 0-100 safety score with tiered verdicts, optionally passes the scan to Grok (OpenAI-compatible) for a structured BUY/AVOID/CAUTION recommendation, and fires a sniper when thresholds pass.
- **Key pattern:** Two portable patterns. The RH-4663 scanner (src/aegis/scanners/robinhood.scanner.js) is pure EVM fact-gathering: reads token name/symbol/supply/decimals, checks owner() for ZeroAddress (mint authority revoked), bytecode-scans for dangerous selectors (pause/unpause/blacklist/mint/burn), estimates top-holder concentration from known addresses (owner, PONS_V2_FACTORY, PONS_V2_LOCKER, PONS_V2_GRADUATION), detects same-block bundle launches by counting >=5 Transfer logs in one block, and checks LP lock via the PonsV2LaunchLocker balance. src/aegis/utils/score.js is a weighted 0-100 score (mintAuthority 20, freezeAuthority 15, topHolderConc 20, bundleDetected 20, lpStatus 15, metadataFlags 10) with interpolated holder-concentration decay, tiered SAFE/CAUTION/WARNING/DANGER verdicts, and a "second brain" memory adjustment. The RH monitor uses eth_subscribe on the Pons V2 factory for new-token detection.
- **For Memeland:** The RH-4663 scanner maps almost 1:1 onto Memeland's security/rug-risk voter on EVM chains: it is chain-portable (structured for other EVM chains, no GMGN), and the bytecode selector check, ZeroAddress owner check, same-block bundle detection, and weighted tiered scoring are exactly the kind of on-chain risk gates Memeland wants to port. The weighted scoring + tiered verdicts aligns with Memeland's layered risk gates and calibration style. The Grok risk layer is an optional AI vote that could mirror Memeland's LLM/market-sentinel advisory pattern.
- **Costs:** Uses ethers (Memeland is viem); bytecode pause detection is crude and needs polish; a few hardcoded factory/locker addresses would need config; the Grok integration adds an external API coupling Memeland may not want. Sniping/execution side is per-chain and not the transferable part. Some files cut off mid-read; treat specifics as directionally accurate.
- **Verdict:** Adopt the RH/EVM scanner (ZeroAddress owner, selector scan, bundle detection, weighted tiered scoring) into the security/rug-risk voter; skip the Grok dependency and sniper plumbing.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Uses XAI_API_KEY env for optional Grok; in-process signer custody for sniper; bytecode scanning is heuristic (not full verification); the EVM scanner logic itself is clean and verdict-ready.

### SRC-055 Argona7/stampede (Python) - RH rotation radar, calibrated verdicts, risk sizing, and honest paper ledger
- **What it is:** A Robinhood Chain read-only explorer that detects wallet rotations, classifies token sell-to-buy sequences, turns radar features into ENTER/WAIT/AVOID decisions, sizes positions, and replays the exact policy through a paper-trading ledger. It uses direct JSON-RPC plus optional HyperSync data rather than GMGN.
- **Key pattern:** rotation.py grades direct, clean, and ambiguous sell-to-buy sequences and weights token edges by distinct wallets using a sliding time window and prefix counts. verdict.py combines calibrated rule cells or an optional classifier with hard avoids for stale launches, snipe windows, farms, insider/dev sells, and non-ETH quote. risk.py applies fractional Kelly, volatility scaling, per-trade/bankroll/concurrency caps, a daily stop, and exit-now triggers. rpc.py has provider isolation, endpoint cooldowns, public-RPC pacing, Alchemy log-range chunking, batching, redacted errors, and per-host latency/error accounting. engine/paper.py fills at the first block after a signal, models curve fees/tax/snipe tax and impact, applies TP/stop/trailing/time/inflow-death exits, and stores fills and equity for expectancy, drawdown, and bootstrap statistics.
- **For Memeland:** Adapt the rotation graph as a GMGN-independent smart-money voter feature: count distinct wallets and favor direct/clean transitions while keeping ambiguous edges out of the primary score. Adapt the ordered hard-avoid cells and Kelly-plus-volatility sizing into RiskManager, retaining Memeland calibration and the 80% consensus gate. Adopt the RPC mechanics for RH-4663 and other EVM chains: bounded provider failover, range-aware log chunking, batches, cooldowns, redacted errors, and honest coverage metrics. Study the paper ledger's next-block latency and reserve-based fill simulation as a validation oracle for Engine V2 and fill-simulation.
- **Costs:** Python/SQLite and RH-specific curve assumptions require a TypeScript/viem port and chain adapters; the classifier and calibrated cells need Memeland's own data and walk-forward validation. HyperSync/Alchemy remain external dependencies, and the public endpoint is slow/rate-limited. Do not copy the whole explorer or the optional pickle model.
- **Verdict:** Adopt the portable RPC failover/accounting and distinct-wallet rotation weighting; Adapt the calibrated hard walls, Kelly/volatility sizing, and next-block paper ledger into existing Memeland risk and simulation layers.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Read-only by default and API keys are redacted from errors/logs; optional Alchemy/HyperSync credentials and SQLite persistence need secret hygiene; classifier/policy thresholds are overfit risks and require calibration before live use.

### SRC-057 asavinov/intelligent-trading-bot (Python) - column-oriented ML feature engineering, extremum labeling, threshold signals
- **What it is:** A framework that turns OHLCV data into a column-oriented feature matrix, generates top/bottom extremum labels with configurable tolerance, trains classifiers (gradient boosting, linear, NN, SVC) per label, converts point-wise ML scores into buy/sell signals via threshold rules, and backtests trades by simulating buy/sell signal alternation.
- **Key pattern:** Three concrete ideas. (1) Extremum labeling in common/gen_labels_topbot.py: find one or more local tops/bottoms in a rolling interval and label them true only within a tolerance fraction of the extreme, generating many label variants per horizon for anti-overfit evaluation. (2) Score combination and thresholds in common/gen_signals.py: pair buy and sell scores and combine them into a single [-1,+1] trade score (relative or difference), then generate buy/sell/do-nothing signals from threshold rules on score and/or slope. (3) Interval-based precision in find_interval_precision: aggregate point-wise score/label pairs into per-interval scores under an "all or none" principle and grid-search level/tolerance/threshold/aggregation to pick calibrated cut-offs.
