- **For Memeland:** The tolerance-based extremum labeling and interval-aggregated precision are a portable anti-overfit calibration recipe Memeland can reuse to tune its voters and the 80% consensus gate, independent of any chain or market-data source. The buy/sell score-combination into one [-1,+1] score is a clean pattern for combining the seven voter signals into a single confidence to gate positions. Signal rules that require both a score level and a slope threshold echo Memeland's layered risk gates.
- **Costs:** Pure pandas/sklearn research pipeline on bar data, not live trading; no crypto-specific or memecoin insight, and none of the RH/sniper mechanics apply. Porting means rewriting the pandas idioms as TypeScript research utilities. The generic backtest simulates simple alternation, not fills or fees.
- **Verdict:** Study the extremum-label + interval-precision methodology for Memeland's calibration/anti-overfit work, and the buy/sell score combination for voter fusion; do not adopt the framework.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No secrets, no network trading; pure offline research code. Verdict-ready as a methodology reference only.

### SRC-060 Benita2001/SpecterAI (Python/TypeScript) - Solana wallet-consensus and zombie-token paper agent
- **What it is:** A FastAPI plus Next.js paper-trading demo built around eight Birdeye REST endpoints and Claude. It tracks profitable early buyers, excludes simple bot-like wallets, fires when several tracked wallets buy within a time window, and separately hunts dormant tokens with a volume spike and holder-profile checks.
- **Key pattern:** The useful logic is the wallet database and consensus window: promote early buyers from successful tokens, filter identical-amount/new wallets, then require multiple proven wallets to buy the same token inside a bounded interval. The zombie strategy combines dormancy, volume-spike ratio, smart-money entry, and a bundler-count hard wall. Both use staged exits and explainable activity logs.
- **For Memeland:** Adapt the distinct-wallet consensus and bot-exclusion features into the smart-money voter, but use Memeland's chain-neutral transfer/event data rather than Birdeye. Dormancy-to-volume-revival is a possible secondary regime signal for long-tail tokens. The staged exits are already covered by Engine V2; the activity explanation can improve voter traceability.
- **Costs:** Strong Birdeye/Solana coupling, Claude dependence, Telegram/UI surface, mutable global state, paper-only execution, and very thin validation. Its README advertises editable natural-language strategies but the actual backend is a small demo, not a robust strategy compiler.
- **Verdict:** Adapt the bounded multi-wallet consensus and bot-filter features; skip Birdeye, Claude, dashboard, and the zombie agent as a complete subsystem.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Birdeye and Anthropic keys are environment variables, but the server-side agent and Telegram integrations widen secret exposure; no live signer is needed because execution is paper-only. Single-chain hosted API coupling is verdict-limiting.

### SRC-067 build23w/fdv.lol (JavaScript) - client-side SOL memecoin radar with a multi-policy sell engine
- **What it is:** A huge browser client that scores SOL memecoins into GOOD/WATCH/SHILL, runs trader (auto), sniper, hold, follow and bot agents, and centralizes exits in an auto-addon sell pipeline made of many small, composable policies that each can set or mutate the sell decision.
- **Key pattern:** The sell policies under src/vista/addons/auto/lib/sell/policies are concrete and reusable. dynamicHardStop computes a trailing drawdown stop against the all-time high-water mark, triggers only after a remorse/min-hold window, floors it at the configured SL in the first seconds, and never undercuts SL. profitLock arms at a net-PnL threshold, sells a fixed harvest, then keeps a floor = max(BE cushion, peak*retain) and sells all when breached. earlyFade watches 5m change and pumpScore slope, fires on a sustained negative slope or a big momentum drop, but holds through "jiggle" reversals. rugPumpDrop forces a sell and blacklists the mint for 30m on high-severity rug signals, else stages a soft flag. reboundGate defers soft sells when it predicts a rebound and wakes a re-evaluation. volatilityGuard suppresses observer/pump-drop exits during a post-buy cooldown or warming hold. rpcThrottle adds stress-aware jittered backoff on 429/403 across the client.
- **For Memeland:** These are exact fits for Engine V2 and RiskManager exit orchestration, and they are chain-agnostic tuples of (age, HWM, PnL, momentum slope, regime flags) that port to every chain Memeland trades. The HWM trailing hard-stop with SL floor, profit-lock trailing floor, and the momentum-fade-with-jiggle-filter are worth Adapting into Memeland exit policies. The composable decompose/sell-decide/transit policy shape is a clean alternative to a monolithic exit routine, and the rug force-sell-plus-mint-blacklist pattern strengthens the kill-switch response.
- **Costs:** Huge browser monolith with ads, SHILL rank manipulation, an LLM "Gary" agent, encrypted CSVs and onboarding; largely single-chain Solana and UI plumbing. Only the sell-policy files are worth extracting, and they must be rewritten in TypeScript/viem. Some policies depend on an embedded "gate intensity" model that is not obviously portable or validated.
- **Verdict:** Adapt the HWM trailing hard-stop, profit-lock floor, momentum-fade, and rug blacklist exit policies; skip the rank/shill, LLM agent, and browser dashboard.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Contains client-side RPC handling and encrypted/decryptable data (shill CSVs, ads), plus LLM agent keys; heavily obfuscated/minified vendor code; risk of carrying failure-silent try/catch around all policies. Verdict-ready only for the isolated exit-policy patterns.

### SRC-069 chainbase-labs/manuscript-core (Rust/Go) - omnichain data streaming language and Flink runtime
- **What it is:** Chainbase's 914-file "Manuscript" framework: a data-streaming language plus a Flink-backed runtime, CLI, and network fabric to move on-chain and off-chain data into target stores (S3, BigQuery, Postgres, object storage) for querying and analysis across any chain.
- **Key pattern:** The only recognizable patterns are generic data-engineering ones: a declarative streaming language, Flink job pipelines, and a Go/Rust CLI for deploying/managing those jobs. Nothing here is a market, risk, execution, or scoring algorithm; it is a storage/ETL layer for the Chainbase data network.
- **For Memeland:** Little transferable value. Memeland already has the risk service and needs to reduce coupling, not add a heavyweight streaming pipeline. A Rust/Flink stack would fight Memeland's TypeScript/ESM architecture and RH-4663-centric sourcing, and adopting it introduces a heavy external data network as a dependency, the opposite of low coupling. At most, its RPC-to-object-store trick is a pattern Memeland already covers with lighter tooling.
- **Costs:** Very heavy: Rust, Go, Apache Flink, a gRPC/HTTP data network, and 900+ files. Requires a full cluster to run, huge complexity, and is tightly bound to Chainbase's hosted network rather than a portable trading edge.
- **Verdict:** Skip heavy monolithic chainbase data-streaming framework with no transferable trading or risk algorithm; rejects Memeland's low-complexity, chain-portable goals.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Large multi-language attack surface and dependency footprint; no secrets in the inspected surface but huge operational complexity and an external hosted-data coupling; verdict-ready as a skip.

### SRC-075 ctubio/Krypto-trading-bot (C++) - self-hosted low-latency market-making bot with fair-value and EWMA trend-position model
- **What it is:** "K" is a family of C++ low-latency market-making bots (trading-bot, scaling-bot, stable--bot) with a web UI, SQLite server-less persistence, and a high-frequency quoting engine that places/cancels orders on centralized exchanges in milliseconds. It includes ping/pong grid quaters, fair-value estimation from the BBO, and an auto-target-position model.
- **Key pattern:** src/bin/trading-bot/trading-bot.data.h builds a fair-value series and runs a ladder of EWMAs over it: VeryLong/Long/Medium/Short/ExtraShort/UltraShort plus a protection EWMA on fair value and on an average spread width. It derives mgEwmaTrendDiff between UltraShort and ExtraShort and, in AutoPositionMode::EWMA_LMS, computes a target inventory from the SMA3-vs-Long trend plus the Short-vs-Medium EWMA crossing, scaled by an sensitivity percentage. Pairs with a SQLite VectorBackup store that persists and reloads the 96h fair-value history, and a strict Decimal price/amount precision model.
- **For Memeland:** The multi-timescale EWMA fair-value smoothing and the trend-difference auto-position (SMA3 vs Long, Short vs Medium crossing) are a concrete, chain-neutral trend/momentum feature that could feed the momentum or trend voter for marking fair value and calibrating target size. The SQLite VectorBackup persistence and explicit tick/Decimal precision are clean engineering references for Memeland's state handling and amount rounding. The ping/pong quoting and rapid order lifecycle apply to centralized exchanges, not to RH-4663 DEX sniping.
- **Costs:** Heavy C++ engine, tight coupling to centralized-exchange REST/WebSocket APIs, a large web UI, and significant build surface; porting the few EWMA/trend formulas to TypeScript is the only sane reuse. Order-book market-making dynamics do not map to Memeland's memecoin DEX execution.
- **Verdict:** Study the EWMA-of-EWMA fair-value smoothing and trend-diff auto-position as possible voter/sizing inputs; skip the C++ market-making engine for Memeland.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No committed secrets found; a large native build surface and centralized-exchange key handling would require hardening; verdict-ready as a Study reference for the EWMA/trend math only.

### SRC-082 dragon1086/prism-insight (Python) - AI stock analysis/trading system with a deterministic regime-scoped entry gate
- **What it is:** A monolithic 1500-file AI stock research and trading system (LLM agents, signal archive, simulators, web UI) for the Korean/US markets. The market-relevant module is cores/buy_gate.py, a deterministic, market-independent underwriting check run immediately before any new entry across the simulators.
- **Key pattern:** The buy gate encodes a per-regime rule table REGIME_RULES mapping regime (parabolic, strong/moderate bull, sideways, moderate/moderate-strong bear) to min_score, reward/risk floor, max loss percent, min momentum, and min confirmation counts. It fail-closes on a missing programmatic regime ("data outage must not silently remove the market-risk filter"), steps the effective regime down one notch when distribution-days >= 6, checks score, momentum and confirmation floors, validates target/stop placement, recomputes R/R from target/stop and flags any arithmetic mismatch against the reported R/R, and raises a hard finding if the stop width sits below a volatility noise floor (0.5 x max(ATR20, ADR20)). Findings are split into hard vs shadow, and "allowed" is simply no hard findings. The LLM supplies thesis and judgement but the gate is deterministic.
- **For Memeland:** The regime-scoped rule table, fail-closed-on-missing-data principle, missing-score/regime refusal, distribution caution that downgrades the regime, and the recompute-and-verify of R/R arithmetic are chain-neutral and map directly onto Memeland's layered risk gates and 80% consensus wall. The volatility noise-floor stop check (don't place a stop inside normal ATR noise) is a small, concrete sizing/stop improvement for memecoin vol. Hard-vs-shadow finding separation gives Memeland a "shadow mode" to evaluate a stricter gate before enabling it.
- **Costs:** The repo is a heavy monolithic platform with a ChatGPT OAuth proxy, archives, simulators, and proprietary licensing; the single transferable file is cores/buy_gate.py plus the entry-score policy. Importing the framework wholesale would fight Memeland's TypeScript/vitest, low-coupling goals. Stock quotes/regimes do not map to memecoin taps, so only the gate logic port applies.
- **Verdict:** Adapt the deterministic regime-scoped entry gate pattern (fail-closed, floor tables, R/R recompute, vol noise-floor stops, hard-vs-shadow) into Memeland's risk layer; skip the monolithic AI stock system.
- **Security/complexity flags:** The broader repo risks exposure via an OAuth/ChatGPT proxy and many LLM keys; no secrets found in the inspected gate files. Only the self-contained buy_gate logic is verdict-ready for reuse, and it must be re-validated for memecoins.

### SRC-090 Erfaniaa/financial-dataset-generator (Python) - small ML dataset generator with forward-window labels and augmentation
- **What it is:** A compact, no-DB Python tool that downloads daily quotes from Nasdaq Data Link and yfinance, builds a candle-window feature matrix (raw closes plus optional SMAs), generates a binary target from a future forecast window, splits into train/test/predict, and writes CSVs for offline ML model training.
- **Key pattern:** Two small ideas. The target label compares today's close to the linear weighted moving average (WMA) of the next forecast days rather than a single raw future point, smoothing the label. remove_intersected_rows strides the training rows so windows do not overlap by stride = candles + forecast_days, reducing label leakage and autocorrelation between adjacent samples; add_noise_to_dataset multiplies each feature by bounded random noise to augment a small train set.
- **For Memeland:** Only the label-generation and de-correlation ideas transfer: using a smoothed future-window (WMA) target and striding training rows to cut adjacent-sample overlap map directly onto Memeland's calibration/anti-overfit work for its voters. The rest (daily stock quotes, Nasdaq/yfinance, SMAs, CSV output) has no memecoin or RH-4663 relevance.
- **Costs:** Requires the daily-price domain Memeland does not use; simplistic ATR-less indicators and naive CSV plumbing; the yfinance/Nasdaq sources are stock/macro, not on-chain. No backtest or live-trade logic. The API key is read from a checked-in API_KEY file.
- **Verdict:** Study the forward-WMA label and the strided train-row de-correlation for calibration; skip the generator as a tool.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Includes an empty tracked API_KEY file placeholder (0 bytes) and reads the key from disk at runtime; no committed secret value, but the pattern is a secret-hygiene flag. Verdict-ready as a methodology-only reference.

### SRC-092 Erfaniaa/undervalued-crypto-finder (Python) - mean-reversion screen of cryptos below their long moving average
- **What it is:** A small scanner that pulls daily crypto quotes from yfinance, computes a long moving average (default MA200), and flags coins whose last close is below it as "undervalued", plus lists the most pumped/dumped coins and a short-vs-long golden/death cross label.
- **Key pattern:** A basic mean-reversion cross: is_crypto_undervalued compares last close to the window mean and returns the percent discount; detect_golden_cross_or_death_cross compares a short average against the long average. Supporting stats are simple intraday percent fields (close-to-open, high-to-low) averaged over the window.
- **For Memeland:** No transferable edge. The MA-reversion idea is commodity and the dataset is daily stock/macro via yfinance, with no memecoin, on-chain, RH-4663, or execution mechanics. Memeland already has far stronger momentum/mean-reversion candidates through its curve and rotation features, and this adds no calibration, risk, or backtest value.
- **Costs:** Naive pandas loop over hundreds of Yahoo symbols with requests_html screen-scraping of the Yahoo crypto table; fragile and slow, tied to daily OHLCV that does not match Memeland tap dynamics. No risk gates, no fills, no tests.
- **Verdict:** Skip a trivial single-indicator daily MA screen with no concrete, testable trading algorithm for Memeland's context.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No secrets or key handling; reads public Yahoo data; no network or wallet surface. Verdict-ready as a skip.

### SRC-093 EthanAlgoX/LLM-TradeBot (Python/TypeScript) - multi-agent LLM trading system with risk override/downgrade and profile calibration
- **What it is:** A 1386-file multi-agent AI trading system for equities: LLM agents produce analyses and decision signals, an execution loop trades, and a risk layer can veto or downgrade buy/hold/sell signals. It includes a backtest engine, strategy/portfolio management, and a "profile calibration" system that buckets decision-signal outcomes.
- **Key pattern:** src/agent/risk_override.py defines a canonical BUY/HOLD/SELL signal, a RiskTrigger of risk_veto or risk_downgrade, and only allows downgrade transitions (BUY->HOLD, HOLD->SELL) with a number of steps; an override plan that downgrades or vetoes is then applied with an exhaustive, auditable reason (e.g. post_risk_signal_already_within_risk_limit, risk_veto_applied). docs/decision-signals.md describes per-bucket calibration: each profile bucket needs at least 30 completed samples before reporting hit_rate, avg return, miss_rate and max adverse excursion; under-filled buckets are labeled "insufficient sample" rather than producing numbers. src/phase_decision_guardrail.py adds market-phase and language-aware guardrails around when an action may be taken.
- **For Memeland:** The veto/downgrade model is a clean, chain-neutral risk override pattern Memeland can layer on top of the 80% consensus: keep raw_score/adjusted_score/raw_action/final_action plus an auditable reason, and allow only conservative transitions. The per-bucket min-sample (30) calibration discipline and max-adverse-excursion reporting align directly with Memeland's calibration and anti-overfit goals for its voters. Phase-aware guardrails map to Memeland's trade-window/regime filtering.
- **Costs:** The repository is a very heavy monolith (multi-agent, backtest engine, broker integrations, web apps, Docker, many docs); porting is best limited to the risk-override and calibration concepts. It is equity-oriented (Futu brokers, stock phases) and has no memecoin or RH-4663 mechanics. LLM-driven strategies are non-deterministic unless guardrailed as this repo does.
- **Verdict:** Adapt the veto/downgrade risk-override-with-audit-reason and the per-bucket min-sample calibration into Memeland's risk gates and voter calibration; skip the multi-agent monolith.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Large surface with broker integration, auth middleware and many env-driven keys; only the risk-override and calibration logic are reusable and must be re-implemented in TypeScript. Verdict-ready for those two patterns.

### SRC-095 Fincept-Corporation/FinceptTerminal (C++20 / Qt6 + embedded Python) - finance analytics desktop terminal with paid SaaS
- **What it is:** A 792MB closed-ish C++20/Qt6 desktop terminal for equity/macro market research, portfolio, news, a node editor and data feeds, with an embedded Python 3.11 analytics core. The free AGPL build is a research/analytics UI; live broker routing, algo deployment, agent teams and deeper data are gated to the paid Fincept Enterprise and Quantcept SaaS instead of this repo. This is a bundled Bloomberg-style desktop workstation, not a lightweight trading bot.
- **Key pattern:** No bot runtime, no memecoin or on-chain trading mechanic in the open build; its value is in the (mostly SaaS-gated) quant/backtest/portfolio engine and data connectivity, not a portable algorithm. Backtesting and live algo execution are explicitly commercial features, so they are not in the readable codebase.
- **For Memeland:** Minimal transfer value. The node-editor / data-connectivity demo is UI chrome; there is no RH-4663, no Solana/BSC/Base handling, no GMGN-integrated edge, and no chain-portable TS/Python algorithm to lift. Memeland is better off with its own lightweight market-data layer.
- **Costs:** Enormous compile/runtime footprint (792MB repo, Qt6 desktop, embedded Python); C++/Qt desktop architecture does not fit Memeland's Node ESM swarm; hosted paid APIs (Fincept/Quantcept) are exactly the single-chain/paid-service coupling Memeland wants to avoid.
- **Verdict:** Skip heavy Qt desktop research monolith whose trading/backtest value is SaaS-gated; nothing portable for a memecoin swarm bot.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Bulk of the repo is proprietary/binary and SaaS-gated; reading it adds heavy monolith complexity with no portable edge. Verdict-ready.
### SRC-096 FinStep-AI/ContestTrade (Python) - multi-agent equity trading with internal contest: forward-outcome rewards and sharpe-weighted voter allocation
- **What it is:** An LLM multi-agent trading system for CN/US equities where several data-analysis agents and research agents generate factors/signals, and contests (evaluator + predictor) reward each agent by the realized forward move of what it flagged, then select top-k agents or re-weight them before a signal is acted on. The research side uses a signal judger: multiple independent LLM judges score each researcher signal starting from 100 and only deducting, with each judge given the researcher recent track record.
- **Key pattern:** (1) Forward-outcome reward label: for every factor/observation, agents emit a rating in -2..2 (direction + confidence), then the evaluator computes `rating * capped_realized_change` (cap +-20%) against the next-day open-to-open move and stores it as the agent reward. (2) SearchSharer/ResearchWeightOptimizer.optimize_weights_by_sharpe: each agent gets a predicted Sharpe ratio; positive-Sharpe agents get weight proportional to Sharpe, Sharpe<=0 agents get zero weight (fail-closed), total normalized to 1. (3) ResearchSignalJudger: multiple judges each run the same critique prompt (start 100, only deduct: logic, evidence, risk awareness, data use, historical performance) and each signal gets a list of judge scores; historical 5-day avg returns are injected into the judge prompt to weight credibility.
- **For Memeland:** Directly replaces/augments the flat >=80% consensus gate with reward-weighted voter allocation: keep per-voter calibration, compute a predicted (Sharpe-like) score per voter, give only positive-score voters weight, and require a weighted-sum threshold. The `rating * realized_return` forward reward and the only-deduct judge scoring are clean, chain-neutral, GMGN-independent calibration/critique patterns Memeland already partially has (calibration anchor); the judge ensemble is a check-for-deduction meta-voter that pairs well with the swarm. Historical-track-record injection into judges maps to Memeland calibration.
- **Costs:** Equity/LLM-centric (akshare/sina/polygon webs, LightGBM predictors, CN A-share specifics, many third-party data utils) - none of the data layer ports to RH-4663; only the contest/evaluator/judge abstractions transfer and must be rewritten in TypeScript. Reward labeling needs a forward price to settle (same-day candle or next tick for memecoins) since it assumes a next-day open.
- **Verdict:** Adopt the forward-outcome reward label plus predicted-Sharpe-weighted voter allocation and only-deduct multi-judge critique as Memeland calibration and consensus-weights machinery.
- **Security/complexity flags:** LLM api keys read from python config files (plaintext-ish); loads third-party equity APIs and cached data; no crypto/wallet surface. Only the re-implemented contest math is useful. Verdict-ready.
### SRC-097 flash131307/multi-agent-investment (Python) - deterministic signal-fusion Decision Hub with consistency gates, no LLM in critical path
- **What it is:** A multi-agent equity research system where 3 specialized agents (technical, sentiment, fundamental) each emit a BUY/NEUTRAL/SELL signal with strength and confidence, then a pure mathematical Decision Hub (no LLM in the decision path) fuses them into one direction, confidence, and risk mode. The hub has three stages - consistency scoring, dynamic weight allocation, and weighted aggregation - and degrades gracefully as agents drop out. Agents run in parallel with per-agent timeouts via asyncio.gather.
- **Key pattern:** (1) Direction-gate consistency lookup tables: for each (n_buy, n_neutral, n_sell) count tuple a precomputed gate score encodes agreement AND conflict caution, e.g. 3-way split=0.1, 2BUY+1SELL (majority wins)=0.3 but 1BUY+2SELL (minority BUY)=0.0, all-NEUTRAL=0.5; then consistency = direction^1.5 * (0.6*strength_agree + 0.4*confidence_agree), clamped. (2) Risk-mode dampening: consistency thresholds select NORMAL(1.0)/CAUTIOUS(0.8)/RISK(0.6) multipliers applied to final confidence. (3) Dynamic weights = base_weight * regime_modifier * confidence, normalized to sum 1 (regime-specific modifier tables, e.g. technical boosted in trending markets). (4) Aggregation: score = sum(direction_numeric * strength * weight); direction from +/-0.25 thresholds with NEUTRAL band; confidence = weighted avg * risk multiplier. (5) Degradation: 3 agents x1.0, 2 x0.8, 1 x0.5 confidence dampening; 0 -> hard fail. All decision structures are frozen pydantic models.
- **For Memeland:** This is the missing mathematical core for Memeland's >=80% consensus gate. Memeland can lift the direction-gate lookup (asymmetric conflict caution - a dissenting voter among a majority direction should suppress, not just count), risk-mode dampening, regime-modifier-weighted voters, and degradation multipliers. Critically, it shows how to keep the decision path deterministic (no LLM/GMGN in the fuse step) with LLM reasoning only as a non-blocking post-hoc explanation - exactly Memeland's layered risk-gates + sizing goal. Fully chain-portable TS (RH-4663 safe, no GMGN dependency).
- **Costs:** Repo is equity-focused with heavy data integrations (Finnhub, SEC EDGAR RAG/Qdrant, FinBERT sentiment, Adanos, Yahoo) that do not port; only the decision_hub (consistency/weighting/aggregation + models) transfers and must be re-implemented in TypeScript. Gate tables need Memeland's 7-voter counts and thresholds re-derived rather than copied.
- **Verdict:** Adopt the consistency-gate lookup, risk-mode dampening, regime-weighted voters, degradation multipliers, and deterministic no-LLM fusion path as Memeland consensus/risk-gate machinery.
- **Security/complexity flags:** API keys (OpenAI, Finnhub, Adanos, Qdrant) in .env via pydantic-settings; SEC EDGAR user-agent and external LLM calls; no wallet/crypto surface. Only the decision-hub math is reusable. Verdict-ready.
### SRC-106 gustaffsonKotte/qlo (Python) - pump.fun "slow graduation" scanner: time-on-curve as organic-demand filter
- **What it is:** A Solana-only pump.fun bonding-curve scanner. A Helius webhook streams pool creations on the PumpSwap AMM; ~3/4 of the stream (non-graduations) is dropped locally with no API calls; for each real graduation the bot pages the token signature history back to its first tx to measure how long it spent on the curve, stops paging as soon as it crosses the age threshold (early-stop), and if older than a configurable min_age_hours it alerts to Telegram (optionally membership-gated). Outcome tracking (15m/1h/6h/24h) and a real migration/liquidity/socials check are listed as live-only additions, not in the committed code.
- **Key pattern:** The core claim is an evidence-backed filter: measured over 97,146 graduations (6 months, holdout-validated) tokens that took hours to fill their curve were ~1.5x more likely to double and ~2.4x more likely to 5x after graduation, with the effect strengthening as the threshold rises - "fast fill = bots, slow fill = organic buyers." Technically it uses getSignaturesForAddress paging with an early-stop cutoff (old tokens cost 1-2 RPC calls instead of 12) and local program-account filtering before any remote call. The README also documents a real PumpSwap pricing bug: virtual_quote_reserves is non-zero on most pools despite docs, so correct price = quote_vault_balance + Pool.virtual_quote_reserves (signed i128 at bytes 245..261), avoiding 1.3x-15x mispricing.
- **For Memeland:** The slow-graduation "time-to-full-fill = organic demand proxy" filter is a concrete, chain-portable-concept edge Memeland can study for its curve-based tokens (Solana/BSC/Base); it pairs naturally with Memeland's curve-math and calibration anchors - feed the time-on-curve feature into a voter and forward-outcome-track it exactly as qlo measures outcomes post-launch. The early-stop pagination and local-pre-filter-before-API-call are cheap credit-saving patterns Memeland can adopt in its GMGN/chain fetchers. The raw-pool-account pricing check ("read the actual pool state, don't trust ABI/doc defaults") is a general best practice for Memeland pricing across chains including RH-4663.
- **Costs:** Repo is a single-chain (Solana/Helius/pump.fun) Telegram alert bot with no trading/entry engine; the specific AMM does not exist on RH-4663, so only the concept ports and must be re-validated on Memeland chains (no external RH-4663 data exists to confirm the edge). Outcome tracking that would prove the filter is not in the committed code.
- **Verdict:** Study the time-on-curve organic-demand filter (evidence-backed, chain-portable-concept) and adopt the early-stop pagination + raw-pool-state pricing verification; skip the single-chain alert-bot shell.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Config holds helius_key and Telegram bot token in a plaintext local config.json (gitignored); the RPC URL embeds the API key in the query string; no hardcoded secrets in the repo. Verdict-ready for the two portable patterns.
### SRC-107 h100envy/nerve (Python) - seven-node memecoin protocol for Robinhood Chain Uniswap V3 with SENTINEL round-trip simulation, reflexes, and reconcile
- **What it is:** A memecoin trading protocol whose first production adapter targets Uniswap V3 on Robinhood Chain (chain id 4663 - Memeland's own chain). Seven typed agents (SCANNER, SENTINEL, ANALYST, RISK, EXECUTOR, MONITOR, REPORTER) pass one pydantic Impulse through a deterministic spine; a reflex layer of cheap deterministic checks runs before every expensive or side-effecting node; a SQLite store persists impulses, transitions and execution intents so a restart is fully recoverable. A pure `nerve_score` (0-100) is deterministic, and paper mode needs no RPC, key or model.
- **Key pattern:** (1) SENTINEL "simulate the exit before the entry": a read-only `eth_simulateV1` (multi-call eth_call) round trip from the real WALLET_ADDRESS - fund WETH, approve, quote, buy, then sell in the next simulated block - to prove the wallet can exit, detect honeypots, and measure buy/sell tax from Quoter amountOut vs token/WETH balance deltas (rounded up, never flattering). Replayed buys that do not reproduce the same delivery are discarded as non-deterministic; sell_route false -> score 0, fail closed. (2) Bytecode scan (`eth_getCode` + EIP-1967 impl) for PUSH4 selectors of blacklist/max-wallet/tax-setter/trading-toggle/pause/whitelist/transfer-hook setters -> `bytecode:*` risk flags that feed the score (the model can add flags, never erase them). (3) Exactly-once execution: `_send_once` signs locally, never retries eth_sendRawTransaction (a timeout does not prove absence); RPC timeout -> `unknown` terminal state carrying nonce + local hash; `nerve reconcile` (read-only, by nonce) resolves unknown intents and verifies open positions against on-chain balance before any restart of the live loop. (4) RetryingHTTPProvider retries reads 3x with backoff but never the broadcast; chainread JSON-RPC client refuses any method outside an allowlist of READ_METHODS so a bug cannot turn simulation into a tx. (5) Reflexes (kill-switch file, daily loss, gas cap, buy/sell tax caps, stale simulation by pinned-block age, min liquidity, slippage, min score, duplicate, max positions) fire ordered ahead of ANALYST/RISK/EXECUTOR and trip even if SENTINEL never ran. (6) All facts pinned to one block number so simulation, bytecode and enrichment see identical state, with a freshness reflex (SENTINEL_MAX_AGE_BLOCKS=30 ~ 3s on RH 100ms blocks). (7) NerveNode subclasses must declare node_type/owns/boundary at class-definition time.
- **For Memeland:** The single most on-target source: it is RH-4663 native, GMGN-independent (reads the chain directly), and maps onto Memeland's already-adopt anchors (fill-simulation, execution governance, txlock/fast-submit, layered risk gates + sizing). It adds three concrete things Memeland can adopt: SENTINEL wallet-round-trip simulation for honeypot/tax detection via eth_simulateV1, the bytecode PUSH4 risk-flag scan, and the reconcile-by-nonce exactly-once send with `unknown` as a terminal state plus a read-only recovery CLI. The owns/boundary node contract, fail-closed scored gates (sell_route false = score 0), pinned-block staleness, and reflexes-before-expensive-nodes ordering are all portable TypeScript patterns that strengthen Memeland's consensus + risk-gate stack.
- **Costs:** Python/web3.py code that must be re-implemented in TypeScript/ethers; some ideas (eth_simulateV1 multi-block state, bytecode scan, reconcile) are EVM-specific and port to Solana/BSC/Base only conceptually. The repo is intentionally "one opinion fused into the spine" (strategy interface is only planned), so Memeland would lift the sentinel/reflex/reconcile machinery rather than any alpha. Live path is explicitly unproven against mainnet; RH public RPC is rate-limited so SENTINEL needs a simulateV1-capable provider.
- **Verdict:** Adopt SENTINEL eth_simulateV1 round-trip honeypot/tax detection, the bytecode PUSH4 risk-flag scan, and reconcile-by-nonce exactly-once send; plus owns/boundary node discipline, fail-closed scored gates and pinned-block staleness for the swarm. RH-4663 native, no GMGN dependency.
- **Security/complexity flags:** PRIVATE_KEY is env-only, held in a separate signer process and explicitly never placed on an Impulse; Alchemy key in RPC URL; the signer refuses to leak nonce/hash; design is deliberately fail-closed and read-only by construction. No hardcoded secrets in repo. Verdict-ready.
### SRC-110 HKUSTDial/DeepEar (Python) - LLM deep-research + financial signal tracking with ISQ multi-dimension signal scoring
- **What it is:** An open-source framework (both a skill and an app) for deep research and financial signal tracking: LLM agents (trend/fin/forecast/intent/report) scan CN/equity news, score signals with an "ISQ" (Investment Signal Quality) template, produce research reports, and optionally run a custom PyTorch "Kronos" time-series model with a news-embedding projection layer to forecast price moves conditioned on news. It ships a dashboard, a trained model file (.pt), RAG/hybrid search, and sentiment tooling.
- **Key pattern:** (1) ISQ scoring: each signal is scored across named dimensions - sentiment (-1..1), confidence (0..1), intensity (1..5, scale factor 20), timeliness (0..1), expectation_gap (0..1) - with per-template dimension weights and a weighted-average aggregation (config/risk.json: confidence 0.35, intensity 0.30, timeliness 0.20, expectation_gap 0.10, sentiment 0.05) and a scoring guide (e.g. "risk-first: confidence > intensity > timeliness > expectation_gap > sentiment"). (2) Dual-model agent split: a cheaper tool model does data collection (scanner), a stronger reasoning model does value judgment (evaluator), so tool calls are not in the reasoning model's critical path. (3) News-aware forecast evaluation: base forecast vs news-embedded forecast are compared by MAE and "improvement" = (base_mae - news_mae)/base_mae to test whether a news embedding actually changes the forecast - an honest null/ablation discipline.
- **For Memeland:** The ISQ framework is a clean, portable, chain-neutral way to score each swarm voter signal (and each token signal) on a bounded set of named dimensions with explicit weights - Memeland can adapt it as a structured per-voter signal-quality schema (confidence, intensity/timeliness, expectation_gap) feeding its calibration, rather than opaque LLM prose. The dual-model scanner/evaluator split (cheap data-collection model vs reasoning model, never in the same call) and the "does adding this input change the forecast/score" ablation metric both map to Memeland's anti-overfit and calibration goals.
- **Costs:** The repo is a heavy LLM research monolith (dashboard, custom PyTorch Kronos model with committed .pt weights, RAG, hybrid search, sentiment tooling) all tuned to CN A-share equity/news - no on-chain, memecoin or RH-4663 mechanic, and the LLM/news pipeline does not port. Only the ISQ scoring schema and the scanner/evaluator separation and ablation-metric concepts transfer as TypeScript. Expectation_gap is hard to source for memecoins; it would need a proxy.
- **Verdict:** Study the ISQ weighted multi-dimension signal-quality scoring schema and the dual-model scanner/evaluator + news-ablation evaluation discipline; skip the heavy LLM research/dashboard/monolith.
- **Security/complexity flags:** LLM API keys via .env; a trained model checkpoint and large data files are committed to the repo; no wallet/crypto surface. Not verdict-ready beyond the two portable patterns.
### SRC-114 Im-Madhur-Gupta/maverick (TypeScript, NestJS) - meme-coin AI trading agent with structured LLM trading signals and a signal pipeline
- **What it is:** A NestJS/TypeScript "create your AI trading agent for meme coins" app that combines onchain holdings with social alpha (Farcaster posts) and an LLM to produce typed trading signals, then executes them via an external Fere agent API. A typed pipeline (fetch social posts -> generate trading signal -> process trading signal) runs per coin; a Prisma/Postgres store persists agents, holdings and processed signals; wallet-signature auth and a Next.js dashboard wrap it.
- **Key pattern:** (1) A structured Zod trading-signal schema: type = BUY/SELL/HOLD, strength = WEAK/MODERATE/STRONG, percentage = 0..100 (SELL = percent of current holdings to sell, BUY = percent to add, HOLD = 0), produced via a typed LLM structured-output call. (2) A two-dimensional action gate: `shouldProcessSignal` only executes when type != HOLD AND strength == STRONG - direction plus confidence tier must both pass before any order is sized. (3) Percentage-based partial sizing: a SELL sizes `amountBought * percentage/100` per holding, enabling tapered partial exits rather than all-or-nothing, with batched execution and a cooldown sleep between batches to respect an external API rate limit. (4) A reusable typed pipeline-step abstraction with explicit input/output generics and a validateExecuteParams guard before each step.
- **For Memeland:** The typed signal schema (direction + strength tier + fraction-to-trade) is a clean, portable TypeScript contract Memeland can reuse for its voter verdicts and position-sizing: replace the free-form consensus output with an explicit direction/strength/percentage tuple and gate execution on strength tier in addition to the >=80% gate. The percentage-based partial-sizing (sell a fraction of the position, taper) is a lightweight sizing addition that fits Memeland's layered risk-gates + sizing anchor and is fully chain-portable and GMGN-independent.
- **Costs:** The repo couples decision to a single social source (Farcaster) and to a hosted Fere agent-execution API (paid, single-chain style) - Memeland should skip both couplings. Decision is LLM-driven and non-deterministic with no calibration loop. Notably the process step has an acknowledged duplicate-order hazard (BullMQ retries re-send every instruction even if only some failed, and BUY is an unimplemented TODO) - the opposite of Memeland's exactly-once send goal, so Memeland should NOT copy this execution path. No backtest or on-chain risk gates.
- **Verdict:** Study the structured direction/strength/percentage signal schema, the strength-tier gate, and percentage-based partial sizing; skip the Farcaster-social + Fere-API pipeline and the retry-with-duplicates execution path.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Hosted Fere API key and LLM keys via env; wallet-signature auth with nonce/signature utils; acknowledged duplicate-order risk on retry is an anti-pattern to avoid. Verdict-ready for the schema/gate/sizing patterns only.
### SRC-115 immortalhowwl/fly-high (Python) - causal evolutionary paper-trading lab for RH chain: next-close fills, depth-capped fitness, cold holdout
- **What it is:** A local, inspectable evolutionary paper-trading lab ("natural selection for trading strategies on Robinhood Chain"). A bounded strategy genome (lookback, momentum, min_liquidity, stop, take-profit, holding bars, allocation) competes on training data; elites survive, offspring mutate 1-3 genes, a random immigrant preserves exploration, and a frozen champion faces an untouched chronological holdout. It replays authentic saved RH-chain token candles (COPY/HOPOUT/GPTHEIST, 351 5m OHLCV) with explicit execution-liquidity assumptions, records provenance (SHA-256 hash chain of birth/extinction/generation/freeze/holdout events), and ships an offline browser replay with no keys, no wallet and no live orders.
- **Key pattern:** A rigorous causal simulator in `simulate()` (engine.py): signals queue as "pending" and fill only on a later observed close with slippage applied (fill = price * (1 +/- slippage)); any missing-observation gap over max_gap cancels the pending fill with no interpolation; momentum entries require a contiguous signal window (no trading across holes). Cost model is conservative: 0.3% fee + 0.2% adverse slippage each side; buy notional = min(fee-adjusted allocation, 1% of execution liquidity) with a min-liquidity gate; exits are partial and obey the same 1% depth cap, residual inventory stays at risk. Equity for fitness is depth-capped: `equity = cash + min(inventory_value, liquidity*0.01)` so inventory beyond one-observation exit depth earns zero fitness credit (a separate mark_to_market_equity reports the full value). Fitness = `return - 1.5*max_drawdown - 0.0001*number_of_fills` (penalizes drawdown and overtrading). Training uses a fixed 70/30 train/cold-holdout split (by observation count), requires >=80 observations or evolution refuses, and the report loudly labels the champion "zero return means inactivity, not alpha" - explicitly refusing to claim alpha from sparse data.
- **For Memeland:** A directly usable anti-overfit + fill-simulation methodology (both are already Memeland anchors; this is the disciplinary core to adopt). Memeland can adopt the causal next-close fill simulator (gap-cancelling pending fills, no intrabar path, no interpolation), the depth-capped equity/fitness valuation (do not credit exit value beyond what one observation of depth can absorb - a real memecoin problem), conservative fee+slippage, and the cold-start holdout with drawdown-penalized fitness for validating/calibrating voters and entry/exit parameters on recorded tapes. The data source is GeckoTerminal candles for RH tokens but the engine is data-agnostic and chain-portable (TS rewrite), with zero GMGN coupling - it also fits Memeland's plan to record and replay its own tapes.
- **Costs:** Python engine that must be re-implemented in TypeScript; the checked-in dataset is tiny/biased (highest-liquidity-at-collection selection, GPTheist under the 80-observation floor so evolution on it intentionally fails) and explicitly not a survivorship-free universe; execution liquidity is an assumption, not measured depth; no gas/MEV/spread history/tax/malicious-token modeling (stated). Evolve() has no sure-payment RNG for random-policy baselines beyond a stable seed.
- **Verdict:** Adopt the causal next-close simulator (gap-cancelling fills, depth-capped equity/fitness, conservative fee+slippage) and the cold-out holdout + drawdown-penalized fitness search as Memeland's strategy/voter validation discipline. Data-agnostic, chain-portable, no GMGN dependency.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No secrets, no wallet, no keys; pure stdlib Python; public HTTP server binding 0.0.0.0 with strict Host allowlist and same-origin 405 rejection if made public. The discipline of labeling assumptions is a strong model for honest reporting. Verdict-ready.
### SRC-120 JulienPlanchetCoineo/frostybot-js (JavaScript) - CEX/tradingview webhook order-execution gateway with target-position sizing
- **What it is:** A NodeJS API/HTTP gateway (REST + TradingView webhook + CLI) that turns trading commands into orders on CEX futures/spot (Binance, FTX, Deribit, Bitmex, BinanceUS -> ~612 files with DB, GUI, encryption, Docker). Six commands: long, short, buy, sell, close, cancel; USD/base/quote sizing, percentage closings, layered/relative prices, stop/take-profit bundling. Also ships a signal-provider manager with IP whitelists and per-user account/config routing for multi-tenant webhook feeds.
- **Key pattern:** The trade module's target-position sizing math: it reads the live CEX position (dir+signed size), converts any relative/maxsize/scale input, then computes a *target* position per command (long -> target=|requested|, short -> -abs(requested), buy/sell -> current +/- requested, close -> current +/- partial) and rejects or clamps orders via precedence rules (maxsize cap, would-flip detection, "size exceeds current" guard, close-all). Orders are funneled through a per-request FIFO queue (`mod.queue.js`) that sequentially submits and returns success/fail per order.
- **For Memeland:** Nothing on-chain or RH-4663-specific; it is pure CEX exchange plumbing with no alpha, no gas/fee/gas-spike/RPC logic, and the actual signal source is an external TradingView webhook. The forced-target + would-flip + maxsize-clamp sizing semantics are mildly Study-worthy as a compact spec for position-target sizing, but Memeland already has fill-simulation and layered risk-gate sizing anchors, so there's no net-new edge.
- **Costs:** Pulling any pattern in would mean porting a CEX-centric order model to an on-chain EVM context where positions and fills are fundamentally different (no native long/short, no exchange position API); the repo's DB/GUI/encryption/webhook multi-tenancy layers are heavy and off-target.
- **Verdict:** Skip CEX-only webhook-to-exchange gateway plumbing; position-target sizing is only marginally related and Memeland already covers sizing with on-chain semantics.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Sizable install-heavy codebase (DB, GUI, encryption module); default-credential SSH/PASS env noted in README; not chain-portable to RH-4663. Verdict-ready.
### SRC-121 kabbersokhi-boop/crypto-trend-hunter (JavaScript/Python) - Reddit-sentiment to memecoin correlation pipeline with deterministic scoring
- **What it is:** A small multi-agent pipeline (32 files): Agent1 (`sentiment.py`) classifies Reddit posts via GPT-4o-mini into topic/sentiment_score(-1..1)/label/confidence; Agent2 (`trends.py`) aggregates mention counts and computes a deterministic momentum score per topic; Agent3 (`memecoin.py`) pulls top memecoins from CoinGecko, correlates sentiment vs 24h price change, and labels each coin HOT/COOLING/NEUTRAL. Node backend + React frontend and an n8n workflow glue it into a dashboard; SQLite persistence for posts/sentiment/trends/prices/correlations.
- **Key pattern:** `calculate_momentum` = 70% mention-share (topic mentions / max mentions) + 30% sentiment-strength (abs(avg_sentiment)), capped to 0..1 — a cheap deterministic social-signal fusion. `calculate_correlation_score` = avg_sentiment * normalized-price-change + min(mentions/100, 0.2) boost, clamped to -1..1. `predict_coin_status` is a small threshold state machine (sentiment>0.2 AND (price>5% OR corr>0.3) -> HOT; sentiment<-0.1 OR (price<-10% AND corr<0) -> COOLING). Pipeline runner wraps each agent in its own try/except so one failure never aborts the rest.
- **For Memeland:** The momentum weighting (mention-share + sentiment-strength) and the HOT/COOLING threshold state machine are portable deterministic patterns that could shape how a social/sentiment voter slot scores candidates, and the per-agent try/except isolation maps cleanly to making the 7-voter swarm fail-open per slot. But Memeland has no social-data source on RH-4663 and the whole pipeline leans on GPT-4o-mini sentiment + CoinGecko; there is no on-chain, gas, RPC, or calibration content.
- **Costs:** Sentiment is LLM-based and noisy, CoinGecko gives no RH-4663 coverage; adopting the correlation/sentiment layer wholesale would add a new data dependency with weak grounding against Memeland's actual candidates. Only the deterministic fusion math and per-agent isolation are cheaply transferable.
- **Verdict:** Skip README/dashboard-driven LLM-sentiment pipeline; only the deterministic momentum formula and per-agent fault-isolation are mildly Study-worthy, neither is a Memeland-specific edge.
- **Security/complexity flags:** OpenAI key read from env; no secrets committed; includes a committed `trends.db`. Verdict-ready.
### SRC-122 Kelows/million (TypeScript, NestJS) - Solana whale/copy-trading deck with risk gauntlet, consensus, decision log, control-group calibration
- **What it is:** A self-hosted Solana memecoin intelligence dashboard + copy-trading engine (165 files, api+web+shared monorepo). It watches a whale roster, discovers wallets via tokens they buy, scores candidate tokens through a parallel "risk gauntlet" (TokenCheckService), computes cross-wallet co-entry consensus, maintains a trailing-stop/ledger position model, and lets an AI coding agent drive setup/explanation via Claude skills. Data sources are Solana-only (Helius, Jupiter, DexScreener, RugCheck).
- **Key pattern:** (1) TokenCheckService: a layered gate checklist with statuses pass/warn/fail/unknown; immutable on-chain facts (mint/freeze authority, token program) are cached as **one-way doors** — once observed safe they are reused verbatim and never refetched; volatile checks (rug, deployer) reuse on a TTL; check chains run CONCURRENTLY so total latency = slowest chain. (2) Holder-distribution rule: top-10 aggregate line with a "largest-holder" grace when distributed, and a dev-owned top bag judged by size (<=3% pass / 3-8% warn / >8% fail). (3) Deployer-history serial-rugger detection (>=5 launches, >=80% dead -> fail). (4) Round-trip sell simulation with loss thresholds (0.1 SOL in and back out; >30% fail, >12% warn). (5) LP-lock thresholds (>=80% pass, >=50% warn). (6) ConsensusService: co-entry counted per **owner** (ownerId), not per address, so one actor's five wallets = one confirmation. (7) ConvictionService: snapshots ranked cohort + random **control** group and marks both to market at +6/24/72h — "without a control a rising market makes every cohort look clairvoyant". (8) DecisionLog: persisted structured stage/outcome/code/reason rows answering "why didn't it buy X", with retention pruning and a never-throws guarantee (observability must not break the pipeline). (9) Ledger.ts: average-cost basis where proceeds from tokens never seen bought are tracked as `unbackedSol` and kept OUT of realized PnL (kills phantom 5.7k SOL profit).
- **For Memeland:** Directly maps to the layered risk gates + calibration anchors. Adopt the one-way-door caching of immutable on-chain facts and concurrent parallel check chains to cut pre-trade latency. Adopt the dev-bag-by-size + distributed-top-10 grace rule into the liquidity/ownership voter. Adopt owner-id de-duplication so multi-wallet actors can't inflate the >=80% consensus. Adopt the control-group cohort evaluation (benchmark signals vs random tokens at fixed horizons) for the calibration anchor, and the structured decision-log-as-ledger so every gated rejection is auditable. Ledger unbackedProceeds->non-profit is a good honesty guard for any PnL telemetry.
- **Costs:** All data services are Solana-specific (Helius/Jupiter/DexScreener/RugCheck) with no RH-4663 or EVM/gas support; porting means re-implementing each check against Memeland's RH RPC + a rug-sim equivalent. The full NestJS/Prisma/web monorepo is heavy — take the check-list + decision-log + consensus de-dup patterns, not the stack.
- **Verdict:** Adopt the chain-portable risk-gate structure (one-way-door caching, concurrent check chains, distribution grace, owner de-dup consensus, control-group calibration, decision ledger); Solana data adapters are not portable.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No secrets committed (env-based Helius key); self-hosted local tool, `misc` experimental disclaimer. Verdict-ready.
### SRC-123 kocer6/MEERKAT (TypeScript, viem) - RH-4663 native Pons V2 token lifecycle + wallet intelligence, evidence-first and fail-closed
- **What it is:** A 167-file TypeScript terminal/dashboard ("Meerkat") built directly against Robinhood Chain (chainId 4663) RPC via viem: indexes the Pons V2 factory launch log, reconstructs curve buy/sell/transfer histories per token from launch to head, builds wallet dossiers and a relationship graph, and scores tokens/wallets from evidence. Read-only indexer/observer: "evidence-first, no signer."
- **Key pattern:** (1) `entryReasons`: a granular entry gate that returns EVERY applicable rejection reason at once (not first-fail) and splits outcomes into matched / rejected / **unreadable** — "unreadable data must block entry exactly like a failing filter, never treated as passed or defaulted to a safe value; this is the fix for audit finding A1: a failed tax read must not be silently substituted with 0." Same principle in `reserveDrop` (null = missing evidence, never zero). (2) `scoreToken`/`scoreWallet`: composable named score components (Deployer exposure 25 / Creator tax 20 / Participant breadth 25 / Two-sided market 15 / Holder breadth 15) each carrying an `evidence` string; the token score is **withheld until launch-to-head indexing coverage is 100%**, and an indexing error surfaces as status:'error' instead of a wrong score. (3) `IndexAdmission`: per-client admission control with maxActive/maxQueued/rate-per-window, returning 429 + RetryAfter rather than overloading the RPC. (4) `PonsDiscovery`: bounded 1999-block backfill with a 63-block replacement overlap for reorgs, an RPC-independence `factory.getLaunchedToken` ABI-record cross-check against each launch event to catch reorg discontinuities, persisted cursor + explicit chainId 4663 guard, and credentials scrubbed (`http/https` replaced with `[RPC endpoint]`) from any surfaced error. (5) `exitReason` deterministic exit precedence (stop-loss -> trailing -> max-hold -> take-profit) and `validateRules`/`assertRules` runtime validation of opened-from-JSON rules (bigint gas, bps ranges).
- **For Memeland:** Strongest and most directly on-target audit yet because it is native RH-4663. Adopt the unreadable-as-fail gate discipline (failed reads must block, never default 0) into Memeland's layered risk gates and MarketSentinel, matching the nerve sentinel fail-closed stance. Adopt the evidence-string-carrying named score components and the withhold-until-coverage-complete rule so the smart-money/liquidity voters never emit a score on partial data. Adopt IndexAdmission concurrency/rate control for the scanner/RPC layer and the reorg-overlap plus ABI-record cross-check for Memeland's discovery/backfill.
- **Costs:** Everything is RH-4663/Pons-V2 specific, so the discovery/curve/ABI code is directly usable only for the Pons layer; generalizing the gate and scoring structure to other chains Memeland trades still needs per-chain adapters. No execution/gas-optimization content (read-only project), so it complements rather than covers the exec side.
- **Verdict:** Adopt the fail-closed unreadable-gate discipline, evidence-carrying composable scoring with coverage-gated readiness, and per-client admission control + reorg-overlap scanner checks; all map directly to Memeland's risk gates and RH-4663 data layer.
- **Security/complexity flags:** RPC-credential scrubbing in error paths is a good practice to mirror; `no signer` so no signing-risk surface; moderately large codebase but pattern surfaces are small and clean. Verdict-ready.
### SRC-133 liangdabiao/autogen-financial-analysis (Python) - enterprise AutoGen multi-agent equity analysis with VaR/ES risk models and phased orchestration
- **What it is:** A 98-file "enterprise-grade" AutoGen-based financial analysis system for equities: multi-source data (Yahoo Finance, Alpha Vantage, Quandl), multi-agent AutoGen collaboration for analysis, risk assessment (Value-at-Risk, stress tests, Monte Carlo), quant (factor models, portfolio optimization, ML forecasting), plus a web API, Docker, k8s, Prometheus monitoring and a data validation layer.
- **Key pattern:** `risk_models.py` implements three VaR methods — historical (percentile of returns series), parametric (normal z-score + expected shortfall via norm pdf), and Monte Carlo (simulations) — each returning a VaRResult with confidence/horizon/expected-shortfall, plus a threshold table that buckets each metric (var_95_1d, beta, max_drawdown, volatility, sharpe, concentration, liquidity) into LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH/CRITICAL levels. `data_validator.py` is a rule table (positive price, reasonable range, non-zero volume) each tagged error/warning/info. The orchestrator runs a phased comprehensive workflow where each phase is wrapped in its own try/except so one failing phase is recorded as an error without aborting the rest.
- **For Memeland:** The VaR + Expected-Shortfall/CVaR math and the metric->LOW/MED/HIGH/CRITICAL threshold buckets are concrete, chain-portable risk algorithms that could feed Memeland's position-sizing and layered risk gates (tail-risk-aware sizing on short-horizon memecoins, where CVaR matters more than VaR). The data-validation rule table (severity-tagged) is a lightweight way to formalize sanity checks on GMGN quotes/prices. The phased try/except orchestration is a weaker echo of the per-slot fault-isolation already adopted from million/trend-hunter.
- **Costs:** Heavy enterprise equity monolith (AutoGen LLM agent framework, web API, Docker/k8s/Prometheus, scipy/sklearn) with equities-only data sources (Yahoo/AlphaVantage/Quandl); none of it is on-chain, gas, or RH-4663 aware. Only the ~5 VaR/ES functions and the threshold-bucket table are realistically portable, and VaR needs a returns series that Memeland's per-launch lifecycle data only partially provides.
- **Verdict:** Study the historical/parametric/Monte-Carlo VaR + Expected-Shortfall functions and the LOW/MED/HIGH/CRITICAL threshold-bucket pattern as a tail-risk sizing input; Skip the AutoGen/equity monolith around them.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Standard env-based API keys; heavy dependency surface (scipy, sklearn, AutoGen, FastAPI, orchestration services); no secrets committed. Verdict-ready.
### SRC-138 LuckyOne7777/LLM-Trading-Lab (Python) - real-money LLM-managed micro-cap portfolio lab with FIFO exits and peak-capture evaluation
- **What it is:** A 114-file experiment repo where ChatGPT/DeepSeek/Claude model agents run a real-money micro-cap equity portfolio. It is mostly weekly/daily deep-research artifacts and PDFs, plus the orchestration code: per-model `workflow.py` flows (starting/weekly/daily) that prompt a model for a report, parse an `ORDERS_JSON` block out of the report with `parse_json`, run it through `filter_orders` (with rejected orders saved to a rejections store), then submit. Analysis scripts build FIFO lot-level realized exits, per-ticker PnL/holding-days/episode stats, and a Peak Capture Ratio.
- **Key pattern:** `build_fifo_lot_exits` reconstructs realized PnL per trade via a genuine FIFO lot stack (entry lots with cost basis; exits close the oldest lot first, computing realized PnL and holding days). `episode_pcr.py` splits each ticker's hold-time into episodes (in-position runs) and computes **Peak Capture Ratio = exit_pnl / peak_pnl** (the share of the peak unrealized PnL actually captured at exit), nulling episodes where peak <= 0, and ranks by it. The multi-model flow treats each LLM as a trader, filters its parsed orders against runtime validation, and logs rejections rather than silently dropping them.
- **For Memeland:** Peak Capture Ratio is a concrete, chain-portable exit-quality metric that is directly useful for the calibration anchor: it measures how well Memeland's TP/trailing-stop exits capture the peak move per filled position, and Memeland already has the fill-simulation and position lifecycle data to compute it. The FIFO lot-stack accounting is a clean reference implementation for realized-PnL/holding-time telemetry (echoing nerve/million ledger patterns) and the saved-rejections behavior maps to Memeland's decision-log goal. The LLM-in-the-critical-path order flow is an explicit anti-pattern to avoid (Memeland correctly keeps the 80% consensus deterministic); the TODOs even show false rejections from missing market data.
- **Costs:** Everything is equity-market and LLM-report driven (yfinance/Polygon, prompt parsing) with no on-chain/gas/RH-4663 content; Peak Capture Ratio needs an intra-position peak PnL series, which maps to Memeland's simulated fills but not to GMGN quotes directly. Only the metric + FIFO accounting are worth lifting.
- **Verdict:** Study Peak Capture Ratio as an exit-capture calibration metric and the FIFO lot accounting as a PnL telemetry reference; Skip the LLM-managed portfolio orchestration (LLM in critical path, equities data).
- **Security/complexity flags:** Env-based API keys; artifacts include real trade data/CSVs of the author's live portfolio; the false-rejection TODOs are a good caution about runtime-missing-data handling. Verdict-ready.
### SRC-139 Lumiwealth/lumibot (Python) - full algo-trading framework: mid+slippage fill model, smart limits, DataSource abstraction
- **What it is:** A mature, professional Python algorithmic-trading framework (1496 files, `lumibot/` package) with 12 brokers (Alpaca, Schwab, IBKR, Tradier, CCXT, Tradovate, Polymarket...), pluggable data sources (Yahoo, Polygon, Databento, CCXT, ThetaData, pandas), a strategy lifecycle, a backtesting engine (`backtesting/backtesting_broker.py`), smart-limit order placement, an options chain model, and agent-driven strategy tooling. Supports stocks/options/futures/forex/crypto.
- **Key pattern:** (1) The backtesting fill model (`expected_fill_price(mid, slippage, side)` = buy mid+slippage / sell mid-slippage), clamped to the bid/ask edge, rounded to an inferred/asset tick size via `round_to_tick` (buy rounds up, sell rounds down), with a per-fill **audit payload** (`fill.model`, `fill.price`, bid/ask/mid, slippage_amount) recorded on every order — a transparent, conservative, inside-the-spread fill simulation. (2) SMART_LIMIT order semantics (`smart_limit_utils.py`): `compute_final_price` parametrizes how far toward the aggressive edge of the spread to go via `final_price_pct` (0=mid, 1=aggressive edge), `build_price_ladder` produces a stepped ladder, `infer_tick_size` infers tick from quote. Missing-quote handling downgrades a smart limit to a market fill rather than blocking. (3) `DataSource(ABC)` with abstract `get_historical_prices`, `get_last_price`, `get_bars`, `get_quote`, `get_spread`, `get_midpoint` — a clean interface that concrete adapters implement, decoupling strategy logic from any single market-data provider.
- **For Memeland:** The conservative `mid +/- slippage`, clamp-to-spread, round-to-tick fill model with a per-fill audit payload is a strong reference for Memeland's existing fill-simulation anchor — the audit-trail-on-every-fill habit (record the exact fill model + quote state) is worth adopting for calibration. The DataSource ABC is the decoupling Memeland explicitly wants for reducing GMGN coupling: define a narrow MarketData Protocol (quote, last price, spread, midpoint, history) and swap GMGN/RH-RPC/DexScreener adapters behind it so voters and MarketSentinel depend on the interface, not GMGN. The `final_price_pct`-toward-aggressive-edge smart-limit math is a mild Study for how Memeland's fast-submit/Nitro could express a configurable aggression parameter.
- **Costs:** Enormous monolithic framework (1496 files, 12 brokers, options/futures/equity/forex, agent tooling) far beyond Memeland's need; CCXT/IBKR/etc. are heavy CEX/equity deps; the backtest engine and order model are built for continuous equities/options auctions, not on-chain memecoin launches (no gas, RPC, or RH-4663 mechanics), so pulling it in wholesale is off-target — only the ~3-4 helper functions and the data-source interface concept are portable.
- **Verdict:** Study the mid+slippage/clamp/round-to-tick fill model with per-fill audit payload and the DataSource ABC as the pattern for a pluggable market-data interface to cut GMGN coupling; Skip the monolithic framework itself.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Large attack/dependency surface; `.secrets` template uses env vars; very heavy install; no on-chain secrets handling relevant to Memeland. Verdict-ready.
### SRC-261 tsukiema1/FLYWHEEL (JavaScript + optional Python) - connectome-gated RH-4663 paper sim: signals propose, pure-code risk decides
- **What it is:** A 26-file paper-trading simulator for Robinhood Chain (chainId 4663, PONS/STOCK tokens) that pipes market momentum and wallet conviction through a "fly-inspired" neural adapter (browser surrogate or an optional local MaleCNS connectome bridge via `flybrain`) to produce a descending-spike proposal, which a deterministic six-check risk gate then accepts or rejects before a stateful $500 PaperBroker records a simulated fill. No signer/broadcaster; fixture radar feeds the loop; tests cover the encoder, risk gate, and engine end-to-end.
- **Key pattern:** (1) Propose-vs-decide separation: "A descending spike is necessary but insufficient. Momentum, flow, and liquidity gates must also pass"; `engine.step()` only buys when `neural.descendingSpike && encoded.rising && encoded.strongFlow && encoded.liquidityGate === 1` AND `risk.passed` — the neural layer can never bypass `config.json`. (2) `evaluateRisk` returns six structured checks `{id,label,passed,observed,limit}`: trade size, **position weight computed on the resulting post-add weight** `(current + amount)/cashStart`, slippage cap, asset-type allowlist, daily-loss drawdown, and a human-approval-above-usd threshold. (3) `encodeMarket` deterministically normalizes noisy signal fields into bounded 0..1 currents (`clamp`), including a **two-sided liquidity band** (must be >= min AND <= max USD) and boolean momentum/conviction/reputation gates. (4) Risk policy + signal thresholds live in versioned `config.json` evaluated by pure code. (5) An append-only in-session decision ledger records a frozen full-snapshot event (signal/encoded/neural/risk/proposal/order/verdict/portfolio) per tick. (6) Explicit trust boundaries and production gaps (independent price source, stale-price handling, fees/latency/failed-quote backtesting, random-network controls) are documented, not hand-waved.
- **For Memeland:** Directly reinforces and refines Memeland's propose-vs-decide architecture: voters/sentinel propose but RiskManager + Engine + kill-switch decide. Adopt the structured six-check risk-gate shape `{id,label,passed,observed,limit}` (it makes every rejection auditable, like the million decision-log) and the resulting-post-add weight sizing for the layered risk gates. Adopt the two-sided liquidity band (min AND max USD window) for memecoin liquidity targeting, and the deterministic clamped signal encoder as a normalization pattern for voter scores. Adopt the append-only full-snapshot decision ledger and the config-driven pure-code policy so neural/voter output can never mutate risk parameters. The connectome neural reservoir itself is experimental and explicitly unproven (needs random-network controls), so treat it as Skip.
- **Costs:** Simulation-only (no gas/execution/RPC calls — the public RPC is noted as rate-limited and unused), so it adds risk-gate/paper-sim patterns rather than execution mechanics; the connectome bridge pulls heavy neural deps for a feature with no demonstrated edge. Code is small and clean but single-asset-type fixtures only.
- **Verdict:** Adopt the propose-vs-decide risk architecture, structured observed/limit risk checks, resulting-weight sizing, two-sided liquidity band, and append-only decision ledger; Skip the biological/connectome signal layer.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No signer/key handling at all (good for a paper lab); fixture addresses are truncated/hardcoded; clear trust-boundary documentation. Verdict-ready.
### SRC-262 zostaff/grok-trading-desk (Python) - fail-closed 13-bot LLM desk: code vetoes, pessimistic fallbacks, cross-market risk sizing, outcome memory
- **What it is:** A two-market LLM trading desk (12-13 Grok agents) trading Solana memecoins via pump.fun WebSocket plus US equities via Alpaca. Ten generators, two adversarial checkers (stronger deep model), plus a pure-code scout and shared risk/allocator/exit/log modules. Ships paper-first, crypto executor is a deliberate NotImplementedError stub. The core design principle: a model that fails is a model that says no - nothing about a failure looks like permission.
- **Key pattern:** (1) Hard vetoes live in pure code before any weighted scoring: coordinated_buys or wash_trading veto a token, pulse.go_signal below 0.3 pauses the whole market, and a parser failure in a veto agent returns the veto=on value. (2) Every LLM agent has a pessimistic fallback: unparseable audit vetoes the buy, broken exit manager returns HOLD, dead allocator returns 50/50. (3) Two-stage scout cost-gating: a pump.fun create event carries no holders/buy/sell/age, so stage-1 screens on what it really carries, stage-2 subscribes to that mint's trades and accumulates real stats over a bounded watch window; only survivors cost a model call. (4) Cross-market RiskManager with one set of counters (daily loss limit shared, open-position cap counts both books), sizing = tightest of market-budget share, remaining-loss-room share, and free budget, scaled 0.5-1.0 by confidence. (5) Outcome memory: joins buy to close, recalls comparable past trades matched on symbol/sector/theme ranked by specificity, plus a per-market summary (win_rate, avg_win/avg_loss, worst_themes); deliberately no vector store. (6) Strict JSON-schema structured outputs, static-first prompts keyed for caching, exact API-tick cost tracking, append-only typed JSONL log carrying every agent score.
- **For Memeland:** Adopt the fail-closed contract wholesale into the consensus gate: veto agents return veto=true on parse failure, exit manager HOLD-on-failure, and code-level vetoes (wash trading, coordinated buys, regime pause) enforced before scoring - this sharpens Memeland's layered risk gates and kill-switch. Adopt the cross-market RiskManager sizing (tightest-of-three-bounds scaled by confidence) as a portable sizing module for all chains Memeland trades. Adapt the two-stage cost-gated scout: only spend expensive GMGN/data or model calls on tokens that pass cheap code filters first - this is a concrete lever to reduce GMGN coupling (this repo uses a live WS feed plus model search instead of a single aggregator). Adapt outcome memory: join the existing ledger buy-close records, compute win_rate/avg_win/avg_loss/worst_themes, and feed comparable past trades back to risk/exit decisions - cheap and explainable. Adapt pulse.go_signal as a per-market regime kill-switch. Study the market allocator as a chain-allocation pattern (crypto vs stocks -> which chains to weight) with code-clamped ceilings and 50/50 fallback.
- **Costs:** Python codebase, Memeland is TS - port the pure-code modules (risk.py, scoring.py, scout stage gates, memory.py, log.py), skip the 13-bot Grok orchestration. The pump.fun WS feed and Alpaca/xAI specifics are single-chain and single-hosted-LLM; only the two-stage-filter and fail-closed shapes transfer. Outcome memory as prompt-injection leans on an LLM, which Memeland wants to reduce - but the join/recall/summary math stands alone. Live event feeds and wallet-graph audit data are real data-engineering work.
- **Verdict:** Adopt Fail-closed vetoes, pessimistic fallbacks, cross-market sizing, and cost-gated two-stage filtering map to real operational edges and port to every chain Memeland trades; also a concrete GMGN-decoupling pattern.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Secrets are gitignored (config.example only) and paper-first with a live-mode CLI flag; crypto signing intentionally not shipped (user writes the executor). Moderate complexity overall (13 bots) but the transferable parts are small pure-code modules. No rank-manipulation; adversarial checkers add cost. Verdict-ready.


## Batch 19 - general GitHub repos deep-read (subagent Einstein)
### SRC-140 lunarresearcher/copy (TypeScript/Node ESM) - CLI-first copy-trading bot with rule walls and webhook executor boundary
- **What it is:** A CLI-first copy-trading bot (terminal/hunt/paper modes) that normalizes a Fomo leaderboard, DEX markets, and Robinhood chain data into candidates, scores them, and mirrors/papers them. Robinhood chain 4663 is first-class (contract-log provider, chainId=4663 health check). Optional HTTP/SSE server path plus an external webhook executor; no bundled signer, non-custodial.
- **Key pattern:** evaluate() returns SKIP/FIRE with a reasons[] array across five market walls (EDGE source score>=58, DEPTH liq/mcap>=1%, TURN vol/liq>=0.45x, MOMO momentum in -15..+38%, PRICE>0) plus exposure walls (maxPositions=5, buySizeEth, sessionBudgetEth). Exit box is TP/SL/trailing/max-hold. Bootstrap seed then merge-by-key (a shorter provider response never shrinks the universe). NativeExecutor POSTs a normalized order {chainId:4663,...} to a webhook with a QUEUED/ARMED/DISPATCHING/SENT/EXECUTED retry state machine; empty webhook stays queue-only.
- **For Memeland:** The wall evaluator with per-wall human-readable refusal reasons strengthens the smart-money/screening layer; exposure+session-budget gates and the exit box mirror Memeland's layered risk gates; the RH-4663 webhook executor boundary and status machine match the execution-governance anchor and keep the signer out of the engine. Bootstrap-seed + merge-by-key is a cheap resilience win for GMGN decoupling (cache last-known-good, merge, never replace).
- **Costs:** The CLI/replay/showcase surface is unnecessary; only rules.mjs semantics and the native-executor boundary are worth porting. Fomo leaderboard is a copy-signal source, not a DEX edge. Tiny dependency footprint, no DB.
- **Verdict:** Study - copy walls and webhook executor state machine are portable and match existing anchors, but need validation against Memeland's current voters before adoption.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Non-custodial by design; secrets (FOMO_BEARER_TOKEN, NATIVE_EXECUTOR_TOKEN) are env-only. README fetch failed over our network, so this review used docs/ARCHITECTURE, docs/SAFETY, docs/STRATEGY plus source. Low complexity.

### SRC-142 lyc0603/copytrading (Python) - WWW'26 multi-agent manipulation-resistant meme copy trading
- **What it is:** Research artifacts (ACM WWW 2026) for detecting manipulative bots in meme-coin copy trading. Fetches PumpFun/Raydium data, computes per-trader t-stats of meme-token returns, classifies traders as winner/loser/neutral at a 99% confidence threshold, and runs a two-LLM-agent (wallet agent + coin agent) chain-of-thought classifier with logprobs-based confidence.
- **Key pattern:** Trader profitability is judged by a one-sample t-statistic of per-token return/cost (threshold abs(t)>2.576) rather than raw PnL - a significance filter that removes noise. Coin-side features are manipulation flags: launch bundle, sniper bot, bump/volume bot, comment bot. LLM agents get CoT + JSON schema, temperature 0, top_logprobs for confidence.
- **For Memeland:** The t-stat significance filter for smart-money profitability is a concrete, chain-agnostic algorithm that could harden Memeland's smart-money voter (score traders by statistical edge, not headline winrate). The explicit bot-manipulation feature set (bundle/sniper/bump/comment) maps directly to the bot-risk voter. Chain-of-thought LLM classification is portable to TS but expensive.
- **Costs:** Snowflake + OpenAI LLM per token/trader pair with 1s sleeps is heavy and external; academic pipeline, no live execution. The value is in the two algorithms (t-stat filter, manipulation features), not the stack. Memeland has no Snowflake and should not add it.
- **Verdict:** Study - the t-stat profitability filter and bot-manipulation feature list are worth copying; the Snowflake+LLM pipeline itself is too heavy.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Needs SNOWFLAKE_USER and OPENAI_API from .env; no hardcoded secrets seen. Research/data pipeline complexity is high relative to the portable signal. Verdict-ready once the two algorithms are extracted.

### SRC-150 milesdeutscher/garchmethod (Python) - GARCH(1,1) walk-forward vol forecast and vol targeting
- **What it is:** A Claude Code skill that fits GARCH(1,1) (t-dist errors) walk-forward on an expanding window with zero lookahead, produces 1-day-ahead vol forecasts, classifies vol regime (calm/normal/storm by trailing-year percentile), and converts forecast vol into a position-size multiplier target_vol/forecast_vol capped at [0.25x,2.0x]. Ships an A/B comparison harness (fixed vs vol-targeted sizing) with Sharpe, max drawdown, worst month.
- **Key pattern:** Volatility is forecast with a rolling GARCH recursion (omega + alpha*eps^2 + beta*sigma2), refit every 21 days, min 500 training days; sizing = target_vol / forecast_vol clipped to [0.25,2.0]; regime from percentile vs 365-day lookback. It deliberately answers how much, never which way.
- **For Memeland:** A concrete, chain-agnostic sizing/risk algorithm that plugs into RiskManager: forecast per-token vol and scale size down in storm regimes instead of flat size. The honest A/B harness is a reusable backtest template for Memeland's anti-overfit goals.
- **Costs:** Python/arch dependency; Memeland would port the ~50 lines to TS (no direct GARCH lib needed - the recursion is explicit). Needs enough history (min 500 days) which may not exist for freshly-launched memes; use with token liquidity/age gating.
- **Verdict:** Adapt - port the walk-forward GARCH(1,1) + vol-target sizing into RiskManager for vol-aware position sizing.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No secrets, no network keys (optional yfinance ticker). Math is explicit and auditable; low complexity. Verdict-ready.

### SRC-164 nirholas/crypto-vision (TypeScript/Hono) - large crypto intelligence monolith with failover, cache and anomaly modules
- **What it is:** A production-grade TypeScript/Hono crypto data platform: 200+ endpoints, 37 source adapters (CoinGecko, DeFiLlama, GMGN, GeckoTerminal, Jupiter, etc.), 58 AI agents, multi-provider AI with automatic fallback, two-tier caching, per-host circuit breaker, anomaly detection, BigQuery warehouse and GCP infra.
