- **Key pattern:** tryMultipleSources() runs a cascading source list behind a per-host circuit breaker (closed/open/half-open, 5-failure threshold, 30s reset, 2 successes to reclose), returns stale-cache data when all sources fail, and tracks degraded routes for a /health endpoint. fetcher.ts adds exponential backoff+jitter, 429 Retry-After respect, per-host concurrency limits and timeout enforcement. anomaly.ts uses modified Z-Score (MAD) + EWMA on sliding windows for 16 anomaly types. cache.ts is two-tier LRU+Redis with single-flight stampede protection and stale-while-revalidate.
- **For Memeland:** The cascading source fallback + per-host circuit breaker is precisely the abstraction to decouple GMGN: put GeckoTerminal/DexScreener/Uniswap-graph behind tryMultipleSources and let the breaker skip dead hosts. Stale-cache fallback plus a degraded-mode health flag keeps the engine alive on source outages. Modified Z-Score/EWMA anomaly detection maps directly to the security/bot-risk voter (price/volume/liquidity spikes).
- **Costs:** The platform is far too heavy (GCP, BigQuery, Redis, 37 sources, 58 agents); only ~4 lib modules (fallback.ts, fetcher.ts, cache.ts, anomaly.ts) are worth porting and each needs trimming. It also has its own GMGN adapter, so it is not GMGN-free. Two-tier cache is overkill for personal use; keep single-flight + stale-while-revalidate subset.
- **Verdict:** Study - extract the circuit-breaker/failover and anomaly-detector modules; skip the platform.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Repo ships solwallets.json and pump-fun-sdk-main.zip (data-leak risk) and a large .env.example with many provider keys. Very high overall complexity; verdict-ready only after isolating the 3-4 modules.

### SRC-191 rimtoln/fletch (vanilla JS) - pump.fun hunt tape with 8-factor token scoring
- **What it is:** A non-custodial pump.fun live hunt tape. Every few seconds it pulls new/trending pump.fun/PumpSwap pools from GeckoTerminal + DexScreener, collapses eight factors into one percent score, and bands them SING>=68, HUM 42-67, MUTE below. Claims a $81k Robinhood Chain ATH.
- **Key pattern:** Clean factor-normalization toolkit: logScore (log-scaled value vs a reference full), clamp, tanh-based transforms for flow bias and impulse, an age sweet-spot curve (too raw and too old both lose), and a weighted sum of tape/book/flow/impulse/crowd/fresh/structure/x-ray with penalties for thin books, sell-side tape, dumps, and exit-risk market cap.
- **For Memeland:** The scoring math (log-scaled factors, clamp, tanh aggregation, weighted sum with penalties) is chain-agnostic and could sharpen the security/liquidity/bot-risk voters. The freshness age-sweet-spot curve is a ready-made gate for any chain including RH-4663.
- **Costs:** Discovery logic is Solana/pump.fun-specific (GeckoTerminal pools, DexScreener social) and does not port; only the scoring functions transfer. No testable evidence beyond a README ATH claim.
- **Verdict:** Study - borrow the factor-normalization and freshness scoring; the pump.fun screener itself is out of scope.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No API keys required; marketing-heavy README (ATH claim is not evidence). Low complexity, single-file scoring, low validation.

### SRC-193 ryan-yuuu/crypto-trading-arena (Python) - LLM agent trading arena on calfkit + Kafka
- **What it is:** A multi-agent crypto trading arena where LLM agents trade live Coinbase/Binance data. Built on the calfkit SDK with a Kafka broker (Docker), paper wallets, per-agent LLM clients, shared trading tools, and a live dashboard.
- **Key pattern:** One exchange connector acts as a market-data proxy that fans a single event stream out to every agent (no replicated fetching). Per-agent model selection lets different voters use different LLMs/providers. Shared tools resolve the calling agent's identity at runtime via ToolContext, and accounts appear dynamically on first trade.
- **For Memeland:** The single-connector fan-out is a clean design for decoupling GMGN: swap one data adapter and all seven voters stay wired to the same stream. ToolContext identity resolution maps to per-voter configuration in Memeland's swarm. Otherwise it is an LLM evaluation harness with no concrete trading edge.
- **Costs:** Requires Kafka/Docker, calfkit SDK, and one LLM per agent; heavy infra for a personal bot. Data is CEX (Binance/Coinbase), not the DEX chains Memeland trades; strategies are prompt-only with no validated edge.
- **Verdict:** Study - the single-connector fan-out and per-voter tool identity are useful for GMGN decoupling, but the harness/infra is too heavy to adopt.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Needs an LLM provider API key; Docker/Kafka footprint. No committed secrets. Medium complexity; verdict-ready for the two design patterns only.

### SRC-152 moazamdotdev/Trading-platform-frontend (Next.js/React) - Solana memecoin swap frontend
- **What it is:** "Azal", a Next.js 15 + React 19 frontend for swapping Solana memecoins. Includes wallet adapter (Phantom/Solflare), trading chart, analytics, whale-watching and alert UI. Solana Web3.js, Tailwind, Zustand, Vercel deploy.
- **Key pattern:** No backend and no trading logic; pure UI with client-side wallet integration and DEX swap surfaces. Components are presentation-only (tables, buy/sell, token info).
- **For Memeland:** Nothing transferable: Memeland already has its own frontend, and there is no strategy, scoring, risk, or data-decoupling algorithm here. The whale-watch/alert surfaces are static UI claims.
- **Costs:** Frontend-only demo with no executable trading path; would require wiring to a backend to be useful at all.
- **Verdict:** Skip - a wallet-connected swap UI with no backend, no edge, and no transferable logic.
- **Security/complexity flags:** NEXT_PUBLIC-adjacent frontend demo; relies on browser wallet integration (not suitable for a server-side bot). Low complexity, zero audit value.

### SRC-155 moorcheh-ai/memanto (Python) - Memory Agent that manages other agents' memories
- **What it is:** Memanto is a "Memory Agent": a companion agent that manages what its other agents keep, resolves contradictions, expires stale entries, and briefs each agent before it acts. Ships as a Python package with agent-framework integrations (CrewAI, LangGraph, AG2, Claude Code, Hermes), lifecycle hooks, and adversarial-memory benchmarks.
- **Key pattern:** Storage is a filing cabinet; Memanto is the chief of staff - it decides what to keep, watches access, resolves what contradicts (two agents believing opposite things), discards what is stale, and briefs each agent before action. Includes explicit conflict-resolution, expiry, and adversarial-resilience scoring.
- **For Memeland:** A shared token-history ledger across the seven voters would let the swarm agree on ground truth: if the security voter flagged a token as honeypot and the liquidity voter later sees it liquid, conflict resolution decides which wins; stale flags expire; each voter is "briefed" with the filtered history before scoring. This is directly relevant to consensus integrity and fits Memeland's swarm architecture.
- **Costs:** Heavy Python package with many integrations (MCP, vector stores, framework adapters); Memeland would only need the memory-lifecycle and conflict-resolution semantics, not the dependency stack. Not a trading edge by itself.
- **Verdict:** Study - the memory-lifecycle/conflict-resolution protocol is a good fit for the voter swarm, but the package is heavy and not trading-specific.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No secrets committed; large dependency/plugin surface (Docker, MCP, vector stores) is overkill for a personal bot. Verdict-ready once the lifecycle/conflict rules are isolated.

### SRC-158 mvanhorn/last30days-skill (Python + MCP) - agent-led search engine plugin
- **What it is:** An AI agent-led search engine plugin for Claude Code / Codex / etc. that searches recent (last-30-days) content across many sources (X, Reddit, TikTok, GitHub, arXiv, web) and scores results by upvotes/likes/engagement rather than editors. A large search/plugin repo with a Go MCP engine, scrapers, and dedupe/freshness logic.
- **Key pattern:** Fan-out search across multiple backends, freshness windowing, entity extraction, dedupe/fusion, and engagement-based relevance scoring. A reusable research/surfacing pipeline.
- **For Memeland:** Out of domain - it surfaces recent web/social research for coding agents, not trading signals. Nothing here maps to an execution edge, risk gate, or data-decoupling algorithm for a trading bot.
- **Costs:** Huge plugin surface (50+ backends, browser CDP, cookies, LLM reranking); would be a whole side-project to integrate and is orthogonal to trading.
- **Verdict:** Skip - a research/search plugin with no trading edge and no transferable trading algorithm.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Large attack surface (browser automation, cookie extraction, many API integrations); very high complexity relative to any Memeland need. README-only relevance.

### SRC-163 nikmcfly/MiroFish-Offline (Python + Neo4j) - local multi-agent social sentiment simulation engine
- **What it is:** A fully-local fork of MiroFish: upload a document (press release, report) and it generates hundreds of AI agents with distinct personalities to simulate public reaction on social media - posts, arguments, opinion shifts - tracking sentiment evolution hour by hour. Uses Neo4j knowledge graph, Ollama LLM/embeddings, Docker.
- **Key pattern:** Graph build (entity/relationship extraction), persona generation (opinion bias, reaction speed, influence), then multi-agent simulation where agents post/reply/argue and shift opinions; a report agent interviews a focus group and searches the graph for evidence. Zero cloud dependencies.
- **For Memeland:** The sentiment-swarm simulation is a candidate for a social/market-sentiment voter: project a token announcement through simulated personas to forecast sentiment direction instead of naive keyword counts. However it is document/offline-oriented, not live market data, so it would need a live feed wrapper.
- **Costs:** Heavy: Neo4j + Ollama + Docker; hundreds of LLM agent calls per run; no live market integration and no validation against real outcomes. Overkill for a personal bot until the sentiment signal is proven.
- **Verdict:** Study - the persona-based sentiment-simulation concept is novel for a sentiment voter, but infra and offline nature make adoption premature.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No cloud API keys required (offline). High complexity (Neo4j, Docker, Ollama); verdict-ready only if a sentiment voter becomes a priority.

### SRC-179 oratis/influencex (Node/Express + React) - KOL marketing automation platform
- **What it is:** A self-hostable KOL marketing platform: discover creators on YouTube/TikTok/Instagram/Twitch (YouTube API, Apify), run outreach pipelines (Resend email), and track campaign ROI (funnels, CPM, cost-per-outcome). Express backend + React SPA, job queue, scheduled follow-ups, RBAC, i18n.
- **Key pattern:** Multi-platform influencer discovery, email-outreach pipeline with drafting/review/approval/send, and an ROI dashboard converting pipeline stages to cost metrics. Production-grade scaffolding (rate limiting, CORS, ETag cache, health checks).
- **For Memeland:** Out of domain. It automates influencer marketing and outreach, not trading. The only tangential idea is treating profitable traders/KOLs as discovery targets, but the platform gives no trading signal, scoring, or data-decoupling algorithm and leans on Apify/Resend/YouTube coupling.
- **Costs:** Full marketing stack (Apify, Resend, GA4, Feishu); substantial surface with no relevance to Memeland's seven trading voters or chain portability.
- **Verdict:** Skip - a KOL marketing platform with no trading edge or transferable algorithm.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Multi-provider API keys (YouTube, Apify, Resend, Hunter); large web surface (auth, RBAC, CORS). High complexity, no Memeland value.

### SRC-194 semkazz1/FlySwarm (JavaScript/Node ESM) - Robinhood-chain wallet-cohort intelligence engine
- **What it is:** A Node 20+ ESM live adapter and swarm engine that watches profitable wallets, tracks continuing cohorts hiding behind fresh proxy addresses, and emits an explainable FIRE/WATCH/NOISE signal for tokens. Zero third-party packages; reads RH Pons V2 launch logs directly and attaches DexScreener market data.
- **Key pattern:** The engine scores a candidate token on four weighted factors computed from live wallet traffic: cohort overlap with a stored profitable-cohort memory (44%), timing proximity of recent transfers (28%), liquidity level (18%), and cohort size (up to 10%). Thresholds are explicit: NOISE below 70, WATCH 70-83, FIRE at or above 84. Cohort memory carries runs, hit-rate, and median return per named group; when addresses rotate, membership overlap still links the new cluster to the remembered formation. The live adapter calls eth_getLogs on the Pons V2 factory for launches, runs concurrent eth_call metadata in a small pool, keeps a 25-second read cache, and falls back to DexScreener robinhood pairs on RPC failure. A holder heuristic classifies wallets as NEW (nonce <= 3) / WARM (<=25) / ESTABLISHED (age) and detects contract vs EOA via eth_getCode.
- **For Memeland:** This maps almost one-to-one onto the smart-money voter. Memeland can replicate the cohort-memory store that binds recurring wallet groups even as they rotate to fresh relay addresses, and reuse the weighted, deduplicated, thresholded scoring as a transparent voter input. The RH RPC launch-log decoder and 25-second cache with DexScreener fallback are a concrete GMGN-independent market-data path for RH-4663. The NEW/WARM/ESTABLISHED holder-age heuristic and contract/EOA detection can feed the liquidity/security and smart-money voters.
- **Costs:** Cohort memory needs a local store plus a feedback loop that records outcomes and updates hit-rate / median return per group, which Memeland does not yet run for wallet clusters. Wallet-crawl eth_getLogs and per-holder balance/nonce/code calls add RPC load, so it needs the same rate-limiting and pooling discipline shown here. The scoring weights and thresholds are hand-tuned and would need local calibration on Memeland tick data.
- **Verdict:** Adopt - same Node/ESM ecosystem, RH-4663 native, GMGN-independent, and a concrete weighted cohort-voter with explainable thresholds and a working RPC adapter.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Read-only adapter, no secrets in code; dex fallback only enriches, and live mode stays on RPC. Complexity is low (one engine, one adapter, ~500 lines); main risk is RPC rate limits, so cap pool size and cache aggressively.

### SRC-151 mnemox-ai/tradememory-protocol (Python) - trading memory layer with adaptive risk and outcome-weighted recall
- **What it is:** A maintenance-mode Python memory protocol for trading agents: records every trade across five memory layers (episodic, semantic, procedural, affective, trade records), recalls past trades weighted by outcome quality and context similarity, runs a SHA-256 tamper-evident audit trail, and derives risk constraints from closed-trade history. Ships as an MCP server plus REST and includes an evolution/simulation subsystem and MT5 sync.
- **Key pattern:** Two concrete algorithms stand out. AdaptiveRisk runs five rule-based risk algorithms over a 30-day closed-trade journal - quarter-Kelly sizing, drawdown scale factor (DD>10% 0.5x, >5% 0.75x), per-session win-rate adjustments, consecutive-loss limit, daily-loss limit - and merges them worst-status-wins into one RiskConstraints object. check_trade only ever reduces lot size, never increases, with a floor. The OWM module scores recalled memories (outcome quality, context similarity, recency, confidence, emotional state) and computes fractional Kelly from weighted win/loss statistics (p/a - q/b), returning 0.0 below a 10-memory minimum. Audit chain hashes every decision for tamper evidence.
- **For Memeland:** AdaptiveRisk is a clean, dependency-light blueprint for Memeland layered risk gates + sizing: it already matches the worst-status-wins merge and only-reduce behavior Memeland wants, and adds two fresh levers - per-session/condition win-rate multipliers and drawdown-proportional scale. The OWM outcome-weighted recall feeding Kelly is a genuine memory-protocol edge: Memeland can persist each closed position as an R-multiple record tagged by market condition and weight recalls by similarity and recency instead of replaying raw PnL. Audit hashing of each decision is cheap and strengthens the green label documentation story.
- **Costs:** The repo as a whole is heavy - MCP server, LLM evolution loop, simulation runners, MT5 adapters - which is too much for the low-complexity target and those parts should be ignored. The two portable pieces are Python and must be re-expressed in TS. Memory recall needs a persistent store and a condition-similarity function Memeland does not currently maintain; Kelly requires at least 10 closed trades with R-multiples, so it is inactive early on.
- **Verdict:** Adapt - copy the AdaptiveRisk worst-status-wins constraint merge and the outcome-weighted recall-to-Kelly sizing as lightweight TS modules; skip the MCP, evolution, and simulation scaffolding.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No secrets in core modules; audit-chain hashing adds minimal risk. Main complexity risk is importing the heavy OWM/evolution surface - restrict adoption to adaptive_risk.py and the Kelly recall math, not the MCP layer.

### SRC-184 pgen0x/azimuth (Go daemon + Node executors) - layered multi-mode screen with fail-open safety and sizing governance on Solana DLMM + RH-4663 Uni v3/v4
- **What it is:** A continuous pool-discovery daemon that screens Solana Meteora DLMM pools and RH-4663 Uniswap v3/v4 pools through a long stack of gates, selects the strongest candidate per cycle, and governs deployment via live-wallet sizing, tenure ramps and cooldowns. The LP/executor half is heavy and niche, but the screening and governance layers are the transferable core.
- **Key pattern:** (1) Keyless discovery decoupled from GMGN - RH pools come from the GeckoTerminal public new_pools/trending feeds plus the Uniswap interface GraphQL gateway for mature pools; GMGN OpenAPI is used ONLY for contract-security and holder-quality enrichment, and it is singled out because it is the only security source that supports RH-4663 (GoPlus and honeypot.is do not, verified in safety.go). (2) A split safety convention: missing or unfetchable data passes (fail-open), but POSITIVE detection - honeypot, blacklist, sell-tax over 10% - hard-rejects (fail-closed), and a sticky SecurityBlacklist store persists a conviction so a later unknown cannot undo an earlier honeypot verdict (GMGN flaps on BLINK). (3) Gates run in a fixed budget-aware order in screen.go: quote-asset pin, v4 hook and dynamic-fee hard rejects, age band, reserve band, fee tier, fee/TVL day pace (realized 24h vs h1 extrapolation), h1 txn and unique-buyer counts, then the decisive m15 live-window flow gates (m15 txns, USD volume, fee/TVL) which catch books that stopped trading, a no-sells honeypot pre-check, an FDV band, and 5m/1h/6h/24h downtrend cuts. (4) A geometric-mean 0-100 score of turnover, buyers, fee pace and liquidity in which any zero sub-score zeroes the whole score, plus a RankByFeeDensity variant that squares fee pace. (5) Governance: lone-candidate conviction floor (a solo pool ships only when its score clears an explicit floor - only option is not good option), copycat symbol guard (advisory, demotes), PVP rival/ticker-war flag (bounded to one symbol search per unique symbol per cycle), per-mode dedup TTL, re-entry cooldown (1-2h base escalating to 24h/72h on repeat losses), and a tenure sizing ramp that sizes untested pools to a floor probe. (6) Modes partition the age axis (fresh/mature/turnover/pulse-ladder), each with its own discovery source, and thresholds are recalibrated from outcome journals with per-gate reason tallies (reasonKey) for diagnostics.
- **For Memeland:** This is the strongest reference for GMGN decoupling on RH-4663: adopt the GeckoTerminal + Uniswap-gateway discovery path so GMGN becomes optional enrichment for the security voter rather than the market-data spine. Port the fail-open-unless-positive-security convention with a sticky conviction cache into the security/bot-risk voters. Port the layered gate order and the m15 live-window flow gates into the liquidity voter. Port the lone-candidate conviction floor and the PVP symbol-war guard into the consensus gate so a single candidate cannot force a >80% pass. Reuse the tenure sizing ramp and escalating re-entry cooldown directly in RiskManager and the exit path.
- **Costs:** The repo as a whole is a heavy monolithic Go daemon over Redis with bundled Node executors - far too much to adopt wholesale; only the screen, safety, and sizing patterns transfer as small TS modules. Every threshold here is calibrated from azimuth close journals and must be re-fit on Memeland tick data. GeckoTerminal and the Uniswap gateway do not cover every chain, so Memeland still needs per-chain discovery adapters for Solana/BSC/Base/ETH.
- **Verdict:** Adapt - copy the keyless multi-source discovery, fail-closed-on-positive-security with sticky convictions, layered live-flow gates, lone-candidate conviction floor, PVP guard, tenure sizing and escalating cooldown; skip the heavy daemon, Redis and executor shell.
- **Security/complexity flags:** GMGN API key lives server-side in env only and is guarded by a secrets hook; the deliberate fail-open default is acceptable only because the positive-detection arm fails closed and convictions are sticky. Pattern complexity is low once extracted; watch GeckoTerminal rate limits (the daemon explicitly sequences modes so a spent budget starves lower-priority feeds last).

### SRC-185 PillCrew/claimchain (Python + JS port) - on-chain groundedness checker for AI analysis
- **What it is:** A small, zero-dependency tool that extracts every falsifiable numeric claim from an LLM agent's written analysis (regex: price-change %, market cap, FDV, liquidity, volume, price, pool age, vol/liq ratio) and adjudicates each against live on-chain ground truth fetched from the free keyless DexScreener API. Each claim gets one of VERIFIED / CONTRADICTED / UNRESOLVED, plus a 0-100 groundedness score summarizing how much of the analysis is actually true. Ships as stdio JSON-RPC MCP tools (verify_claims, token_snapshot) so any MCP-speaking agent can check itself before trusting or repeating a call. A synchronized JS port is kept in sync via shared test cases.
- **Key pattern:** The neutral-ground-truth verification loop: extract structured claims from free text -> normalize provider data to a TokenTruth dataclass -> per-metric tolerance comparison (percentage-point tolerance of +/-2.0 for change claims, relative tolerance of 20% for dollar/scalar claims, with edge handling for zero actual). Provider selection picks the native-chain pair with the most real 24h volume to avoid wrapped-pair poisoning. The design is transport-agnostic (DexScreener provider is swappable for a static in-memory provider for offline tests) and entirely stdlib, so it is trivially testable and portable.
- **For Memeland:** Directly attacks a real weakness in the 7-voter swarm: LLM voters emit narrative reasons that are cited as consensus input but are rarely grounded in verifiable numbers. Adopting the extract-verify-groundedness loop lets Memeland sanitize each voter's output against a neutral per-chain data source, down-weight or void voters whose claims get CONTRADICTED, and derive a confidence cut before the >=80% consensus gate. It is a portable TS/Python pattern covering multiple chains (DexScreener spans all of them incl. some RH-4663 pairs), so it does not add GMGN coupling and can be backstopped by another provider. The MCP tool wrapper also maps cleanly to a per-voter check hook. The tolerance parameters (percent_pp, relative) mirror existing calibration thinking.
- **Costs:** Claim extraction is regex-based, so it only catches numbers phrased like its templates (systematic but not semantic); meaning-extraction tuning is needed for other markets Memeland observes. DexScreener ground truth is off-chain API pricing with its own staleness, so verdicts inherit that caveat. No position logic here, only verification, so it is a hardening layer, not an edge source.
- **Verdict:** Adapt Groundedness-gate is a real, low-complexity, chain-portable cross-check that belongs in the consensus/sanction path.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No secrets (keyless public API); all stdlib, low complexity; regex precision is the main fragility risk to test; port to TS rather than pull in a Python runtime for Memeland.

### SRC-186 PillCrew/PillCrew (JavaScript/Electron) - desktop pet meme-coin UI with multi-source keyless data
- **What it is:** A Solana meme-coin desktop pet app (Pilly) that shows a watchlist, trending coins, whales, chat and a pick scorecard. Not a trading engine: it has no execution, no strategy beyond surfacing coins, and no portfolio logic. The two files of real substance are src/coins.js (keyless market data) and src/picks.js (a proactive-pick win/loss track record).
- **Key pattern:** src/coins.js fetches free Solana market data with no API keys, trying four sources in order (pump.fun, DexScreener, Jupiter, GeckoTerminal), each degrading independently, with a retry/backoff helper that treats 429/408/5xx as transient and retries before ever reporting the feed as unavailable. src/picks.js records each proactive pick at call-time price and later resolves it to win/loss/flat against +/-1% bounds, deduplicating the same mint within 30 minutes and keeping the last 20 entries, then aggregates a win-rate scorecard.
- **For Memeland:** The retry/backoff-on-429/5xx fetch pattern and the independent multi-source degradation are directly relevant to reducing Memeland's GMGN coupling and hardening its data layer, though Memeland already has a similar resilient-fetch philosophy in its RPC/data failover. The pick scorecard is a simpler, cruder version of the calibration/track-record machinery Memeland already anchors on. No meaningful edge transfers beyond these two generic robustness habits.
- **Costs:** Everything is Solana-specific (pump.fun, Solana DEXes) and most of the repo is UI/pet animation noise. The scorecard uses a single short-horizon pct bound with no per-token context, so it is not a strategy signal. Adopting would mean re-encoding the retry/backoff and multi-source fallback in TS, which is largely redundant with existing work.
- **Verdict:** Skip No real edge; Solana-only UI app whose only useful patterns (resilient keyless fetch, simple scorecard) are already covered by Memeland anchors.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Keyless and secret-free; desktop app code is UI-heavy and low relevance; nothing risky to take, and nothing worth the port.

### SRC-196 sevenlabs-hq/carbon (Rust) - Solana indexer framework: datasource -> decoder -> processor pipeline
- **What it is:** A Rust framework for building Solana indexers and data pipelines. A pipeline streams Solana updates from datasources (RPC/Geyser streams incl. Yellowstone gRPC, Jetstream, Jito ShredStream), decoders turn raw account/transaction data into typed Rust structures (hand-written or generated from an Anchor IDL via a CLI), and processors consume decoded output to feed Postgres, GraphQL, log metrics, analytics, bots and other sinks. Each stage is modular and swappable.
- **Key pattern:** The clean three-stage streaming abstraction (datasource -> decoder -> processor) with typed decode via account/instruction/transaction decoders and a filter layer; heavy use of Postgres + GraphQL for storage/query of decoded on-chain data. Also an IDL-driven decoder generator so new programs can be parsed without hand-rolled code.
- **For Memeland:** The datasource/decoder/processor separation is a sound architecture concept, but Memeland is TypeScript and has no Solana-native indexing need (it reads market data via GMGN/APIs, not raw chain stream decode). The IDL-driven decoder generation and Postgres+GraphQL storage are Solana/Rust-specific and do not map to RH-4663 or Memeland's dependency-light direction. MarketSentinel already gives Memeland a decoupled risk/market-data service without this level of infrastructure.
- **Costs:** Heavy: Rust workspace, Postgres, GraphQL, multiple streaming datasources, gRPC/Geyser infrastructure. Pulling any of it in would fight Memeland's low-complexity, TS, low-external-coupling goals and would not port to RH-4663.
- **Verdict:** Skip Rust Solana-indexer infrastructure with no portable edge for a TS multi-chain bot; architecture concept only.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No secrets; but a large infra monolith (Rust workspace + Postgres + GraphQL + gRPC stream deps) that is far too heavy and chain-specific to adopt.

### SRC-197 shmidtqq65/loxley (JavaScript/Node, viem) - Robinhood-chain exit desk: pons v2 launchpad screening and execution to the wei
- **What it is:** A read-by-default, arm-to-trade terminal for the Robinhood Chain (chain id robinhood) plus a browser desk. It reads DexScreener, GeckoTerminal, the RH Blockscout explorer and the RH RPC (eth_call / eth_getLogs) - no GMGN at all - and its sniper signs buys/sells on viem. The core value is cli/engine.js, a pure-math module with no I/O: computeMetrics -> hardFlags -> scoreOf -> verdictsOf for pools, plus curveMaths / exitMaths / launchScore / farmOf / refusals for the pons v2 bonding-curve launchpad.
- **Key pattern:** (1) A fully side-effect-free scoring engine organised as pure functions so every rule is unit-testable without a chain. (2) launchScore for the first minute of a fresh launch: dev-buy share banding (1-6% good, over 10% a dump bag), creator tax banding, ETH pair check, socials, declared-exempt/bundle wallets, block-0 bundle %, top-5 concentration, fill-to-graduation, and a per-deployer record (launches vs graduated). (3) refusals(): the sniper names every reason it will not fire (score below min, non-ETH pair, dev share/tax ceilings, twins, exempt count, bundle pct, no socials, launch farm, open positions max). (4) farmOf(): fingerprint a launch farm as the same dev-buy-to-the-wei plus same creator tax plus same social links from other fresh wallets inside 30 minutes. (5) 8 named agents (SCOUT/AUDIT/WHALE/LP/FLOW/VOL/SNIPER/EXIT) each with ok/warn/bad thresholds that colour a verdict ring - structurally the same 7-voter swarm idea as Memeland. (6) Execution to the wei: quoteBuy/quoteSell handle fee + creator tax + opening snipe tax, cap output at sellable tokens, compute refunds, use exact quoter and Uniswap v4 universal-router encoding, and every send is simulated before it is signed. (7) Data-layer hardening: per-host circuit breaker (two timeouts skip the host for 20s), rate-limit spacing between explorer calls, honest user-agent, and key redaction so no printed message can leak an API key.
- **For Memeland:** This is native RH-4663 work with no external paid coupling and directly portable Node/TS. The cleanly incremental items beyond Memeland's existing anchors are: the launchScore/refusals/farmOf launchpad gate (a concrete, named-reason deny path for RH pons v2 launches), the no-I/O pure-engine file discipline matching the calibration and layered-risk-gate anchors, and the host circuit-breaker + spacing + redaction in the data layer. The 8-agent ok/warn/bad voting pattern is a strong reference design for Memeland's 7-voter consensus. The wei-exact quote/fee/tax/cap/refund bookkeeping and sim-before-send reinforce the fill-simulation and RH execution-governance anchors.
- **Costs:** Largest value is in reading and re-implementing patterns rather than copying code; the terminal is opinionated to the pons v2 launchpad and RH Uni-v4 router, so adaptation is needed to generalize to the full chain set. Uses DexScreener/GeckoTerminal still (a paid-free diversification away from GMGN, not a removal). Medium complexity to lift the engine cleanly.
- **Verdict:** Adopt The pons launchpad scoring + named refusals + farm fingerprint and the pure-engine module are concrete, RH-4663-native, low-coupling algorithms that map straight to a real edge.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Key handling is conservative (redacted, query-param key never printed, mover-signing isolated in one file gated behind arming); signing is deliberately separated from reads. Moderate complexity mostly in trade/sniper; the engine itself is simple and testable.

### SRC-198 Shradhesh71/YellowStone-gRPC (TypeScript) - Solana Yellowstone gRPC indexer with health monitoring and multi-channel alerts
- **What it is:** A TypeScript real-time indexer that subscribes to the Solana Yellowstone gRPC stream, tracks a configured set of tokens (USDC, USDT, SOL, BONK, JUP), watches account updates and transactions for whale-sized transfers with per-token thresholds, writes to Prisma/Postgres, and fans alerts out to Discord, Telegram and custom webhooks. A PerformanceMonitor tracks throughput, latency, memory/CPU and derives a health status with threshold-based health checks.
- **Key pattern:** The subscription layer builds filter requests for minimal/whale/full modes; the processing layer normalizes stream events into typed token activity; the alert layer abstracts multiple channels behind a config with per-token alertThreshold; the monitor computes messages-per-second, average processing time, and a four-part health check (database latency, stream silence, memory, alert failure rate) with graceful reconnection/shutdown handling. Per-token config in one file makes adding a mint trivial.
- **For Memeland:** The transport (Solana gRPC account subscriptions) does not port to RH-4663 (EVM), so the indexer core is not directly reusable. The transferable pieces are generic operational patterns: stream-silence detection and reconnection counting for a live market-data feed (directly useful for GMGN/sentinel observability), the alert-channel abstraction behind a config with thresholds, and the lightweight health-check thresholds. These are hardening/observability ideas, not a trading edge.
- **Costs:** Solana-only gRPC stack (Yellowstone endpoint + token, Postgres/Prisma); no EVM/RH-4663 equivalent. Even the generic monitoring would need re-implementation in TS rather than reuse. Low algorithmic depth beyond the monitoring scaffolding.
- **Verdict:** Study Stream-health and multi-channel alert patterns are worth noting for Memeland's sentinel/data-layer observability, but the indexer core is Solana-only and portable to RH-4663.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Requires a Yellowstone gRPC token and Postgres URL via env (.env), plus Discord/Telegram secrets; secrets are plaintext-env style. Moderate complexity and no real edge to justify adopting wholesale.

### SRC-202 solo-agent/solo (Go + Next.js) - local-first workspace for coordinating multiple AI coding agents
- **What it is:** An open-source, local-first platform (Go server, Postgres, Next.js/React frontend) for running and coordinating multiple AI coding agents (Claude Code, Codex CLI, OpenCode, Hermes, OpenClaw) through channels, DMs, threads, task boards and channel-scoped teams. It adds long-term agent memory, task claiming/submission/review, budget gating, a code-gate, agent selection/revision and observability. It is a workspace for human + coding-agent teams, not an execution system.
- **Key pattern:** Agent lifecycle orchestration: message-to-task claiming, task review dispatch, agent selection with independent review, a budget gate, a code-gate that verifies intended changes before apply, revision/skills management, and agent observability dashboards. Backed by a large Postgres schema with per-agent runs, transcripts, and equipment.
- **For Memeland:** No trading-specific signal, risk or execution logic exists here. The agent-selection/budget-gate/code-gate ideas are about governing LLM coding agents, not about market consensus or position sizing, so they do not map to Memeland's 7-voter consensus or RiskManager. Memeland's existing layered risk-gates and consensus model already cover the analogous governance concerns at the trading layer.
- **Costs:** A heavy monolith (Go server + Postgres + Next.js + agent CLIs + Docker) with a huge migration surface - entirely disproportionate for a personal trading bot and it would add high complexity with no edge. Nothing here is chain-portable into a TS trading engine.
- **Verdict:** Skip Heavy general-purpose AI-agent workspace with no trading algorithm or edge that maps to Memeland's goals.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Large multi-service deployment with many secrets (.env, Postgres, auth/JWT, agent tokens); code-gate and budget controls show care, but it is far too heavy and off-topic to adopt.

### SRC-203 sopersone/CROWBRAIN (Python stdlib) - read-only SMA market observation terminal
- **What it is:** A small, read-only Python standard-library research terminal (threaded HTTP server + SQLite journal). It polls a single DexScreener pair (configurable for Solana or Robinhood chain), keeps a 180-sample price window, runs a trivial 5-vs-20-sample SMA decision (BUY/SELL/WAIT past a +/-0.30% threshold) and journals every signal with its reason to SQLite. It explicitly has no wallet access and sends no orders.
- **Key pattern:** Data hygiene rather than edge: strict chain-id and pair-address matching on the provider response so a cross-chain/cross-pair result cannot be ingested, a bounded (2 MB cap) response read, honest user-agent, and a live-mode rule that clears the sample window after a 90 s feed gap so stale data cannot straddle an outage (it emits WAIT while the feed is down rather than a synthetic estimate). An illustrative showcase mode is kept clearly separate from live mode.
- **For Memeland:** The chain/pair-matching guard and the feed-gap-clearing rule are decent hygiene ideas, but Memeland's data layer and MarketSentinel already treat stale-data and cross-chain mismatches with more rigor (and real failover). The 5/20 SMA engine is orders of magnitude simpler than Memeland's 7-voter consensus and adds no edge. Nothing here is worth porting.
- **Costs:** Trivial rule engine with no risk sizing, no multi-factor scoring, and no execution; adopting any of it would be a regression in capability with no benefit.
- **Verdict:** Skip A toy SMA observation loop with no edge; its only hygiene patterns are already covered by Memeland's data layer.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Local-only, no secrets, no execution capability (safe); minimal complexity. Its own docs call out that it is a transparent baseline rather than a real model.

### SRC-211 thetateman/Trading-API (Markdown readme) - README-only rundown of the hosted PumpPortal API
- **What it is:** Not a codebase - the repo contains only a README that documents the PumpPortal.fun third-party API for trading on the pump.fun bonding curve (Solana). It describes HTTPS endpoints, a free data tier, a key-gated Trading API that charges a 0.5% fee per trade, and a trade-local endpoint that returns a serialized transaction for signing locally, plus web3.js send examples.
- **Key pattern:** The only idea of interest is trade-local: fetch a serialized unsigned transaction from a hosted service and sign it with your own keypair + custom RPC, i.e. keeping the signing key out of the service. This is standard and is essentially what Memeland's local signing and execution layer already does.
- **For Memeland:** Nothing transferable. The project is README-only with zero code, describes a hosted single-chain (Solana) paid API, and is outside Memeland's low-coupling, chain-portable direction. The sign-locally idea adds no new information.
- **Costs:** No implementation, and adopting the described service would mean a hosted, key-gated, per-trade-fee dependency on a non-RH chain - the opposite of reducing external coupling.
- **Verdict:** Skip README-only repo for a hosted single-chain paid API with no code to audit and no portable edge.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No secrets in the repo; the described service requires API keys and takes a fee - nothing to evaluate technically since there is no code.

### SRC-216 tow3web3/agentinu (Python demo only) - landing page for a claimed autonomous Solana memecoin agent
- **What it is:** A marketing/landing repo for a claimed 24/7 autonomous Solana memecoin trading agent. The README describes a detect -> analyze -> trade -> distribute loop (pump.fun/Raydium pair scanning, a multi-factor deployer/LP/social/momentum scoring model, Claude-derived 0-100 conviction score, Jupiter V6 swaps with conviction-scaled sizing and trailing stops, and automatic SOL profit distribution to token holders).
- **Key pattern:** The only code present is demo.py - a terminal animation that types out simulated trades (SCAN/EVAL/CONF/BUY/TRAIL/SELL/DIST lines). There is no scanner, no scoring implementation, no execution code, no conviction model in the repo; all substance is asserted in prose and a dashboard GIF. The described conviction-scaled sizing and trailing-stop management are behavioral ideas, not an auditable algorithm here.
- **For Memeland:** Nothing transferable: the repo contains no real code to read, the alleged edge is Solana-only (pump.fun/Jupiter/Helius), and it leans on a closed external AI (Claude) for conviction with no exposed method, which runs against Memeland's low-external-coupling and chain-portability goals. Memeland's conviction/consensus and trailing-exit machinery already cover the ideas that prose claims.
- **Costs:** Zero implementation to lift; the stated stack (Binance WS, Jupiter, pump.fun, Helius RPC, Claude) is external, Solana-specific, and not RH-4663 transferable.
- **Verdict:** Skip No auditable code (README + animation demo only); asserted edge is opaque and external to Memeland's coupling and portability goals.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No secrets in the repo; pages advertise a live wallet and dashboards off-chain. No code path to review, so nothing to assess technically.

### SRC-219 uerax/all-in-one-bot (Go) - Telegram bot with kline win-rate + multi-source meme screening
- **What it is:** A Go Telegram bot that monitors crypto prices, tracks smart wallets via Etherscan, probes meme tokens (土狗币), and computes a win-rate (胜率) for tokens using 5-minute K-lines. It is a broad utility bot (chatgpt, video download, stickers), so only the crypto modules are relevant.
- **Key pattern:** KlineAnalyze reads DexScreener-style klines (open/high/close USD) at 5-min or 60-min resolution, uses the first candle open/close max as the initial buy price, counts how many candles exceed that price, and only counts a rise as valid if at least 2 candles (10 min) stay above entry (gto<2 -> profit zeroed). MemePrice fans out to multiple feeds (DexScreener for price, GoPlus/honeypot.is for honeypot, dextools/dex.guru/dexview/ave.ai links) in parallel goroutines, and tracks smart wallets with Etherscan API + HTML scrape.
- **For Memeland:** The kline win-rate hygiene (require >=2 candles above entry before counting a rise valid) is a concrete, simple anti-noise rule transferable to Memeland exit math. The redundant multi-source meme data fan-out (price + honeypot + taxes + LP lock, run concurrently) is a portable TS pattern for cutting single-source GMGN coupling on BSC/ETH tokens, and the smart-wallet tracking mirrors the existing smart-money voter semantics.
- **Costs:** Written in Go, tightly wired to Telegram and rate-limited paid APIs (Etherscan, GoPlus, honeypot.is), ETH/BSC-focused with no RH-4663 support; the kline logic is a heuristic, not a backtested edge. Porting to TS means reimplementing the resolution-selection and candle-count logic against Memeland kline sources.
- **Verdict:** Study the two-candle-above-entry win-rate rule and the concurrent multi-source screening fan-out as portable TS patterns.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Config holds plaintext Telegram tokens and Etherscan API keys in YAML (local-only demo); Etherscan HTML scraping and multiple paid API keys add operational fragility. Verdict-ready (Study).
### SRC-221 ValueCell-ai/valuecell (Python + Tauri) - LLM-driven multi-agent stock/crypto platform
- **What it is:** A large community-driven multi-agent platform for financial apps (A-shares + crypto). Runs named investment agents (Buffett, Graham, Munger, Damodaran portfolios), a deep-research agent, a news agent, and a grid trading strategy, with a FastAPI server, SQLite persistence, and a Tauri/React desktop frontend. Trading is executed through a ccxt adapter.
- **Key pattern:** The trading decision layer is LLM-driven: a BaseComposer turns user requests into normalized TradeInstructions through guardrail functions (_init_buying_power_context, _normalize_quantity) that cap quantity by choosing power, max positions, and leverage policy, and a grid_composer implements grid math with an optional LLM parameter advisor.
- **For Memeland:** The only remappable ideas are the buying-power/leverage guardrail pipeline and the multi-agent name-branding of voters, both of which Mapeland already has (named voter swarm, layered risk gates + sizing). The rest is A-share/centralized-exchange oriented, ccxt-based, and stock-centric, not portable to RH-4663 token trades.
- **Costs:** Heavy monolith: Python + ccxt + Tauri desktop + FastAPI + object-relational persistence; no on-chain/defi market data; GMGN-free but chain-agnostic centralized-exchange trading only; high complexity with no single concrete edge to borrow.
- **Verdict:** Skip heavy LLM-driven monolith with ccxt dependency; no concrete portable algorithm beyond guardrails already in Memeland.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Keeps secrets local by design but adds ccxt and many LLM provider configs; no RH-4663 or on-chain coverage. Verdict-ready (Skip).
### SRC-228 wealthfolio/wealthfolio (Rust + Tauri + Svelte) - Local-first portfolio & net-worth tracker
- **What it is:** An open-source, private, local-first personal finance tracker covering investments, net worth, spending, and simulations, with a Rust backend, Tauri desktop shell, Svelte web frontend, mobile apps, and a Docker self-host server. Data is stored locally, with optional brokerage sync (read-only) behind a paid connect service.
- **Key pattern:** No trading logic: it tracks holdings and net worth, imports brokerage CSV, and runs basic simulations. The notable engineering concerns are local encrypted storage and read-only external brokerage connectors, not any market/entry/exit algorithm.
- **For Memeland:** Nothing maps to a trading edge: no scoring, no risk gates, no execution, no chain or swap logic, and no market-data coupling of any kind. At best it offers a reference for private local persistence of PnL, which is not a gap Memeland needs to fill for the green label.
- **Costs:** Rust/Tauri/Svelte heavy frontend stack; focused on centralized brokerage/net-worth, none on RH-4663 or any EVM/Solana defi token trading; adding its patterns would pull in unrelated portfolio-UI complexity.
- **Verdict:** Skip a portfolio/net-worth tracker with no strategy, scoring, or execution code relevant to Memeland.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No secrets misuse; local-first design is sound, but the app targets brokerage, not on-chain trading, so it is not applicable. Verdict-ready (Skip).
### SRC-231 ygwyg/MAHORAGA (TypeScript) - LLM trading agent on Cloudflare Workers with a policy engine
- **What it is:** An autonomous, LLM-powered trading agent that runs 24/7 on Cloudflare Workers (Durable Object), monitors social sentiment from StockTwits/Reddit/Twitter, uses OpenAI/Anthropic/DeepSeek etc. to analyze signals, and executes trades (stocks, options, BTC/ETH/SOL) through Alpaca, with a dashboard and D1 persistence.
- **Key pattern:** A PolicyEngine validates every order before execution and, if all checks pass, issues an approval token valid for a TTL (default 5 min) that must be presented to the broker (policy-broker.ts wraps requests and enforces the token). Checks: kill switch, loss cooldown, daily loss limit, market-hours, symbol allow/deny lists, order-type, notional caps, position size % of equity, max open positions, short-sell and buying power. Separate pluggable strategies select entries/exits via selectEntries/selectExits against a 30-second alarm heartbeat.
- **For Memeland:** The approval-token-with-TTL pattern (bind a validated order to a time-limited execution token so policy checks cannot go stale) is a clean reinforcement of Memeland execution governance. Its kill switch + cooldown + daily-loss + position-size gate list is exactly the layered-risk-gates + sizing anchor Memeland already adopts. The pluggable strategy-selectEntries/selectExits harness is a lightweight way to decouple strategy modules, mirroring the decoupled MarketSentinel/Engine model.
- **Costs:** Locks to Cloudflare Workers + Alpaca (CEX/stocks/options) and to LLM-driven sentiment signals; nothing touches RH-4663 or any EVM/Solana DEX. The policy engine re-uses ideas already behind Memeland risk gates, so only the token-TTL binding is genuinely additive.
- **Verdict:** Study the approval-token TTL binding and pluggable entry/exit strategy harness to harden execution governance.
- **Security/complexity flags:** API keys via Workers secrets (not plaintext in repo); no chain/defi coverage; Alpaca-only means code is not portable to RH-4663. Verdict-ready (Study).
### SRC-233 zetryn-ai/ai-agent (Python) - Solana memecoin decision engine with downgrade-only guardrails and calibrated confidence
- **What it is:** Zetryn is a decision-only Python framework for Solana memecoins (bring your own bot/wallet/RPC): raw token data flows through hard gates (safety/intel/market), then an LLM analyst returns per-aspect scores (safety/market/wallets/social) plus an alert/watch/skip recommendation, then hard guardrails sanity-check the recommendation, and a final Decision carries action + confidence + reasons.
- **Key pattern:** (1) Deterministic pre-LLM hard gates in filters.py (safety/intel/market) abort junk cheaply before any LLM call, with weighted deterministic scorers for momentum (bias to neutral 0.5 for fresh launches, not zero), wallet intel (smart money + KOL minus sniper-density penalty), and social. (2) Downgrade-only guardrails in decide.py: hard checks can only demote an LLM recommendation (alert->watch->skip), never promote, e.g. liquidity below half minimum, sniper-wallet density too high, buy-ratio sell pressure, and a score/recommendation consistency cascade. (3) Confidence CalibrationMap: Decision.confidence becomes the empirical win rate observed for a given final score per token source, fit from closed-trade history. (4) A backtest module scores historical TokenInput + TokenOutcome (rugged, max_gain_pct, pnl_pct) into win rate, avg/total PnL, and rug-avoidance precision/recall plus entry precision.
- **For Memeland:** Directly maps to the 7-voter swarm + 80% gate: adopt the downgrade-only guardrail mechanism so hard rule checks (liquidity, sniper density, sell pressure) can demote but never promote a voter or consensus verdict; adopt the rug-avoidance precision/recall and entry-precision backtest metrics to score the voter gate; reuse the momentum neutral-0.5-for-fresh-launches bias; and treat the CalibrationMap as a concrete implementation of the existing confidence-calibration anchor.
- **Costs:** Solana/PumpFun data model and GMGN-external-safety-score reference (min_gmgn_safety_score) are chain-specific, so the scorers need re-mapping to RH-4663/BSC/Base inputs; the LLM-analyst piece adds a model dependency Memeland may not want for a deterministic gate. Purely a decision layer, no execution to borrow.
- **Verdict:** Adopt the downgrade-only guardrail pipeline and the rug-avoidance/entry backtest metrics as portable TS patterns for the consensus gate.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No keys handled (framework never holds the private key); declarative graph-spec + eight reference agents add moderate complexity; source agnostic on data feeds but tuned to Solana/PumpFun, requiring re-derivation for RH-4663. Verdict-ready (Adopt).
### SRC-234 ZhuLinsen/daily_stock_analysis (Python) - AI-driven multi-market stock analysis & decision-report system
- **What it is:** A large AI/LLM-based watchlist analysis system for A-shares/HK/US/JP/KR/TW equities that daily analyzes selected stocks and pushes a decision dashboard (score, trend, buy/sell points, risk alerts, catalysts) to WeCom/Feishu/Telegram/Discord/Slack/email. It aggregates quotes, klines, technical indicators, news, fundamentals via AkShare/Tushare/YFinance/TickFlow/Longbridge and search via SerpAPI/Tavily/etc., with a FastAPI server and web/desktop workbench.
- **Key pattern:** LLM decision reports over multi-provider market data with a data-source priority/fallback model and a per-stock failure-isolation thread pool. Trading discipline is encoded as stock-TA heuristics in the prompt/config: no chasing when bias ratio >5%, only MA5>MA10>MA20 bullish alignment, buy on low-volume pullback to MA5/MA10 support.
- **For Memeland:** The multi-provider market-data fallback (AkShare/Tushare/YFinance with priority) is the same decoupling goal Memeland has for GMGN, but it is centralized-exchange/stock oriented and adds nothing chain-specific for RH-4663. The MA-bias trading rules are equity TA, not a portable on-chain meme edge; the LLM decision-report framing is heavier than Memeland deterministic voters and duplicates existing risk/report capabilities.
- **Costs:** Very large LLM-driven platform (env-driven, many paid data/search APIs, FastAPI + desktop + CI pipelines); no defi/swap/RPC/safety data; no GMGN but no RH-4663 or Solana coverage either; complexity far exceeds any concrete pattern worth porting.
- **Verdict:** Skip heavy LLM-driven stock-analysis report platform; multi-source fallback is a generic pattern already pursued by Memeland, and the TA rules are equity-only.
- **Security/complexity flags:** Large .env.example with many third-party keys (local-only); no secrets in code but many paid API dependencies; A-shares/equities focus means no on-chain relevance. Verdict-ready (Skip).
### SRC-235 zostaff/agent-arena (TypeScript, "DEGEN VILLAGE") - Robinhood-Chain trading sim with pure detectors and deterministic net backtest
- **What it is:** An open, isometric "village" where nameable trading agents (SCOUT/SNIPER/WHALE/ARB/FLY) run on RH-4663 with Pons launchpad context: a FORGE tunes strategy into engine params (polling, context depth, size, slippage, model budget), FROG RADAR observes market detectors, SPIDER watches up to 12 Robinhood Chain wallets, and a ledger backtests BUILDS. Chain support is raw JSON-RPC to the free public RPC (rpc.mainnet.chain.robinhood.com), no indexer and no API key.
- **Key pattern:** (1) Pure deterministic detectors in src/frog/radar.ts with strict candle hygiene: RIPPLE (latest closed-candle volume >= 2x the mean of the prior 8), LEAP (price beyond the prior 8-candle high/low by >= 0.2%), DEPTH (top-5 one-side depth >= 65% with >= 3 valid levels and an uncrossed book), with closed-vs-forming-candle handling, freshness windows, finite/chronological OHLCV validation and per-field provenance. (2) Verdict hardening in src/core/brain.ts: parseVerdict never trusts a model on size (clamps sizeEth to maxSizeEth), forces SKIP when SELL is not openable, clamps holdTicks/confidence and truncates reason; guardDecide resolves every failure path to SKIP so it can never throw or hang. (3) A deterministic backtest (fixed seed 42, 7000 ticks) that is byte-identical across providers: GROSS is house-independent because the sim brain never reads the model id, and NET = gross minus model cost, so a leaderboard cannot be gamed by cheap moves.
- **For Memeland:** Direct RH-4663 play: the FROG RADAR detector set (volume pulse, range break, book imbalance) is a concrete multi-sensor entry/momentum layer that substitutes for a single market-data API and reduces GMGN coupling. Adopt the pure-function detector contract with closed-candle/freshness hygiene, the never-trust-the-model-on-external-parameters + fail-safe-SKIP hardening to reinforce execution governance, and the gross-house-independent/net-includes-cost backtest split for fairly scoring voter gate vs execution cost. SPIDER shared-incoming-token clustering (>= 2 wallets receiving the same token inside 60s) and raw Pons factory/router RPC reading are direct RH-4663 additions to the smart-money voter and the RH fast-submit pipeline.
- **Costs:** A game/sim codebase (sim candles, synthetic book, UI-heavy) so the engine pieces are embedded in a larger build; the sim brain is an LLM-prompt heuristic rather than a tested strategy, so only the detector/hardening/backtest-instrumentation patterns transfer, not the edge itself. Pons-specific addresses/topics must be re-validated against current RH-4663 deployments on Memeland.
- **Verdict:** Adopt the pure radar detectors, verdict-hardening/fail-safe SKIP, and the gross-net deterministic backtest split as portable TS patterns for the RH-4663 entry layer and evaluation ledger.
- **Security/complexity flags:** No secrets (public free RPC, no API key); raw JSON-RPC chain reader is a clean no-key design. Moderate complexity from the sim/game shell; the LLM heuristic brain itself is not an edge. Verdict-ready (Adopt).


## Batch 20 - market-data, chain-data-infra & general external web deep-reads (subagent Planck)
### SRC-005 market-data - predictions.exchange DEX (dead site)
- **What it is:** The predictions.exchange/dex page is dead; the domain serves HTTP 410 Gone for both /dex and the site root, and web search surfaces no surviving product content. It was a self-hosted DEX/predictions data page that has since been retired.
- **Key pattern:** None retrievable. A hard 410 on the root and /dex indicates a retired project rather than a temporarily down service.
- **For Memeland:** Nothing transferable, only a negative signal: no prediction-market style probability feed to integrate, so no dependency should be built on it.
- **Costs:** Zero (nothing to integrate).
- **Verdict:** Skip dead page (HTTP 410) with no retrievable content; no data or patterns map to a Memeland edge.
- **Security/complexity flags:** none/verdict-ready.
### SRC-009 market-data - CoinPaprika aggregated market API
- **What it is:** Free REST crypto market-data API covering 12,000+ coins across 350+ centralized exchanges with no API key required to start. Key endpoints: GET /coins, /tickers/{id}, /exchanges, /coins/{id}/ohlcv (historical OHLCV with start/end/interval), /global market overview, plus search and a price converter; paid tiers add history depth and WebSocket streaming. Returns 429/402 when rate limits are hit.
- **Key pattern:** Aggregated CEX-sourced quote, OHLCV, and global-market breadth (total market cap, BTC dominance) served keylessly for macro/regime context; historical OHLCV makes it easy to backfill indicators without a full node.
- **For Memeland:** Useful as a keyless complement to GMGN for macro risk-regime feeds (global cap, dominance, blue-chip/BTC correlation) and for backfill of top-coin OHLCV, but it does not list long-tail dex memecoins, does not cover RH-4663, and gives no velocity/volume-per-lp or smart-money signal, so it cannot replace the DEX data layer.
- **Costs:** Free tier with generous limits and no key; stays redundant unless Memeland only needs macro breadth. Adds one more market-data dependency for secondary signals.
- **Verdict:** Study keyless aggregated macro/market-breadth feed that complements GMGN for regime signals but cannot cover RH-4663 or memecoin universe.
- **Security/complexity flags:** none/verdict-ready; no key, simple REST, low penetration into core flow.
### SRC-010 market-data - DexPaprika keyless multi-chain DEX API
- **What it is:** A 36-chain on-chain DEX data API from the CoinPaprika team: liquidity pools, swap transactions, OHLCV and token prices over REST plus SSE streaming, keyless to start. Networks include Robinhood Chain (id robinhood, Uniswap V2/V3/V4, ~202k pools, ~950M USD 24h volume), Solana, BSC, Base, Ethereum, and more. Verified live: /networks returns all chains with 24h volume/txn/pool counts, /networks/robinhood/dexes lists the three Uniswap families, and pools/search returns per-pool volume 24h/7d/30d, liquidity_usd, created_at + created_at_block_number, price change 5m/1h/6h/24h, transactions_24h, fee, and cursor paging that orders by volume_usd_24h.
- **Key pattern:** Uniform pool/token/swap pagination with cursor and block-number creation timestamps for new-pool detection; batch price endpoint for up to 10 tokens; SSE feeds for token prices, per-block pool reserves, individual swaps with USD amounts, and (paid) sealed OHLCV candles; documented liquidity-drain detector in ~70 lines using /sse/reserves; join-on-chain guidance says key on (chain,address) tuples, never symbol.
- **For Memeland:** This is the strongest GMGN-coupling reducer on the list: keyless REST already covers RH-4663 plus Solana/BSC/Base/ETH with the exact fields the liquidity and momentum voters need (liquidity_usd, volume usd multi-window, creation block, price-change windows). Adapt the per-block reserve drain pattern into the liquidity/bot-risk voter and MarketSentinel, and consider pool/search created_at_block_number scanning for launch detection.
- **Costs:** Keyless to start, free key for full SSE (reserves/transactions) and higher limits; paid Pro on api-pro.dexpaprika.com for real-time candles. One new REST+SSE dependency, but it can replace rather than duplicate parts of the GMGN layer.
- **Verdict:** Adopt keyless multi-chain feed (incl. RH-4663) plus Adapt drain-detection/SSE reserve-streaming pattern to de-couple from GMGN.
- **Security/complexity flags:** none/verdict-ready; keyless and simple REST, low complexity, covers portability to all target chains.
### SRC-011 market-data - Santiment on-chain/social/development metrics
- **What it is:** A GraphQL analytics API (SanAPI) covering 2000-3000+ assets with ~850 metrics spanning on-chain activity (daily active addresses, network growth, MVRV, NVT, realized value, supply distribution/HODL waves), social volume (Twitter/Reddit/Telegram/Discord mentions), and developer activity (GitHub commits). The app.santiment.net/assets/list path is a JS UI over the same metric catalog and returns 404 to raw fetches; the real feed is the GraphQL endpoint behind an account API key with free-tier rate limits around 100 requests/min. Queries use getMetric with timeseriesDataPerSlug selectors and allProjects with metric filters/ordering.
- **Key pattern:** Precomputed metric catalog plus slug selectors; cross-asset screening via allProjects with metric thresholds and ordering; social-volume and network-growth signals are normalized server-side rather than computed client-side.
- **For Memeland:** The transferable value is the social-sentiment and network-growth metric idea for a social/momentum voter, and the screening-by-metric-threshold pattern. As a live feed it needs a paid API key, has no RH-4663 coverage, and its social signal on long-tail memecoins is weak, so it is redundant with GMGN for the tokens Memeland actually trades.
- **Costs:** Paid/free API-key dependency with request caps; heavier GraphQL client; no RH coverage and weak long-tail signal.
- **Verdict:** Study portable sentiment/network-growth metric patterns; Skip as a live feed because it is a paid key with no RH coverage.
- **Security/complexity flags:** key/verdict-ready; adds a key dependency and GraphQL client without covering the traded universe.

### SRC-013 market-data - Birdeye multi-chain DEX analytics
- **What it is:** A decision-grade DEX data API that is Solana-first with the deepest trade-level granularity in the lineup, now also covering Robinhood, Ethereum, BSC, Base, Sui, Aptos, and more, authenticated via X-API-KEY plus x-chain headers. The birdeye.so landing page is Cloudflare-gated (403 to raw fetches), but the data-api marketing page enumerates chains including Robinhood. Enriched endpoints span token overview, v3 market data, token security, price, history, liquidity OHLC, holder/distribution, trader, and search, metered on compute units (CU) with a free tier to start.
- **Key pattern:** Header-based key plus per-chain parameter on every request; batched token pricing; precomputed token_security and holder-distribution signals and liquidity history (OHLC) exposed as ready-made decision-grade inputs.
- **For Memeland:** Complementary feed to GMGN for Solana and now RH tokens: adds token_security, holder distribution, and liquidity time-series that map cleanly onto the security and bot-risk voters, and would widen market-data redundancy. The price/volume layer overlaps GMGN, so value is in the risk/safety signals rather than replacing GMGN clicks for quotes.
- **Costs:** Requires an API key (free to start, CU-metered paid tiers), so it adds a key dependency and metered pricing that can drift.
- **Verdict:** Study complementary keyed feed with RH and Solana coverage that adds security/holder/liquidity signals to the voter stack.
- **Security/complexity flags:** key/verdict-ready; adds a key dependency and CU-metered cost without being fully keyless.

### SRC-022 api-docs - Birdeye Data API reference
- **What it is:** The official Birdeye Data API documentation, structured around an llms.txt index under data.birdeye.so. It documents X-API-KEY auth from the dashboard Security tab, rate limiting, common error codes, compute-unit (CU) metering and package tiers, and a supported-networks table that confirms Robinhood (param robinhood) alongside Solana, Ethereum, BSC, Base, Sui, Aptos, and others. Endpoint families split across DeFi, token, trader, wallet, pair, and search, with batch token endpoints that cut CU spend.
- **Key pattern:** Consistent x-chain plus X-API-KEY request contract; batch multi-token endpoints to amortize CU cost; explicit rate-limit and error-code documentation; CU-based pricing with data accessibility varying by package.
- **For Memeland:** A concrete, versioned API contract for a market-data source that reaches RH-4663; the batch-multi-token and CU-cost patterns are worth mirroring in Memeland market-data client to keep request count low, though the key dependency and CU-metered pricing are the standing risks.
- **Costs:** API key plus CU-metered pricing; docs currently live at data.birdeye.so rather than docs.birdeye.so (old paths 404).
- **Verdict:** Study the documented API contract and batch/CU-cost patterns; supported-network table confirms RH coverage.
- **Security/complexity flags:** key/verdict-ready; replacement docs host and key-based auth noted.


### SRC-016 market-data - DeBank Cloud wallet/protocol/token API
- **What it is:** DeBank is a Web3 portfolio tracker and DeFi data platform. Its developer arm, DeBank Cloud, exposes the data core as the DeBank OpenAPI (Swagger 2.0 at pro-openapi.debank.com) covering chain, protocol, pool, token, user-portfolio, wallet tx-simulation endpoints. Auth is an AccessKey header metered in prepaid "units" from the dashboard, with a 14-day free trial, ~100 requests/sec on Pro, plus a separate DeBank Connect OAuth sign-in. Landing page (debank.com/ranking/dex) is a JS SPA shell / region-blocked; details fetched via docs and search.
- **Key pattern:** Chain + protocol + token + user + wallet endpoint families; token top-holders, token historical price, wallet tx "explain/check"; per-chain_id routing (eth/bsc/xdai). Portfolio/liquidity oriented rather than memecoin trade signals; no chain-agnostic swap/router data comparable to a DEX screener.
- **For Memeland:** Nothing unique vs GMGN/DexScreener for low-cap memecoin alpha on RH-4663/Solana. Some transferable shapes: the top-holder endpoint mirrors holder-concentration checks the smart-money/security voters already approximate; tx "explain" token-transfer-like simulation overlaps Engine V2's fill-simulation anchor. Mostly redundant wallet-centric data; RH-4663 coverage not confirmed and it is a paid-key dependency.
- **Costs:** Prepaid AccessKey units, 14-day trial, ~100 rps Pro; adds a paid third-party key with per-call billing and no RH confirmation. Moderate complexity to add an unlisted-feature provider for marginal gain.
- **Verdict:** Skip <wallet/portfolio-centric, redundant with GMGN/DexScreener, paid keyed units, no RH-4663 trade edge>
- **Security/complexity flags:** key-based auth, prepaid metering, region-blocked page; verdict-ready skip unless a holder/whale feed above the existing voter stack is specifically wanted.
### SRC-017 market-data - Defined.fi (formerly DEX Guru) trading/analytics platform
- **What it is:** Defined.fi is an on-chain trading and analytics platform combining token discovery, trader tracking, watchlists, charting, and cross-chain trading in one interface. Live product exposes token/trader/portfolio/launchpad surfaces across many networks with TradingView charts and routing across multiple liquidity sources. Top-level domain is behind a Vercel Security Checkpoint (bot-check 429); details from docs/marketing and third-party profiles.
- **Key pattern:** Professional/institutional-grade DEX analytics: order-flow analysis, wallet tracking, and API access primarily for institutional clients. Its "Transfers API" (token-transfer tracking per wallet) is the one transferable shape - similar to what the smart-money voter approximates from GMGN.
- **For Memeland:** No keyless programmatic feed and RH-4663 coverage not established. Trader/wallet tracking duplicates the GMGN smart-money data already in the voter stack; charting/routing is a terminal product, not a data API. Little new edge for the green label.
- **Costs:** Institutional/paid API; closed product; Vercel bot-check blocks casual scraping. High opacity, no clear per-call metering docs reachable.
- **Verdict:** Skip <closed institutional terminal, redundant wallet/order-flow data, no keyless RH feed, bot-check protected>
- **Security/complexity flags:** client-only JS behind security checkpoint, institutional paywall, opaque data terms; verdict-ready skip.
### SRC-019 market-data - dex.watch DEX dashboard (unreachable)
- **What it is:** dex.watch was an Ethereum-centric DEX analytics/monitoring dashboard popular around 2018-2020 for tracking Uniswap and related liquidity pools. It has no public documented API. The domain is currently unreachable from this network (curl HTTP 000 / connection timeout), and web-search pivots toward the modern aggregator (DefiLlama, Bitquery, CMC) rather than any live dex.watch service.
- **Key pattern:** Historical dead pattern; no documented endpoint shape, no API reference, no RH/Solana coverage ever indicated.
- **For Memeland:** Nothing concrete to transfer; there is no fetchable feed, key, or documented schema. Redundant with GMGN/DexScreener if it were alive.
- **Costs:** None applicable; site not reachable, so no integration possible.
- **Verdict:** Skip <dead/unreachable site, no documented API, redundant with current DEX screener stack>
- **Security/complexity flags:** connection timeout/HTTP 000, no API surface; verdict-ready skip on dead page.
### SRC-020 market-data - dexindex.io DEX market index (unreachable)
- **What it is:** dexindex.io was positioned as a DEX market-data / volume-index platform aggregating decentralized-exchange metrics across chains. The domain is currently unreachable from this network (curl HTTP 000 / no route), and web-search results surface generic DEX-volume rankings (CryptoRank, CoinGecko, The Block) rather than any live dexindex service or documented API.
- **Key pattern:** Generic DEX volume/TVL index pattern; no fetchable endpoint shape, key, or schema reached. No RH-4663 or memecoin-specific coverage identified.

- **Costs:** None applicable; site unreachable, no integration possible.
